# Cluster catalog

**72 dashes in 9 categories of 8** (TJX, 2026-11-28), each built in its own thread, in the build order below. Phase 3 (design system) wrote the first catalog: 29 rebuilds and 19 new concepts across 7 families, with one pattern-setter per family (★). Phase 7 setup (chat 07-00) scaffolded every row and built the pilot. The re-cut of 2026-11-28 grouped the dashes into TJX's categories, kept all 48, added 24 (eight of them a ninth category, Abstract) and set up the [auto-chain](#the-auto-chain). Every dash obeys [docs/design/TOKENS.md](../../../docs/design/TOKENS.md), [COMPONENTS.md](../../../docs/design/COMPONENTS.md) and its family spec in [docs/design/families/](../../../docs/design/families/).

Status: ☐ todo · ⧖ waits for its module (the [code re-cut](#code-re-cut) adds it) · ◐ a thread is building or rebuilding it · ✅ done (merged into `main`). Only the [dash chain](#the-auto-chain) writes a status.

## How to read this page
- **Dash, cluster, theme.** One thing, three words: TJX says *dash*, the code and the OSD say *cluster*, and the config stores a *theme* (`ClusterTheme`). A dash's **key** is its theme name, for example `AmgMbuxSupersport`. The key is the dash's identity in code, in thread titles and in every chain message.
- **Build # and catalog row.** *Build #* is the dash's place in the queue and may change. *Catalog row* is its permanent number in `catalog.rs`, the telemetry matrix, its `spec.md` and its goldens: rows 1 to 48 are the Phase 3 catalog and rows 49 to 72 are the dashes added on 2026-11-28. This page never writes a bare "row".
- **Category and family.** Every dash has one of each, and the two are independent. **Family** decides how a dash is built: tokens, palette, type roles, shared components and the family spec. **Category** decides where it is listed, when it is built and, for a dash with no real-car target, where its idea comes from. `PorscheAnalog` is category Retro and family Classic Analog. Two names are used three ways, so this page always says which: the Liquid Glass *category*, the Liquid Glass *family* and the `LiquidGlass` *dash*; the Sci-Fi *category* and the Sci-Fi *family*.
- **Coordinator and dash chain.** The *coordinator* is the main project chat (older files call it the conductor), and only it creates threads. The *dash chain* is the scheduled task that decides which dash starts next.

## Categories
TJX set the categories on 2026-11-28, eight dashes in each: eight categories at first, and a ninth, Abstract, later the same morning ("eight brand-new dashes on top of these"). The last column is the shelf order: how the configurator lists a category, with its lead dash first. It is separate from the build order.

| Category | What it is | A render must show | Dashes in shelf order |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Supercar** | Road-car driver displays: the AMG MBUX screens and the Lexus LFA rebuilt as closely as the sources allow, and dashes inspired by the 911 GT3 RS, the Lamborghini Revuelto and the McLaren 765LT. | A named car whose recognisable display parts are there. A production screen or dial, not a race logger. | `AmgMbuxSupersport`, `Gt3RsApogee`, `AmgMbuxSuperscreen`, `LfaV10Nurburgring`, `AmgMbuxClassic`, `RevueltoCorsa`, `AmgMbuxTrackPace`, `MclarenSlimDisplay` |
| **Hypercar** | Hypercar instruments shown as machinery: watch movements, a gyro, exposed structure, a floodlit dial, Le Mans and Formula 1 hardware. | A mechanism, movement or structure seen working. Every part looks machined, jewelled or raced. | `BugattiTourbillon`, `AmgOneF1`, `PaganiHorologerie`, `ValkyrieLmp1`, `KoenigseggGyro`, `T50Floodlit`, `RattrapanteSector`, `BionicSpaceframe` |
| **Motorsport** | Race, drift and track-day tools and sim overlays: data first, strict grids, input and timing traces, shift lights that behave like the real hardware. | Exact data on a strict grid in tabular figures. Shift lights that behave like real hardware. Nothing decorative. | `Gt7HoodPro`, `BoschDdu`, `Gt7CockpitPro`, `SimPitWall`, `DriftGlass`, `SimInputTrace`, `Gt7ModularTripod`, `RackFocus` |
| **Retro** | Heritage dials and the electronics of the 1970s to 1990s: five-tube analogue clusters, vacuum-fluorescent and dot-matrix displays, and twists on Skyline and Nismo instruments, some period and some modern. | One named instrument or display technology of its era, drawn as that technology really looks. | `PorscheAnalog`, `SkylineR34Mfd`, `RetroCyber`, `HakosukaS20`, `GlyphDotMatrix`, `GtrNismoMfd`, `SynthwaveOutrun`, `ZNismoSport` |
| **Liquid Glass** | Clear optical glass over OLED black. The glass itself is the instrument, the light lives under it, and every effect is a real optics equation (`docs/specs/optics.md`). | Clear glass that reads by how it bends and catches light. No frost, no flat tint, no painted rim. | `LiquidGlass`, `VernierLoupe`, `ShibuyaChromaticPrisma`, `SapphireMystery`, `DichroicLouvre`, `LumenEtch`, `ApertureNine`, `FibreOpticCorona` |
| **Apple Vision** | Spatial displays in the manner of visionOS and CarPlay: glass panes at different depths, floating ornaments, and graphics anchored in the world around the car. The content sits at depth or in the world. | Content at two or more real depths, which parallax proves, or graphics fixed to the world. Spatial, not frosted. | `VisionSpatialGlass`, `CarplayIsland`, `WindshieldHud`, `StarSailOrigami`, `ArNavWayfinder`, `Lightpainter`, `GeodesicWell`, `TreadSeismograph` |
| **Psychedelic** | Living patterns computed from real physics and geometry: liquid metal, sand figures, ferrofluid, stress fringes, glowing wakes, pendulum drawings, smoke lines, an aurora. See [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths). | A figure that is the solution of a named equation, driven by telemetry and still at idle. Symmetry, interference or morphing; colour only where the model produces it. | `CymaticResonanceField`, `KineticLiquidMetal`, `ChronoMultiverse`, `AuroraVeil`, `HarmonographDrive`, `IsochromaticLoadPath`, `AbyssalShearBloom`, `AeroSmokeTunnel` |
| **Sci-Fi** | Film props and real hardware that looks like the future: self-driving vision, a fighter helmet, bio-monitors, warp cores, plasma and high-voltage machines. | A piece of future or film hardware that still reads as a dash first: gear, speed and shift lights at a glance. | `ChronoEventHorizon`, `TeslaAdas`, `F35HelmetHmd`, `SolarisWarpCore`, `NeuralCyberSynapse`, `EvaBioSync`, `AeroPlasmaBlade`, `ArcLadderCascade` |
| **Abstract** | Motion design in the Apple manner and pure mathematics: glass that blooms and flows, floating ink, mirrors, and figures from geometry and chaos. Added by TJX on 2026-11-28 as eight brand-new dashes. | One abstract figure that is an exact formula of what the car is doing: calm at steady input, alive when driven, clean on black. No texture draws it. | `VisionPetals`, `LiquidMorph`, `LorenzButterfly`, `SuminagashiMarble`, `KaleidoscopeMirror`, `GoldenAngle`, `HopfFibration`, `HyperbolicTiles` |

## Rules for every dash
These rules came with the re-cut of 2026-11-28. `CLUSTER_PROMPT.md` carries the same rules for the thread that builds a dash; where the two differ, the prompt wins and this page gets fixed.

### Target: what a dash is measured against
The **Target** column of the [build-order table](#build-order) has three values.

| Target | What it means | What step 1 produces |
|---|---|---|
| `recreation` | The dash rebuilds one named real display as closely as the sources allow. "The real display leads" (`CLUSTER_PROMPT.md`, "The bar") applies in full. | A feature inventory of the real display, and every deliberate change listed. |
| `inspired` | The dash keeps named, recognisable parts of a real car's display and departs from it on purpose. | The parts kept, measured from the real display, and the departures, each with its reason. |
| `concept` | No real car. The idea comes from the brief and from the dash's category. | One benchmark: a real display or object (it need not be from a car) that the visual review uses as its reference, named in `RESEARCH.md`. |

TJX, 2026-11-28: "it doesnt need a real car target take inspiration from my catagories", and "each chat though should first web search". So **every dash thread searches the web first**, whatever its target, and the Target is a starting point: if the research finds a better real target, the thread says so in `RESEARCH.md` and its handover.

### Order of authority for the look
1. The real display, for a `recreation`; the named parts of the real car, for an `inspired` dash.
2. The brief on this page: its instrument idea and its signature moment. The details are a starting point.
3. The family spec, for tokens, type, materials and the asset rule. On materials, `AGENTS.md` and the family spec always win.
4. The category: its line in the [Categories](#categories) table, and the category note under the brief's heading where there is one. A category never overrides `AGENTS.md` or a family spec.

Every difference between the finished dash and its brief goes in the handover. Where a module's `spec.md` and the brief on this page differ, this page wins.

### The five AMG dashes
TJX, 2026-11-28: "multi versions of AMG MBUX i love this", "each version i want as close as possible". An earlier MBUX cluster was rejected on 2026-11-23 because it looked nothing like MBUX. Each AMG dash recreates exactly one real display and never the style that belongs to another:

| Dash | Recreates | Told apart by | Only this dash has |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AmgMbuxSuperscreen` | The driver display of the E-Class generation that carries the MBUX Superscreen (W214, shown in 2023) in its Sporty style: red, with a central rev counter | a wide glass band | the wide glass panel |
| `AmgMbuxSupersport` | The AMG-only Supersport menu of the second-generation MBUX (AMG GT C192, SL R232): current torque, centre content, current power | one round rev counter between two wings | the torque and power wings |
| `AmgMbuxClassic` | The Classic menu of the same cars: two dials | two tube dials | the two tubes with light needles |
| `AmgMbuxTrackPace` | The AMG TRACK PACE menu: G-meter, centre content, telemetry | a G-meter, a large gear and a timing column | lap and sector timing with a delta, and the driven-line map |
| `AmgOneF1` (Hypercar) | The Mercedes-AMG ONE's free-standing driver display and the shift light on its wheel rim | a floating screen under an arc of shift lights | the wheel-rim shift lights |

- **Close** means layout, geometry, proportions and motion. Colour and type are matched through the dash's own default palette and default font, never by a literal colour or font name in `draw()`, and the product page shows the dash under that palette.
- The logo zone shows the driven car's logo, so no AMG or Mercedes mark is drawn.
- The real displays turn large areas red at the shift point and flash them. The house rule outranks "as close as possible" here: red is a steady ramp and only the shift strip strobes ([TOKENS.md](../../../docs/design/TOKENS.md), limiter strobe). Faster and slower deltas use the `go` and `warning` roles with a sign and a direction, so colour is never the only cue.
- Each AMG `RESEARCH.md` opens with its deliberate changes: no flashing area outside the strip, deltas by role with a sign, no maker's marks, palette roles, a swappable font.
- `AmgMbuxSuperscreen` (build #8) starts first. Its thread writes what all five share (type, colour roles, motion timing, menu structure) under the heading `Shared AMG MBUX notes` in its `RESEARCH.md`, and the later AMG threads read that before they search.
- Where the older `AmgMbuxSuperscreen` brief invents (a convex gear lens, a 3D trail map), the real display wins. The driven-line map belongs to `AmgMbuxTrackPace` alone.

### Flash and telemetry
- **Limiter.** Only the shift strip strobes (10 Hz at 50% duty; a 2 Hz breath in the gentle mode). No large area flashes, in any dash.
- **Decoded telemetry only.** Verified today: rpm (current, idle and maximum), speed, gear, throttle, brake, steer, acceleration, local velocity, power, boost and the timestamp. Tyre slip, suspension, pitch and roll, yaw rate, world position, fuel, lap times, torque and temperatures are not verified: a dash derives what it needs from verified fields or verifies the offset first (the 97% rule) and says so in its handover.
- **Derived values the added briefs use:** torque = power × 60 ÷ (2π × rpm); body slip angle β = atan2(v_x, v_z) from local velocity; acceleration and braking timers from speed and the timestamp; each gear's speed ÷ rpm learned live into a fixed `[f32; 10]` table.
- **Models are labelled.** Anything the game does not send and the dash does not derive exactly is marked `MODEL` on the dash.
- **Rev scales follow the car.** A figure such as "11,000 rpm" in a brief describes the real car. The dash's scale always comes from the driven car's maximum rpm.

### Glass, and the two glass categories
- **The glass rule** (clear optical glass through `fh6_kit::OpticalGlass` and [docs/specs/optics.md](../../../docs/specs/optics.md); no frost, no flat tint, no painted rim, no fresnel band) binds the Liquid Glass family, every dash of the Liquid Glass category, and every glass part of a dash in the Apple Vision category, whatever that dash's family is.
- **Liquid Glass: the glass itself is the instrument.** **Apple Vision: the content sits at depth or in the world.** Apple Vision is spatial, not frosted. It takes stacked panes, floating ornaments, parallax and world anchoring from visionOS, and an Apple Vision dash whose note says "no glass panes" stays a stroke display.
- **The "pure professional" asset rule** binds the Race Dash family. A Motorsport-category dash from another family (`Gt7HoodPro`, `Gt7CockpitPro`, `DriftGlass`, `TreadSeismograph`) takes the Motorsport trait (exact data, a strict grid, shift lights that behave like the real hardware) and keeps its own family's materials.

### Psychedelic and Abstract: pattern first, colour from the model
TJX, 2026-11-28: "the fluid ones i want real geomatry and maths going on", and the psychedelic and abstract ones "will be insane", with textures made in ComfyUI, Blender and Penpot and "very complex geomatry on some". These rules bind every dash of the Psychedelic and Abstract categories.

1. **Every moving figure is the solution of a named equation**, driven by telemetry and still at idle. The equations are in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths). Noise that only looks like physics does not pass.
2. **Psychedelic means pattern:** symmetry, interference, moiré and figures that morph into each other as the car is driven. It never means constant motion.
3. **Colour law** (set by the re-cut on 2026-11-28; TJX can overrule it). On the rank-4 figure, and only there, the hue may change across the figure when it stands for a physical quantity of the dash's own model: phase, mode number, fringe order, sense of rotation, wavelength. The hue comes from a ramp baked from the palette's primary, secondary and accent, so the driver's palette still recolours the figure. A dash whose model has colours of its own (the emission lines of an aurora, interference colours in white light) may use them when its row in the maths table says so. Gear, speed, shift lights and tach stay on their palette roles, red stays reserved, and each brief's luminance caps stay.
4. **Budget.** For a Psychedelic or Abstract dash the colour budget of TOKENS.md (base hue on at least 80% of lit pixels) is measured outside the rank-4 figure. The [code re-cut](#code-re-cut) teaches `preview-export` this. Until then the badge flags such a dash, and its handover says why.
5. **Baked assets dress the surface; they never draw the figure.** ComfyUI, Blender and Penpot make normals, sparkle, caustics, materials and environments. Where a brief names a flipbook as the fallback for its figure, the fallback is the same model at a lower resolution.
6. **Complex geometry is real geometry.** A figure that is a solid (ferrofluid spikes, a folded sail, a liquid column) is real 3D: a mesh through the kit's mesh pass, or computed facets with true normals.

### Tell it apart from its siblings
At the direction check and at the closing review, a `visual-review sheet` puts the dash's `02_mid_cruise` render beside every finished dash of its category (for an AMG dash, also beside every finished AMG dash). Two more yes or no lines are answered: the render shows what its category's line in the table says a render must show, and at thumbnail size the dash cannot be mistaken for a sibling.

## Build order
One thread per dash, at most 6 at once (TJX, 2026-11-28, raised from 4; they share the GPU lanes, root `SKILLS.md` section 5). The seven pattern-setters lead, so every family's language is proven before its siblings. After build #10 every category but Abstract has a dash. From build #11 the order goes round the categories so that none lags: Supercar, Retro, Motorsport, Psychedelic, Abstract, Hypercar, Liquid Glass, Apple Vision, Sci-Fi. Abstract starts at build #15.

- **One table.** It replaces the older "Build order" and "Index" tables, which had drifted apart on five dashes. A status lives here and nowhere else, and only the dash chain writes it.
- **Starting a thread.** The next dash to start is decided by [the auto-chain](#the-auto-chain), never read off this table by a person or a thread. A thread is started with its key: *"You are the dash thread for `<Name>` (build #`<n>`, catalog row `<r>`)"*.
- **Design boards.** A dash's Penpot page and board IDs are in its own `README.md` and `HANDOVER.md`. The IDs recorded before the re-cut are under the dash's heading in [Briefs](#briefs).
- **Earlier work.** `KineticLiquidMetal`, `AmgMbuxSuperscreen`, `DriftGlass`, `GlyphDotMatrix` and `ValkyrieLmp1` were shown as in progress by the older table and have no thread. If a branch or a Penpot page from before 2026-11-27 exists for one of them, the coordinator names it when it starts the thread.

## The auto-chain
TJX, 2026-11-28: "when one is fully finished, reviewed, accepted by github, start the next in the chain". Up to 6 dashes are built at once (TJX, 2026-11-28, raised from 4), and a free slot is never left idle.

### Who does what
- **The dash chain decides which dash starts next, and nobody else does.** It is a scheduled task named `Dash chain` that runs every hour in the chain thread (the project thread "Plan dash categories and auto-chain"). A `MERGED` message sent to that thread runs it at once, so a slot is refilled within minutes of a merge and the hourly run only catches what was missed.
- **The coordinator creates threads.** It creates a dash thread only when the chain asks for one by name (`CHAIN START <Name>`) or when TJX asks in TJX's own words, for that one dash, and only after it has checked that no thread exists for that dash in any state and that fewer than 6 dashes are being built. It answers the chain with the new thread's id, or with the reason it started none. It titles the thread `Build <Name> dash` and starts it with the line in `CLUSTER_PROMPT.md`. No slot letters and no claims.
- **A dash thread builds its one dash.** It never names or starts the next dash, and it never edits this file.

### Words with one meaning
- **Key:** the dash's theme name, for example `AmgMbuxSupersport`. A dash is its key. Build numbers and catalog rows are for people, so every chain message carries the key.
- **Done:** on a freshly fetched `origin/main`, the folder `crates/fh6-clusters/src/clusters/<snake_name>/` holds both `RESEARCH.md` and `HANDOVER.md`, and its `mod.rs` does not contain `Scaffold::new`. That is "finished, reviewed and accepted by GitHub" in files: the dash, its research and its closing review are merged. Nothing else means done: not the Status cell, not a thread's state, not a merged pull request by itself.
- **Bound:** the ledger holds the line `<Name> = <thread id>`. A thread belongs to a dash only through that line. A title is a hint, never proof.
- **Holds a slot:** a dash that is not done and is bound or has an open start request. There are 6 slots.
- **Startable:** not done, not bound, no open request, not set aside, and its module is on `origin/main` (its `mod.rs` exists and its key is in `catalog.rs`). A dash whose module is missing waits (⧖), whatever the table shows.
- **Next:** the first startable dash in the Build # order of the table on `origin/main`.

### The ledger
`dash-chain/state.json` in the project's shared files folder (outside the repo), with a `README.md` beside it that holds the ids the chain needs. It records the bound threads, the open requests, the dashes set aside and a log of what each run did. Every run reads it first and believes it over anything it remembers.

### One run, step by step
1. **Read.** Run `git fetch origin`, then read this section, the build-order table and the module folders **at `origin/main`**, never from a working tree. Read the ledger. List every thread of the project (follow the cursor until the count equals the total) and the open pull requests. If any of these reads fails: start nothing, change nothing, write one log line and end.
2. **Close what is done.** Every bound dash that is now done moves from the bound list to the log. Its slot is free.
3. **Settle the requests.** For each open request: if the coordinator has answered with a thread id, bind it and close the request. If it answered that it started none, close the request and log the reason. A request with no answer is sent once more after 2 hours. After 4 hours without an answer the run writes one line to TJX in the chain thread, once.
4. **Find strays and doubles.** A thread whose title holds a dash's exact key and that is not in the ledger is read: if its introduction opens with `DASH <Name>`, it is bound and holds a slot (TJX or the coordinator started it by hand). If two live threads claim one key, start nothing for that key and tell the coordinator once.
5. **Count.** Slots in use = bound dashes that are not done + open requests.
6. **Start.** While fewer than 6 slots are in use and a startable dash exists: take the next one, write the request into the ledger first, then send the coordinator `CHAIN START <Name> (build #<n>) · <UTC time>` with TJX's message that asked for the chain attached. One open request per key.
7. **Mark.** Work out the Status cell each dash should show: ✅ done; ◐ bound and not done; ⧖ module missing; ☐ otherwise. If the table on `origin/main` differs, change only those cells in one commit (as `TJX <TJX@Ngage.com>`, no trailer) and get it onto `main`: a plain push, or a pull request merged at once where the push is refused. If neither works, drop the commit; the next run does it. No rule reads the Status cell. It is there for people.
8. **Watch.** A bound thread that is failed or stopped, or has posted nothing for 2 hours, gets one message: `CHAIN CHECK <Name>`. If nothing has changed 2 hours later, the run asks the coordinator to restart the thread or to set the dash aside. A dash that posted `READY FOR MERGE` and is still not done 2 hours later: send its thread `CHAIN CHECK <Name>` (it merges itself, AGENTS.md rule 16), then remind the coordinator every 4 hours, three times at most, then one line to TJX.
9. **Save.** Write the ledger and bring the chain thread's checklist up to date. If nothing needs TJX, end without a message.

### Rules that keep it safe
- **One writer.** Only the chain writes the build-order table. A dash thread keeps its Penpot page and board IDs in its own `README.md` and `HANDOVER.md`, so no two writers ever meet in this file.
- **The chain never replaces a thread by itself.** Only `SET ASIDE <Name>` from the coordinator, or TJX's own word, frees the slot of a dash that is not done. A second thread for a key is started only on TJX's own word.
- **A revert on `main`** makes its dash not done again, and the next run sees that. The fix goes on a new branch whose first commit reverts the revert.
- **The [code re-cut](#code-re-cut)** and every other thread that is not a dash thread use no slot.
- **The end.** When all 72 dashes are done, the run writes one line to TJX and deletes the scheduled task.

### What a dash thread sends
| Line | When | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `DASH <Name>` | as the first line of its introduction | its own thread |
| `READY FOR MERGE <Name> · PR #<n> · head <sha>` | the closing review is all yes, `origin/main` is merged into the branch and the tests, goldens and workspace gate have passed again, just before the thread merges itself | its own thread, on record |
| `MERGED <Name> · <sha>` | the thread's own merge is on `origin/main` (AGENTS.md rule 16) | its own thread and the chain thread; the thread that merged sends it |

Neither line frees a slot by itself. Each makes the chain look, and the chain decides by the Done rule.

### Going live (2026-11-28)
In this order: this page on `origin/main`; the coordinator told its half; the ledger written with the four running rebuilds bound (`Gt7HoodPro`, `BoschDdu`, `TeslaAdas`, `RetroCyber`), each told its closing lines; one run by hand, which must report 4 slots in use, 0 requests, 2 dashes done and 24 waiting for their module; and only then the scheduled task.

## Design boards (Penpot)
Phase 7 designs in **Penpot** (unlimited plan; see [PENPOT.md](../../../docs/design/PENPOT.md)).

- **One Penpot page per dash:** `07 · <Theme>` in the Penpot master file, holding a 3840×2160 board with the binnacle guide: cockpit anchor (1920, 1835), envelope 1440×560 from (1200, 1515); hood anchor (1920, 1760), envelope 1760×400 from (1040, 1540). The file ID is in `docs/design/PENPOT.md`. Each dash's page and board IDs go in its own `README.md` and `HANDOVER.md`, never in this file.
- **The master file exists** (ID `e7ca6b81-c0d3-8029-8008-fd87488aab8f`, built 2026-11-23; see PENPOT.md). Each dash thread creates its own `07 · <Theme>` page there in step 2 of `CLUSTER_PROMPT.md`, if it is missing. Never create another file.
- **The HTML board stays in use alongside Penpot:** `cargo run --release -p preview-export -- --cluster <Theme>` writes `mockups/<Theme>/index.html` (the 4 states at 4K per view over OLED black, 1:1 crops, the cockpit composite over the reference photo with the scrim, the 60-frame sequence and measured badges). The hosted Penpot MCP cannot import local images, so the renders are reviewed there, not pasted into Penpot.
- **The brief and targets:** the dash's brief on this page (its `spec.md` is a copy), its family spec and template (`docs/design/families/`) and the family hero frames (`docs/design/heroes/<family>/`).

## How this catalog was made
**Phase 3 (the first 48).** Following the `brainstorming` discipline (lay out 2 or 3 approaches, pick, record why), the build order was chosen from three approaches:

| Approach | For | Against |
|---|---|---|
| A. Order by family, pattern-setter first | Each family's language and helpers are proven once, then reused | Popular dashes in late families wait |
| B. Order by shared-component dependency | Fewest helper changes after the factory starts | Ignores which themes drivers run most |
| C. Order by expected use (defaults first) | The default cockpit and hood dashes ship early | Can strand a family with no pattern-setter |

**Chosen then: A, seeded by C, grouped by B.** Seven Design Council directors drafted one family each, each proposing one more concept than needed. Five near-duplicates were dropped: EdgeLitEngraving and EdgelitStrata (the same edge-lit slab as `LumenEtch`), TwinBeamCollider (its signature repeats the Modern Digital hero), VortexStreetWake (the same smoke tunnel as `AeroSmokeTunnel`) and WanderingSatellite (a candidate for later).

**The re-cut of 2026-11-28 (72 in 9 categories).** TJX asked for the dashes to be grouped into categories of equal size (supercar, hypercar, motorsport, retro, liquid glass, apple vision, psychedelic, sci-fi), for several AMG MBUX versions "as close as possible", for supercar and hypercar inspired dashes, for Skyline and Nismo twists in Retro, for more clean and sim-like Motorsport dashes, for real geometry and maths in the fluid ones, and for a chain that starts the next dash when one is merged.

| Approach | For | Against |
|---|---|---|
| 6 per category, 48 dashes | No extra builds | Seven existing concepts would be set aside to make room for the cars TJX asked for |
| **8 per category, 64 dashes (TJX's choice)** | Keeps all 48 and adds the 16 dashes TJX asked for | 16 more builds |
| 7 per category, 56 dashes | Fewer new builds | Still needs invented fillers in most categories |

- **Membership.** The six built or half-built pattern-setters stay where they naturally sit. Each of the other 42 was placed by what it is to the driver, and a review that sorted the briefs without seeing the plan checked the result: `AuroraVeil` moved to Psychedelic and `StarSailOrigami` to Apple Vision because of it. A dash whose brief was written before its category carries a category note.
- **The 24 added dashes** follow TJX's own words: three more AMG MBUX displays and the AMG ONE; the GT3 RS "with an out of this world design"; dashes inspired by the Lamborghini Revuelto, the McLaren 765LT and the Gordon Murray T.50; four Skyline and Nismo twists, two period and two modern; a cockpit sibling of `Gt7HoodPro` and two sim tools; and a dash inspired by CarPlay for Apple Vision (TJX: "carplay inspired"), which took the seat of a cut-glass dash for Liquid Glass (`EdoKiriko`, kept as a spare) while `SapphireMystery` moved to Liquid Glass. Then TJX asked for "eight brand-new dashes on top of these", psychedelic and abstract, "fluid / animations", "apple vision style": they became the ninth category, Abstract, with an exact formula behind every figure.
- **Build order.** The seven pattern-setters lead, so every family's language is proven first and the code's own test still holds. `AmgMbuxSuperscreen`, `ValkyrieLmp1` and `VisionSpatialGlass` follow, so that every category has a dash by build #10. From build #11 the order goes round the categories, and inside a category TJX's named favourites come first: all four AMG MBUX dashes and the GT3 RS are built before any other Supercar dash.
- **Reviews.** The plan was read against the project's review skills before it was written here (taxonomy and category fit, UX copy, design critique, the HMI rules, accessibility, and the ways the chain could fail). Their findings shaped the Target column, the one-writer rule for the table, the Done rule of the chain and the colour law for Psychedelic. Findings and yes or no answers only; no scores.

**Honesty rules carried by every row:** telemetry comes only from the Forza Data Out packet; anything else is synthesised and labelled as such. Fields that `fh6-core` does not decode yet are called out in the brief, so the dash verifies them against `tests/golden/packets` first (the 97% rule).

## Briefs
One brief per dash, grouped by category, in build order inside each category. A brief is a starting point: its thread searches the web first, and for a `recreation` the real display leads (`CLUSTER_PROMPT.md`, "The bar" and step 1). Where a brief was written before its category existed, a category note under its heading says how to read it. The briefs of the first 48 are unchanged from Phase 3 apart from those notes.

### Supercar

#### AmgMbuxSuperscreen (wide glass panel) — build #8 · cockpit
Category **Supercar** · target `recreation` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 4 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Recreates the Sporty style of the driver display that sits beside the MBUX Superscreen glass ([The five AMG dashes](#the-five-amg-dashes)). Where this older brief invents (the convex gear lens, the 3D trail map), the real display wins, and the driven-line map belongs to `AmgMbuxTrackPace`.

'Pillar-to-pillar black glass': a 1400×320 curved superscreen with no fill, implied only by a light-driven 1 px top rim, a 40% bottom rim and a 60 px reflection band (alpha 10) that slides across the 'curved' sheet with lateral G. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 700 gear, cap 200, inside a Ø280 convex glass lens (fresnel rim, crescent) at X 1920, with modern11 diamonds above. Rank 2: Saira 200 light speed, cap 112, 360 px left, ghost 8s. Rank 3: a 1120×18 px extruded performance bar along the band top. Its bevelled glass cells are lit by the virtual light and fill in primary, with a phase-coloured end cap. Rank 4: a 640×180 3D trail map under the lens. Shy Titillium power/torque/boost tiles sit right.

- **Signature moment:** Phase-painted contrail: under the gear lens, the last 120 world-position trail points rise as a 3D light curtain behind a brand-tinted ego chevron, seen heading-up from an orbit camera pitched 55° down. Curtain height is elevation change. Each point is coloured by the phase recorded there: primary on power, ink when coasting, warning under braking. So the braking points of the last corner curl toward you, while a dashed 1.5 s predicted arc leads ahead. The performance bar's end cap shows the same phase colour live. At the limiter only the diamond strip strobes, and the lens rim holds a steady `shift`.
- **Telemetry:** world position x/y/z (bytes 244..255) → 120-point trail and elevation curtain (Y-up must be checked against the golden packets, 97% rule); throttle/brake at each trail sample → a phase byte stored with it; velocity + angular velocity y → κ = ω_y / max(v, 3 m/s) predicted arc; yaw → heading-up rotation; pitch/roll → map camera tilt ±3°; rpm/idle/max + shift → performance bar and diamonds; gear → lens glyph; speed → light numerals; power, torque, boost → shy tiles; lateral G → reflection band and light azimuth. There is no map or navigation data: the map is only the driven trail, labelled TRAIL, never NAV.
- **References:** Mercedes MBUX Hyperscreen and CLA Superscreen (Unity real-time 3D scene); BMW Panoramic iDrive black print band; Apple CarPlay Ultra thick-bar parametric themes; glider flight-recorder 3D track replays
- **Phase 6 assets:** Shared MSDF atlases for Saira 200, Saira Condensed 700 and Titillium Web 600; an optional low-poly ego chevron glTF (procedural fallback); blue-noise dither tile; optional mono brand wordmark (≤220×44) for a logo zone at the band's lower right. Lens, rims and bar cells are procedural.
- **Risk:** Design: the 3D map is rank 4 but the most complex thing on screen, so it could out-shout gear and speed or be confused with the ADAS family's forward road ribbons. Fix: curtain ≤35% luminance except the head, no emissive, an orbit view of the past trail (ADAS shows the road ahead), and mask-dissolved viewport edges. Perf: a 240-triangle strip plus dashed arc rendered at half-res into a 640×180 target ≈0.05 ms; instanced bar cells; ≈0.2 ms total. It needs a fixed [u8; 120] phase ring beside fh6-core::trail, with no allocation.

#### AmgMbuxSupersport (round rev counter) — build #11 · cockpit
Category **Supercar** · target `recreation` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 49 · new.

**Real display first** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "multi versions of AMG MBUX i love this … each version i want as close as possible"). This dash is the **Supersport** menu of the second-generation MBUX driver display: the 12.3-inch screen of the Mercedes-AMG GT (C192), SL (R232) and C 63 S E Performance. What the sources say (owner's-manual supplements and press texts, read 2026-11-28): the screen has three parts, *current torque* on the left, a *centre display content* (the manual's example is the gear display) and *current power output* on the right; the centre content is chosen from a vertical menu (gear, G-force, engine data, temperatures, set-up); in manual mode the gear carries a yellow `M`; a refused shift lights segments red. Mercedes describes the first-generation Supersport as "a central, round rev counter" with "additional information presented in the form of bars to the left and right", drawn "with a three-dimensional perspective" so that "they reach far into the background to an artificial horizon", plus "a prominent prompt to change up a gear ('shift light')". **Not in any text source, so take it from frame grabs in step 1:** whether the second-generation centre is still a round rev counter, where the digital speed sits, the scale end and red area, the shape of the torque and power elements, the colours and the typeface. Where the frames and this brief disagree, the frames win.

Starting layout, three detached parts on true black with no housing (the real screen's bezel is not part of the look). Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 190) at (0, −40) inside a Ø440 rev counter drawn with depth, a yellow `M` tag beside it, and an upshift bar of 12 cells (760 × 30) above the dial crown, filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira 300, cap 96) at the dial top, 150 px above the gear. Rank 3: the rev counter's lit arc, numbered from `EngineMaxRpm`. Rank 4: two wings that sweep away from the dial to an artificial horizon at y −60, torque on the left and power on the right, each a perspective bar with a tabular value and a peak-hold tick. Rank 5: micro-labels and the AMG logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The dyno in the wings. Through a pull the torque wing rises, peaks and falls back while the power wing keeps climbing, and each leaves a hold tick at its maximum, so the driver sees the engine's two peaks every time. At the shift rpm the upshift bar closes on its centre and the rev counter's rim holds a steady `shift` colour; on the limiter only the bar strobes. On an upshift both wings collapse toward the dial on the `fast` spring and re-open at the new rpm, and the gear enters at 1.12 → 1.0.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → rev counter, upshift bar and limiter; gear → centre numeral, with the `M` tag always on (TJX drives manual); speed → tabular speed; power → right wing, in `signals.power_unit`; torque is not a verified field, so the left wing shows power ÷ angular engine speed (τ = P·60 / (2π·rpm), rpm clamped to idle) and the handover says so; boost → the engine-data centre page; acceleration → the G-force centre page. The centre page is the driver's choice in the OSD (`config.extra` key `AmgMbuxSupersportCentre`: gear, G-force or engine data); nothing pages by itself. Temperatures and the set-up page have no telemetry and are left out.
- **References:** MBUSA owner's-manual supplements for the AMG C-Class, CLE and SL (the Supersport figure and its legend); Mercedes-AMG press texts for the A 45 (2019) and the C 63 S E Performance (2022); owner videos of the AMG GT (C192) and SL 63 cluster for frame grabs; `AmgMbuxSuperscreen`, `AmgMbuxClassic` and `AmgMbuxTrackPace` in this catalog, which share its palette and type
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the rev counter's ring and the two wing blades as rendered 3D parts with three light azimuths (the real screen shows rendered dials, so the depth must be real). ComfyUI: the dark dial-face material the frame grabs show, as a 4096 px tileable. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 800, Saira 300 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** An MBUX cluster was rejected on 2026-11-23 for looking nothing like MBUX: no invention before the frame grabs are in `RESEARCH.md`. Three near-equal parts can flatten the rank order, so the wings stay at rank 4 luminance and never bloom. The real style leans on red: red must stay under the tokens' 5% budget away from the limiter unless the frames prove otherwise, in which case the real display wins and the exception is written in the handover; nothing red ever flashes, and red is never the only cue. Perf: one dial plate, two instanced wing bars, est. 0.15 ms.

#### Gt3RsApogee — build #20 · cockpit
Category **Supercar** · target `inspired` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 54 · new.

**Inspired by the Porsche 911 GT3 RS (992), taken out of this world** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "the gt3 rs inspired would look dope with an out of this world design"). `PorscheAnalog` already recreates the 992 five-tube cluster, so this dash does not repeat it. It keeps what an owner would recognise from the GT3 RS and rebuilds each part as a spaceflight instrument. From the car, as the research note of 2026-11-28 found it in Porsche's own material (check it again in step 1): the 992.1 GT3 RS has one physical rev counter between two 7-inch screens, with 5,000 rpm at 12 o'clock, a 9,000 rpm limit and a small digital window for speed and gear; a Track Screen that cuts the two screens down to tyre pressure, oil pressure, oil temperature, water temperature and fuel level; a shift assistant of two yellow bars beside the rev counter that fill with the revs and flash blue at the shift point; and four rotary controls on the wheel. On the 992.2 the scale can be turned so that the 9,000 rpm limit stands at 12 o'clock, which is the idea this dash is built on. From spaceflight: the Apollo attitude ball (black on one side, red at the poles, yellow director needles), an orbit map and docking lights.

Starting layout, three detached parts. Rank 3: a Ø500 rev counter at (0, −20) with no hub, where the needle is a small craft riding the rim and the red zone ends at 12 o'clock, the apogee. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 190) in the dial centre, which stands for the planet, and 12 docking lights on a straight strip above it (760 × 30), filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 96) under the gear inside the dial. Rank 4, left: a Ø260 attitude ball, a real 3D sphere in a gimbal cage. Rank 4, right: a 300 × 300 transfer plot, where each gear is a circular orbit and the craft flies the ellipse between this gear's orbit and the next. Rank 5: mission-clock style micro-labels and the Porsche crest.

- **Signature moment:** The transfer burn. Through a pull the craft climbs its transfer ellipse, brightest at the low point where it is fastest, and reaches apogee exactly at the shift rpm, where the rim marker stands at 12 o'clock. On the upshift the ellipse closes into the next gear's circle in 300 ms on the `fast` spring and a new ellipse opens from it. Two thin yellow bars beside the dial fill with the revs, as the car's shift assistant does, and stay steady. On the limiter only the docking lights strobe, and the apogee marker holds a steady `shift` colour; the car flashes its bars blue, and the handover records the steady bars as a deliberate change (the house rule keeps the flash on the shift strip). The attitude ball shows a drift as a pilot would see it: the nose marker stays centred while the prograde marker, where the car is really going, slides away by the slip angle.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → rim craft, docking lights, limiter and the craft's place on the ellipse (true anomaly θ = π × the rpm fraction between idle and shift, so the plot repeats the rev counter and cannot disagree with it); gear → centre numeral and the active orbit; per-gear speed ÷ rpm learned live into a fixed `[f32; 10]` table → orbit radii and the readout "speed at shift"; speed → tabular speed; acceleration (felt force) → ball pitch and roll on the `gBall` spring; local velocity → slip angle β = atan2(v_x, v_z) → prograde marker; throttle → plume brightness. Orbit shapes are computed, never drawn by hand: r(θ) = a(1 − e²) / (1 + e·cos θ) with a = (r₁ + r₂) / 2 and e = (r₂ − r₁) / (r₂ + r₁), and brightness along the arc follows the vis-viva speed v² ∝ 2/r − 1/a. Pitch, roll and yaw from the packet are not verified and are not used.
- **References:** Porsche 911 GT3 RS (992) instrument cluster, Track Screen and shift assistant; the Apollo flight director attitude indicator and the DSKY; Hohmann transfer diagrams; the orbit view of mission-control displays; `PorscheAnalog` in this catalog for the rendered-dial method
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the rev counter's ring as flight hardware (machined titanium, safety-wired fasteners, a ceramic-white index ring), the attitude ball as a textured sphere in its cage (glTF for the kit's mesh pass, with a sprite fallback), and the docking-light caps. ComfyUI: crinkled gold insulation foil for the dial's backing, a heat-shield tile surface for the side parts, and a faint star-field environment plate for reflections. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800, B612 Mono and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** Two ideas in one dash can end as a costume: every space part must do a driving job (the ball is the G and slip display, the plot is the shift coach), and anything that does neither is cut. The transfer plot must never be read as a map or as real navigation. `GeodesicWell` already owns the gravity-well lattice, so no warped grid here. A rotating 3D ball can pull the eye: it moves only with real forces and sits at rank 4 luminance. Perf: one mesh of about 2,000 triangles, two ellipses in chunks of 8 segments, est. 0.25 ms.

#### AmgMbuxTrackPace (lap timer) — build #29 · cockpit+hood
Category **Supercar** · target `recreation` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 57 · new.

**Real display first** (TJX, 2026-11-28: each AMG MBUX version "as close as possible"). This dash is the **Track Pace** menu of the second-generation MBUX driver display, the screen that goes with AMG TRACK PACE, the car's data logger. What the sources say: the screen has three parts, a *G-meter* on the left, a *centre display content* (the manual's example is the gear display) and a *telemetry display* on the right (the example is the selected race track); the G-meter shows lateral and longitudinal force in a coordinate system and "the maximum values will be shown in red"; TRACK PACE records more than 80 values ten times a second, shows lap and sector times, and marks them "in green or red" against the reference lap; it also times acceleration, the quarter mile and braking; timing starts when the car crosses the start line. **Take from frame grabs in step 1:** the drawn form of the G-meter, how lap time, delta and sectors are laid out in the cluster, whether a rev band is part of the menu, the colours and the typeface.

Starting layout, three detached parts. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 200) at (0, −50) with the yellow `M` tag, under an upshift bar of 12 cells (760 × 30) at y −290, filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira 300, cap 96) under the gear. Rank 3: a slim rev band across the top of the centre part. Rank 4, left: a Ø300 G-meter with rings at 0.5 g, a cross-hair, a dot on the `gBall` spring and the maxima held as ticks with their values. Rank 4, right: the telemetry part, 400 × 300, with the driven line as a heading-up map, the current lap, the last and best lap, and the delta. Hood view: the same three parts spread across 1760 × 400 with the map dropped to a delta bar.

- **Signature moment:** The delta swing. As the car closes a lap the time freezes for 2 s, the delta flips in with its sign and an up or down arrow, and the finished lap becomes the reference line on the map, with a ghost dot showing where the best lap was at the same elapsed time. Ahead is `go`, behind is `warning`, always with the sign and the arrow, so colour is never the only cue. As the real car tints its cabin light, the kit's ambient wash leans green or red with the delta, as a steady ramp under 20% that never flashes and can be switched off. On the limiter only the upshift bar strobes.
- **Telemetry:** acceleration → G-meter and its held maxima; gear, speed, rpm → the centre part; speed and the packet timestamp → the acceleration timers (0 to 100 km/h, 0 to 200, the quarter mile by integrating speed, and braking from 100), which use verified fields only and ship first. The game's lap and sector fields are not decoded, so the dash times laps itself: world position and the timestamp give the driven line, and a lap closes when the line returns to its own start within 15 m on the same heading. World position is decoded but not covered by the golden packets: verify it in the real game before the map and the lap timer ship (97% rule) and say so in the handover. The best lap's samples live in a fixed ring buffer; nothing allocates.
- **References:** MBUSA owner's-manual supplements for the AMG C-Class and CLE (the Track Pace figure and its legend); Mercedes-AMG TRACK PACE press and manual texts; owner videos of TRACK PACE on the AMG GT and C 63 for frame grabs; `AmgMbuxSupersport` in this catalog, whose parts and palette it shares
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the G-meter's ring and lens as a rendered part with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: the dark panel material the frame grabs show behind the telemetry part. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 800, Saira 300 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** The real function depends on stored circuits and the game has none, so the lap timer must never claim more than it knows: label the line TRAIL and the timer LAP only after a closed lap, and show dashes until then. Green and red deltas fail for colour-blind drivers unless the sign and the arrow carry the same message. Three busy parts can out-shout gear and speed: the side parts stay at rank 4 luminance with no bloom. Perf: the map is a polyline of at most 240 points drawn in chunks of 8, est. 0.2 ms.

#### AmgMbuxClassic (two tube dials) — build #38 · cockpit
Category **Supercar** · target `recreation` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 61 · new.

**Real display first** (TJX, 2026-11-28: each AMG MBUX version "as close as possible"). This dash is the **Classic** menu of the second-generation MBUX driver display on AMG cars (AMG GT C192, SL R232, C 43 / C 63, CLE 53). What the sources say: "The Classic menu shows the driver display separated into speedometer and tachometer"; the press calls it "the proven display with two tubes and changing content between them"; the manual's legend lists speedometer, tachometer, gear display, a temperature or battery gauge, outside temperature, the centre display content, the time and the fuel level; a review of the SL 63 describes "two analogue (but digital) dials, speed and fuel on the left, and then rpm and oil temp on the right", and another notes that both dials have "a red digitized needle". On AMG cars the centre content can be engine data (current power and torque), set-up or temperatures. **Take from frame grabs in step 1:** the AMG dial artwork, both scale ends and the red area, where the small gauges sit, the needle shape and the typeface.

Starting layout: two Ø430 tubes at (−400, −30) and (+400, −30), speedometer left and rev counter right, each a rendered dial under a glass lens with a red light needle (rank 3), and nothing joining them. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 180) between the tubes at (0, −60) with the yellow `M` tag, under an upshift bar of 12 cells (760 × 30) that bridges the two dial crowns and fills outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira 300, cap 96) inside the speedometer, 400 px from the gear. Rank 4: the centre content under the gear, 360 × 150. Rank 5: the small inset gauges, the labels and the AMG logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The light needles. Each needle is a blade of light inside the lens: it lights the ticks it passes and leaves a short afterglow on the scale, so a hard pull paints a red sweep that fades in 200 ms. At the shift rpm the rev counter's red area holds a steady `shift` glow and the upshift bar closes on its centre; on the limiter only the bar strobes. On an upshift the rev needle drops on the `needle` spring with feed-forward and the afterglow shows the size of the drop.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → rev counter, upshift bar and limiter; speed → speedometer needle and the tabular readout; gear → centre numeral with the `M` tag; power and derived torque (power ÷ angular engine speed) → the engine-data centre content; acceleration → a G-force centre content; boost → the rev counter's inset gauge. Fuel, coolant and oil temperature, outside temperature and the clock have no verified telemetry: their places hold boost and throttle, or the field is verified first (97% rule). The centre content is the driver's choice in the OSD (`AmgMbuxClassicCentre`).
- **References:** MBUSA owner's-manual supplement for the AMG C-Class (the Classic figure and its nine-item legend); Mercedes press text for the C-Class W206; reviews of the SL 63 and GLC driver displays; owner videos of the AMG GT (C192) cluster for frame grabs; `PorscheAnalog` in this catalog for how rendered dials are built
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the two dial tubes (ring, face, lens) with three light azimuths, and the needle blade. ComfyUI: the AMG dial-face material the frame grabs show. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 800, Saira 300, Barlow Semi Condensed 500 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** Two equal dials pull the eye from the gear: the gear numeral is the brightest thing on screen and the dial faces stay under 20% luminance. Twin round dials are also what `PorscheAnalog` and `PaganiHorologerie` use, so this one must read as a Mercedes screen, not as hardware: follow the frame grabs for the lens, the needle and the type. The red needles and red area are steady, never flashing, and the scale numerals carry the reading without colour. Perf: two dial plates and two needles, est. 0.15 ms.

#### LfaV10Nurburgring — build #47 · cockpit
Category **Supercar** · target `recreation` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 10 · rebuild.

**Category note.** The Lexus LFA's own instrument leads: its single central ring, its needle and its numerals, as step 1 finds them. This brief's ring is where the design starts, not where it ends.

A hubless TFT tach in the LFA spirit. One Ø480 circular-grain titanium ring, 24 px wide, floats on true black with no pivot and no face. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 190, in the ring centre; 16 slim LED bars sit on a straight shelf above it, 26 × 760 px. Rank 2: tabular Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 88, stacked directly under the gear. Rank 3: the needle is a light wedge from R 0.72 to R 0.95, a primary-colour core with a white edge, over Barlow Semi Condensed numerals on a scale rounded up from EngineMaxRpm. Above 180°/s it sheds up to four ghost wedges. Rank 4: a lap wing on the left (current, last and best lap in Titillium tabular figures) and a 4-step tyre-temperature quad on the right, both without glow. Rank 5: the crest, low inside the ring.

- **Signature moment:** Attack mode. After 0.4 s at ≥90% throttle above 70% rpm, the ring, numerals and needle rotate as one rigid group (slow spring, 363 ms) until the redline stands at 12. The numerals grow from cap 34 to cap 42 with a 500→700 weight crossfade. The ring's circular-grain highlight stays fixed to the light, as real circular grain would. After 3 s under 50% throttle the mode reverses; it never toggles during a gear change. From 92% of limiter rpm the face takes a steady red bath that ramps up to 30% alpha and never flashes. Only the LED shelf strobes. Opening the OSD glides the ring 160 px left on the 12 rad/s spring, which is the LFA's menu move.
- **Telemetry:** rpm drives the light wedge (needle spring with feed-forward), the LED thresholds and the red-bath ramp. The wedge's angular velocity feeds the afterimage, a fixed [f32; 4] ring buffer. throttle and rpm feed the attack-mode state machine (0.4 s in, 3 s out). EngineMaxRpm sets the scale end. speed and gear drive the centre stack. Current, last and best lap plus the lap number drive the lap wing. The four tyre temperatures drive the thermal quad: 4 discrete steps with a 150 ms crossfade, plus a count or position change so colour is never the only cue. Lateral G drives the light and the parallax (stack 0, ring 2, wings 4 px). The lap and tyre-temperature fields are not yet decoded by fh6-core; verify them against the golden packets.
- **References:** Lexus LFA TFT meter (moving ring, sport mode with bolder numerals and redline at the top, red bath near redline; co-designed with Polyphony Digital); Nürburgring Nordschleife lap-time culture; AiM/Bosch LED shift shelves
- **Phase 6 assets:** Circular-grain brushed titanium albedo/roughness with a tangential anisotropy map, with a conic-banding fallback. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 500, 700 and 800 (both weights are needed for the crossfade), Barlow Semi Condensed, Titillium Web and Michroma. The brand crest in mono white. Everything else is procedural.
- **Risk:** v1 drew an opaque panel of text lists with an off-palette yellow arc. Rotating the scale under a moving needle could also mislead for 363 ms. The rigid-group rotation keeps every reading correct, the mode needs 0.4 s of intent, and an OSD toggle can lock it off. The red bath must stay a steady ramp at ≤30% alpha and is counted in the red-area budget. Performance: the ring, wedge and four ghosts are SDF, est. 0.15–0.2 ms.

#### RevueltoCorsa — build #56 · cockpit
Category **Supercar** · target `inspired` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 67 · new.

**Inspired by the Lamborghini Revuelto** (in the game as the 2024 Lamborghini Revuelto). Checked against sources on 2026-11-28 (motor1, Automotive Interiors World, CarExpert and GTspirit; Lamborghini's own pages redirected and could not be read): a 12.3-inch reconfigurable driver display beside an 8.4-inch portrait centre screen and a 9.1-inch passenger display, all three run by one computer with 3D graphics, animations and widgets, and content that swipes from the centre screen to either side; drive modes Città, Strada, Sport and Corsa and hybrid modes Recharge, Hybrid and Performance, each on its own rotary selector among four on the wheel spokes (13 combinations in all), with launch control on a button in the centre of the left rotor; a V12 whose injection cuts at 9,500 rpm, with peak power at 9,250; and the Y (head and tail lights, centre of the dashboard) and the hexagon (exhaust outlets, air vents) as recurring shapes of the car. The driver display changes its look with the drive mode (Lamborghini showed separate Strada, Sport and Corsa screens in March 2023, when the cluster was first shown, under the LB744 name, on 21 March 2023), but the shape of each screen's rev display is still to be read from those photos in step 1, and neither the Y nor the hexagon is confirmed as a shape inside the real display. This dash takes the Corsa idea, the Y and the hexagon and goes its own way with them.

Starting layout, detached parts on true black. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 200) in a 300 px forged-carbon hexagon at (0, −30), and a shift strip drawn as the two upper arms of a Y: 2 × 6 slanted cells that run from the outer tips at (±390, −300) down to the hexagon's crown, filling outside-in, 34 px tall. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 100) directly under the hexagon. Rank 3: the rev track, a hexagonal ring around the gear whose six straight sides light in turn, numbered from `EngineMaxRpm` so the scale reaches 10 for the V12. Rank 4: two slanted wing panels that follow the Y's angle, power on the right and pedals with G on the left. Rank 5: the mode name plate and the bull.

- **Signature moment:** Y ignition. As the revs build the two arms of the Y burn inward from their tips. At the shift rpm they meet at the crown and the light drops down the stem into the hexagon, whose machined rim takes a steady `shift` colour: the Y is complete only at the moment to shift. On the limiter only the Y's arms strobe. On an upshift the stem empties first, then both arms fall back outward on the `ribbon` spring, and the six sides of the rev track relight to the new rpm.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → Y arms, rev track, stem and limiter; gear → hexagon numeral; speed → tabular speed; power → right wing bar and value, in `signals.power_unit`; throttle and brake → left wing bars; acceleration → G dot on the `gBall` spring; lateral G → the light on the hexagon's chamfers. The car's drive mode and hybrid mode are not in the telemetry: the look is the driver's choice in the OSD (`config.extra` key `RevueltoCorsaLook`: Corsa by default, Strada as a calmer round variant of this dash's own), and nothing switches by itself.
- **References:** Lamborghini Revuelto three-screen cockpit, 12.3, 8.4 and 9.1 inches (https://www.automotiveinteriorsworld.com/news/connectivity/lamborghini-reveals-details-of-all-new-v12-revuelto-hpev.html , https://www.motor1.com/news/659544/lamborghini-revuelto-debuts-aerospace-inspired-v12-hybrid/ , https://www.carexpert.com.au/lamborghini/revuelto/technology); drive and hybrid modes, the rotors, the launch button and the mode-specific cluster screens (https://www.motor1.com/news/658228/lamborghini-lb744-instrument-cluster/ , https://gtspirit.com/2023/10/17/driving-the-2024-lamborghini-revuelto/); still to open in step 1: the photos in motor1's LB744 cluster article and Lamborghini's own HMI page (https://www.lamborghini.com/en-en/news/the-new-human-machine-interface-of-lamborghini-revuelto), for the rev display of each mode; Lamborghini Forged Composites; `ValkyrieLmp1` in this catalog for forged carbon, so the two do not look alike
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the hexagon pod (forged-carbon body, machined and anodised chamfers) and the slanted cell caps of the Y, with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: a forged-composite surface with larger, flatter chips than `ValkyrieLmp1` uses, and a hexagonal mesh for the wing panels. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** A hexagonal rev track is harder to read than an arc: number every side, keep a lit marker at the exact rpm, and check it against the blur gate. Slanted cells can alias on the OLED: analytic anti-aliasing and a minimum cell width of 30 px. The dash must not become a logo collage: the Y and the hexagon each appear once at full size. Lamborghini's own cluster colours are not established, so the palette roles lead until the frames are in. Perf: instanced cells and one pod plate, est. 0.15 ms.

#### MclarenSlimDisplay — build #65 · cockpit+hood
Category **Supercar** · target `inspired` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 70 · new.

**Inspired by the McLaren Folding Driver Display** of the 720S (in the game as the 2021 McLaren 765LT; whether the 765LT has it too is to confirm in step 1). Checked against sources on 2026-11-28 (McLaren's press release of 1 March 2017, paultan.org, Autoblog, GTspirit and Top Gear): in Full Display Mode an upright full-colour TFT screen behind the wheel shows a round tachometer, the trip computer and fuel and temperature gauges, with its own set of screens in each of the Comfort, Sport and Track settings, and it is the default at unlocking as part of the welcome sequence; in Slim Display Mode the display folds forwards and down and leaves a narrow strip facing the driver with only the gear, the engine revs and the speed, chosen with a button or deployed with the Active Dynamics settings, for the least distraction and a clearer view on track. The strip's rev graphic, the order of its contents, the hinge and the timing of the fold are not established. The 750S that followed moved its display onto the steering column and dropped the fold. This dash maps the two modes onto the two views: the full display is the cockpit view, and the slim strip is the hood view, where a clear road matters most.

Starting layout. Cockpit, the full display as a floating 900 × 330 slab in a thin housing (the real display is a physical body, so this dash may have one): rank 1, the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 200) at centre under a straight shift strip of 12 cells (760 × 30) that fills outside-in; rank 2, tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 100) beside the gear; rank 3, a horizontal rev bar across the slab's top, numbered from `EngineMaxRpm` (the real full display has a round tachometer: the bar is this dash's change, so the full and slim states share one rev graphic, and the handover lists it); rank 4, G and pedals at the left, power and boost at the right. Hood, the slim strip: one 1500 × 86 bar with the rev bar running its full length, the gear in a raised centre tab (cap 150) and the speed beside it, and nothing else.

- **Signature moment:** The fold. When the dash appears (the real display unfolds as part of its welcome sequence), or the driver picks slim in the OSD, the slab tips forwards and down about its lower edge as a rigid body, a true homography foreshortened by cos θ and shaded by the light (the real pivot and timing are to confirm from owner video), while the strip's content rises out of its top edge, 600 ms on the `default` spring; the reverse unfolds it. Gear, speed and revs stay readable through the whole move because they are the only things that exist in both states. On the limiter only the shift strip strobes; in the slim strip the last tenth of the rev bar holds a steady `shift` colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → rev bar, shift strip and limiter; gear → centre numeral; speed → tabular speed; acceleration → G dot; throttle and brake → pedal bars; power and boost → right-hand readouts; lateral G → the light on the housing's edges. The fold follows the view or the driver's setting (`config.extra` key `MclarenSlimDisplayMode`), never a guessed track mode.
- **References:** McLaren Folding Driver Display on the 720S, press release of 1 March 2017 (https://www.conceptcarz.com/z20171/z33361/a6919/events/events.aspx), https://paultan.org/2017/03/03/second-gen-mclaren-super-series-model-to-feature-folding-driver-display-two-modes-available-to-driver/ , https://www.autoblog.com/2017/03/01/mclaren-720s-driver-interface/ and https://gtspirit.com/2017/03/01/mclaren-720s-cool-new-folding-driver-display/; the 750S column-mounted display, which does not fold, for the later graphics (https://www.automotiveinteriorsworld.com/news/mclaren-reveals-driver-focused-cabin-for-all-new-750s-supercar.html , https://www.topgear.com/car-reviews/mclaren/750s/interior); still to confirm in step 1: the 765LT and Senna fitment, and from owner videos of the fold the pivot, the timing, the strip layout and the colours; `Gt7HoodPro` in this catalog as the hood-view benchmark
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the display body (satin-black housing, glass face, the hinge edge) rendered at five fold angles with matching normal maps, and the slim strip's top edge. ComfyUI: the anti-glare matrix of the screen face and an Alcantara surface for the dash top that the slab folds into. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** The fold is a large moving object beside the road view: it runs only on a view change or a setting change, never while driving at speed by itself. The slim strip is 86 px tall and sits over a bright road with the scrim at 0%: the gear tab and the speed need a feathered dimming pad and must pass the blur gate there. The strip's order, its rev graphic and the colours come from the frames, not from this brief. Perf: one slab plate and one bar, est. 0.12 ms.

### Hypercar

#### ValkyrieLmp1 — build #9 · cockpit+hood
Category **Hypercar** · target `recreation` · family Race Dash · catalog row 13 · rebuild.

**Category note.** The Aston Martin Valkyrie's own display leads. Where that is a screen on the steering wheel, step 1's rule applies: take its parts, not its shape. The Porsche 963 and McLaren references in this brief are benchmarks only. The Race Dash asset rule applies.

A hypercar endurance wheel face, not a panel: a 1440×420 forged-carbon yoke (chopped-fibre marble, unlike Bosch's twill) with angled shoulders around a 1000×340 true-black window. Rank 1: a straight 15-LED crown (34 px) across the window top and a `race` 800 gear at cap 250 in a tall portrait well. Rank 3 is the well's frame: an 8 px rpm channel climbing the left side, crossing the top, descending the right. Rank 2: speed, cap 112, left of the well; a signed lap delta at cap 84 right. Rank 4: a 4 s throttle/brake/steer engineer trace under the gear. Shoulders hold knurled anodised encoder rings with intervention arcs. Labels Titillium Web; delta and sectors in B612 Mono.

- **Signature moment:** Predictive crown: below the first per-gear threshold the 15 LEDs run at 40% intensity as a lap-delta meter, growing from the centre pod rightward in `go` when gaining and leftward in `warning` when losing (1 LED = 0.1 s, clamped ±0.7 s). The frame rpm crosses stage 1, the crown hard-switches to outside-in shift mode at full intensity. At the limiter only the crown strip strobes at 10 Hz while the gear-well perimeter tach holds steady in `shift`, the Valkyrie's one never-flashing cue.
- **Telemetry:** rpm vs car-profile per-gear thresholds → crown and perimeter tach; gear → well glyph; speed_ms → speed; distance traveled minus lap-start distance compared against a recorded best-lap time-by-distance table (fixed 2048 bins, no allocation; synthesised, hidden in free roam and until one clean lap exists) → delta crown and readout; lap number, current/last/best lap → sector strip using synthesised thirds of the recorded lap length; accel/brake/steer → engineer trace (go / warning / neutral hairline); per-wheel slip ratio + drivetrain_type + throttle/brake → synthesised TC (outer) and ABS (inner) arcs on the left encoder; boost_psi → right encoder outer arc, fuel → inner arc; tyre temps ×4 → corner tags in thermal steps; acceleration_x → light and parallax.
- **References:** Porsche 963 LMDh wheel (top shift/warning lights, messages, lap times); AiM MXG Strada predictive lap-delta LED mode; MoTeC i2-style throttle/brake overlay traces; McLaren MCL40 2026 wheel (signed bar that vanishes at idle)
- **Phase 6 assets:** Forged-carbon marble albedo+normal (1024², seamless, asset-forge recipe + seed) with a procedural Voronoi-chip fallback; knurled encoder-ring normal strip 2048×64 with an SDF sawtooth fallback; MSDF Saira Condensed 700/800, Titillium Web 600, B612 Mono.
- **Risk:** Design: delta-mode LEDs mistaken for shift lights. Avoid: 40% intensity, centre-out pattern only, a same-frame switch at stage 1 with 120 rpm hysteresis, an OSD toggle, and a dark crown in free roam. Perf: 240-point trace from a fixed ring buffer as one SDF strip ≈0.02 ms; forged carbon one textured pass ≈0.03 ms; bloom on crown, well tach and gear ≈0.12 ms; ≈0.22 ms total. Hood [4]: shoulders spread to the 1760×400 envelope and the trace shortens to 3 s. Needs distance, lap and slip-ratio fields added to the fh6-core decoder.

#### AmgOneF1 (F1 shift lights) — build #16 · cockpit
Category **Hypercar** · target `recreation` · family Race Dash · catalog row 52 · new.

**Real display first.** The Mercedes-AMG ONE (in the game as the 2021 Mercedes-AMG One) carries a Formula 1 power unit that revs to 11,000 rpm. What the sources say: two free-standing 10-inch displays, one in front of the driver and one on the centre console angled toward the driver; a Formula 1-style wheel with a flattened top and bottom and a row of shift lights across the top of the rim; drive programs Race Safe, Race, EV, Race Plus, Strat 2 and Individual; aero states Highway, Track and Race DRS. No text source documents the driver display's graphics. **Take from frame grabs in step 1:** the whole screen layout, the shift-light count, colours and firing order, the typeface and the colours.

Starting layout: the driver's screen as a floating 680 × 380 slab with small rounded corners in a thin machined housing (the real display has one, so this dash may), and above it a detached 780 px arc of the wheel rim in carbon that carries the shift lights, 34 px tall. Rank 1: the rim lights and the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 220) in the screen centre. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 100) under the gear. Rank 3: a rev band along the screen top, numbered from `EngineMaxRpm` up to 11. Rank 4: power on the right as a bar with a tabular value, pedal traces on the left, and a lap delta line under the speed. Rank 5: the drive-program label, shown only as the dash's own name plate, and the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** Lights on the rim. The shift lights follow the real wheel's order and colours once the frames confirm them (the family rule is that shift lights behave like the real hardware, and it wins over the outside-in default). The rim arc sits apart from the screen, so the lights reach the eye before the screen does. At the shift rpm the whole row is lit; on the limiter only the row strobes, 10 Hz, 50% duty, and reduced-flash mode swaps the strobe for a 2 Hz breath. On an upshift the row clears in 2 frames and the gear enters at 1.12 → 1.0 with no other motion on screen.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → rim lights (per-car, per-gear thresholds), rev band and limiter; gear → centre numeral; speed → tabular speed; power → the power bar, in `signals.power_unit`; throttle and brake → pedal traces; boost → a shy readout; speed and timestamp → the acceleration timers shared with `AmgMbuxTrackPace`. The hybrid system's charge and deployment, the drive program and the aero state are not in the telemetry: none of them is drawn as a live value, and no DRS or battery state is invented.
- **References:** Mercedes-AMG ONE press material and interior photos; owner and press videos of the ONE's driver display for frame grabs; the Mercedes-AMG Petronas Formula 1 steering-wheel displays; `BoschDdu` in this catalog for the Race Dash grid and lamp rules
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the screen housing and the carbon rim arc with its LED lens caps, with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: the rim's carbon weave and the anti-glare matrix of the screen face. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** With no documented graphics this is the AMG dash most likely to be invented: no layout work before the frame grabs are in `RESEARCH.md`, and where frames cannot be found the handover says which parts are this thread's own design. A Formula 1 row of green, red and blue lights uses colour as its cue: the position along the row and the fill order carry the same message. An 11,000 rpm scale must still read for a car that revs to 6,000: number the band from the car's own maximum. Perf: one housing plate, a row of instanced lights, est. 0.12 ms.

#### BugattiTourbillon — build #25 · cockpit
Category **Hypercar** · target `recreation` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 9 · rebuild.

**Category note.** No Bugatti is in the game's car list, so no car selects this dash by itself: the driver picks it in the OSD, and its logo zone shows the driven car's logo.

One Ø520 skeleton dial on X = 1920, with no satellites. The movement spills past the dial as openwork bridges reaching 300 px each side on the −4 px layer. They are linearly brushed at ≤20% luminance, with anglage bands that catch the G-driven light. Rank 1: a Ø210 tourbillon cage turns at the dial centre around a gear numeral that stays upright and still (Saira Condensed 800, cap 170). Eleven clear jewels set in the top bridge form the shift strip, 30 × 740 px. Rank 2: tabular Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 90, in an aperture under the cage. Rank 3: there is no centre hand. A lume-tipped peripheral carriage rides a rail round the dial edge over Barlow Semi Condensed numerals and reaches 12 at the shift rpm. Rank 4: the road drives the movement, so wheelspin visibly overspeeds the cage against a ground-speed index. Rank 5: jewel bearings tinted with the palette accent, and the crest at 6.

- **Signature moment:** Escapement beat. The cage is geared to the driven wheels: it turns with the road and stops dead at a standstill, so idle is perfectly still. On each gear change it takes one visible beat, a 50 ms hold then a 30° jump on the fast spring, as the new numeral rises inside it. The numeral is counter-rotated every frame so it never tilts. At the limiter only the 11-jewel bridge strip strobes. The carriage's lume tip, sitting just past 12, burns a steady shift colour with a 6 px halo.
- **Telemetry:** rpm drives the peripheral carriage (needle spring with feed-forward) and the jewel thresholds; the car-profile shift rpm puts the carriage at 12 (fallback: limiter × 0.97). The driven-wheel rotation speeds, chosen by DrivetrainType (FWD, RWD or AWD mean), drive the cage and gear-train angle at wheel rps / 16. speed drives the tabular digits and the ground-speed index ring; wheelspin shows as the cage overspeeding the ring. gear drives the cage numeral. boost and fuel feed two shy apertures in the side bridges (rank 5). Lateral G drives the light on the anglage and the parallax (cage 0, dial 2, bridges 4 px). Wheel rotation speed and fuel are not yet decoded by fh6-core; add them and verify them against tests/golden/packets.
- **References:** Bugatti Tourbillon instrument cluster (Concepto-built, 600+ parts, centre dial with small digital speed and gear); flying-tourbillon haute horlogerie; skeletonised calibres with anglage and Côtes de Genève; peripheral-hand watch architectures
- **Phase 6 assets:** A glTF tourbillon cage, a 5-wheel gear train and bridges (≤15k tris) from tools/asset-forge, with an SDF recipe as the fallback. Also: a linear-brushed steel albedo with an anglage normal map; a Côtes de Genève stripe normal map for the bridges; a jewel-lens matcap tinted at runtime from the palette; the brand-crest dial variant.
- **Risk:** v1 drew a grey plate with a gear smaller than its speed and a cog icon. The skeleton can also become busy grey structure that fights the gear and wears the OLED. Keep the movement layer at ≤20% luminance and ≤12% of the envelope's lit area, with no bloom and no live depth of field. The jewels take the palette accent instead of ruby red so red never becomes structure. Performance: a ≤15k-tri mesh pass with MSAA, drawn only into the envelope-clipped target, est. 0.1 ms; whole cluster est. 0.3 ms.

#### PaganiHorologerie — build #34 · cockpit
Category **Hypercar** · target `recreation` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 8 · rebuild.

**Category note.** The Pagani Huayra is in the game and the Utopia is not. Step 1 names the Pagani whose dials this dash recreates.

An atelier chronograph. Two Ø420 dials, speed on the left and tach on the right, sit inside a tonneau case drawn only as a 1 px polished chamfer (50% lit, 15% shadow) around true black. Between them, on X = 1920, a twin-disc grande-date window holds the gear. Clous-de-Paris guilloché shows only where the G-driven light rakes it. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 700 gear, cap 170, in the window, under 12 lume cartouches set in a straight bridge rail (34 × 800 px) that joins the dial crowns. Rank 2: tabular Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 96, low in the speed dial and 360 px from the gear. Rank 3: openworked primary-colour hands over Barlow Semi Condensed 500 numerals; the tach bezel is set per car so the shift rpm sits at 12. Rank 4: twin retrograde pedal fans under the window. Rank 5: the machined chamfers, and the crest under the tach's 6.

- **Signature moment:** Grande-date jump. On a gear change the units disc jumps behind the window like an outsize date at midnight; the tens disc makes 10 a true two-digit numeral. The old numeral leaves 24 px upward under a 1 px window-edge shadow. The new one rises from 1.12 on the fast spring, and the window's polished frame takes the 1-frame 60% rim flash. At the limiter only the cartouche rail strobes. The steady cue is the tach bezel's 12 o'clock cartouche, which fills with the shift colour and stays lit. On a car change, and only then, the bezel ratchets round (24 detents, 600 ms) to put the new car's shift rpm under 12. The retrograde pedal hands fly back to zero on a critically damped snap spring.
