- **Telemetry:** rpm drives the skeleton tach hand (needle spring with feed-forward) and the cartouche thresholds. CarOrdinal changes plus the car-profile shift rpm drive the bezel rotation. speed drives the speed hand and the tabular digits. gear drives the date discs, with R in warning and N at 72% ink. throttle and brake drive the retrograde fans (go / warning). Lateral G drives the light azimuth over the guilloché and chamfers, and the parallax (window 0, dials 2, case 4 px). fuel drives a small, shy power-reserve arc at 9 o'clock on the speed dial (rank 5, no glow). boost drives an optional secondary hand, shown only for turbo cars. fuel is not yet decoded by fh6-core; verify it against the golden packets.
- **References:** Pagani Utopia analog dials with exposed movement parts and a small screen between them; A. Lange & Söhne outsize-date (twin-disc big date) window; Breguet clous-de-Paris guilloché; retrograde complications
- **Phase 6 assets:** Clous-de-Paris normal map (P ≈ 12 px) and barleycorn (N ≈ 24), baked with mips; black anodised aluminium albedo/roughness for the case chamfer; a polished-steel matcap for the skeleton hands; the brand-crest dial variant. The date-disc numerals are MSDF, not textures. Procedural fallbacks: an analytic guilloché line field and a gradient chamfer.
- **Risk:** v1 drew an opaque navy capsule with gold outlines, a tiny gear and fake sub-dials, so it read as a flat sticker. The case must stay a 1 px chamfer around black, never a plate. Two equal dials can split attention away from a narrow window. Keep the window ≥220 px wide with the gear cap ≥150, keep the dial contrast below the gear's, and hold guilloché at ≤14 alpha with a mip bias to stop WRGB moiré. Performance: two normal-mapped Ø420 faces (~0.28 Mpx) with one fetch per pixel, est. 0.2 ms.

#### T50Floodlit — build #43 · cockpit
Category **Hypercar** · target `inspired` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 63 · new.

**Inspired by the Gordon Murray T.50** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "some hypercars and supercars inspiration clusters"; in the game as the 2022 Gordon Murray T.50). Checked against sources on 2026-11-28 (Gordon Murray Automotive's press release as quoted by Motor1, Top Gear, evo, Goodwood and CarExpert): the driver sits on the centre line and faces one large (120 mm) analogue rev counter, floodlit from the side (whether it is also backlit is not established), with a long needle machined from aluminium and a single orange oil-temperature warning light inside the dial; on each side a plain screen under a hood, white graphics on black ("no fancy dancing graphics"), the left for car, engine and aero information (fan speed, tyre temperatures, underbody pressure), the right for navigation and media; the V12 (by Cosworth, not rechecked here) revs to 12,100 rpm; there are no touchscreens and no stalks, and three aluminium rotary switches sit on each side of the binnacle; the central rev counter is a pared-back tribute to the McLaren F1's. The car was revealed on 4 August 2020. Not established: the number and place of the lamps, the dial's end numeral, its red zone, its colours and typeface, and whether it has shift lights. The idea taken from the car: an instrument that makes no light of its own. Only the gear, the speed, the shift lights and the two side panes emit; the dial is seen by the lamps alone.

Starting layout, mirrored exactly about the centre line, as the car is. Rank 3: one rev counter, Ø480 at (0, −10), with a satin chapter ring that carries the numerals, a matt black centre and a long machined needle that floats above the face. Two small hooded flood lamps sit on the rim at 35° each side of 12 o'clock. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 150) on a Ø200 cap over the needle's hub, so the needle passes under it, and 10 lamps on a straight strip above the dial (760 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 84) in the open sector at the foot of the scale, inside the dial. Rank 4: two equal panes, 300 × 220 at (±470, 0), power with a peak hold on the left and a G-ball on the right, both under small hoods and white on black, with nothing animated but their values, as the real screens are (their contents are this dash's own). Rank 5: engraved labels on the ring and a maker's plate.

- **Signature moment:** The shadows. Each lamp throws the needle's shadow on the chapter ring, so the needle always has two soft shadows, and they are computed, not painted: a point of the needle at height h above the face, lit by a lamp at height H, lands at L + (P − L)·H / (H − h), where L is the lamp's place and P the point's place on the face. The shadows are long and spread wide at low revs, shorten as the needle climbs toward the lamps, and fold under the needle as it passes between them. Over the last tenth of the revs before the shift point the lamps turn from white to the `shift` colour, steadily and without a flash, so the ring and the needle change colour by light alone. On the limiter only the strip above the dial strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → needle (`needle` spring with feed-forward), the scale's end numeral (the next whole thousand above max rpm, so the T.50 reads 0 to 13), red zone, lamp colour, the strip and the limiter; gear → centre cap; speed → tabular speed; power → left pane, with the peak of the current pull held until the next pull begins; acceleration → G-ball on the `gBall` spring; throttle and brake → one thin bar on the inner edge of each pane. The car's engine and throttle maps (announced in 2020 as GT, limited to 9,500 rpm and 600 PS, and Power; called GT and Sport in the 2025 road tests) are not in the telemetry and are not shown, and neither is the dial's orange oil-temperature light, which has no telemetry.
- **References:** Gordon Murray Automotive's T.50 press release as quoted by Motor1 (https://www.motor1.com/news/437374/gordon-murray-automotive-t50-debut/); Top Gear, "Geek out on the Murray T.50's amazing details" (https://www.topgear.com/car-news/british/geek-out-murray-t50s-amazing-details) and its interior review (https://www.topgear.com/car-reviews/gordon-murray-automotive/t50/interior); evo's 2025 review (https://evo.co.uk/gma/t50/207787/gordon-murray-automotive-t50-2025-review-the-ultimate-analogue-hypercar); Goodwood's reveal report (https://www.goodwood.com/grr/road/news/gordon-murrays-v12-t.50-supercar-revealed); CarExpert on the McLaren F1 lineage (https://carexpert.com.au/car-news/gma-t-50-mclaren-f1s-spiritual-successor-revealed-in-full); still to confirm in step 1 from photographs: the lamps, the scale and the colours; floodlit aircraft instruments with post lamps; sundials and gnomon geometry for the shadow; `PorscheAnalog` in this catalog for the rendered-dial method
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the dial as real parts (chapter ring in satin aluminium with engraved, paint-filled numerals; matt centre; the needle and the centre cap machined from solid; the two hooded lamps; a thin polished bezel), rendered once per lamp so the app can mix each lamp's brightness and colour. ComfyUI: the fine circular brushing of the chapter ring, a leather-wrapped binnacle surround for the edges and reflections, and the weave of the panes' carbon frames. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** An instrument lit only by lamps can look dead on an OLED: the gear, the speed and the strip are emissive and carry ranks 1 and 2, and the numerals on the ring must still pass the contrast gate with the lamps at their dimmest. A shadow can be read as a second needle: both shadows stay soft-edged, are never darker than 40% of the ring's lit level, and are drawn only on the ring. One round dial with numerals is close to `PorscheAnalog` and `HakosukaS20`: this dash has one dial and no tubes, visible lamps, exact mirror symmetry and a face that is black where no light falls. The car has no brand palette in the game (it uses the standard palette), so the dash takes its colour from the tokens and never from an invented GMA colour. Perf: two light-variant plates, one needle sprite, two shadow shapes of at most 8 segments each, est. 0.2 ms.

#### KoenigseggGyro — build #52 · cockpit
Category **Hypercar** · target `recreation` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 11 · rebuild.

The phantom wheel. This cockpit is wheel-less, so the cluster becomes the rim. A Ø540 carbon-and-titanium instrument ring rotates with steering input (±32° at full lock) while a gyro-held core inside it stays level, as on the Jesko. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 180, inside a fixed W-shaped reference symbol; 13 chevron pods sit on a level shelf (32 × 740 px) that never rotates. Rank 2: tabular B612 Mono Bold speed, cap 90, in a level window under the gear. Rank 3: a 9 px primary-colour rev arc with a white leading edge on the rotating ring, Barlow Semi Condensed numerals, and shift at the ring's 12 index. Rank 4: a horizon hairline and pitch ladder behind the core at ≤20% luminance. Rank 5: four level corner micro-labels, and the crest on the ring at 6.

- **Signature moment:** Counter-steer split. In a drift the ring turns with the counter-steer, the horizon tilts with body roll the other way, and the level core holds the gear dead still between them. Three layers move three ways and read as 'the car rotating, the hands catching it'. At the limiter only the level chevron shelf strobes. The ring's 12 o'clock index fills with a steady shift colour wherever the ring is turned. On first launch the ring self-tests −32° → +32° → 0 inside the 350/300 ms sweep.
- **Telemetry:** steer (i8, −127..127) drives the ring rotation on a critically damped 12 rad/s spring, clamped to ±32°. roll and pitch drive the horizon tilt and ladder offset (spring ω ≈10 rad/s); roll's sign and units must be verified on tests/golden/packets (97% rule). rpm drives the rev arc (ribbon spring with feed-forward) and the shelf thresholds. gear and speed drive the level core. Yaw rate (angular_velocity_y) and slip angle drive a small drift caret on the horizon. Lateral G drives the light azimuth over the carbon's anisotropic sheen and the parallax (core 0, horizon 2, ring 4 px). IsRaceOn 0→1 (first launch only) plays the self-test.
- **References:** Koenigsegg Jesko gyroscopic wheel display (key information kept upright as the rim turns); aircraft attitude indicator with a fixed W reference; F1 steering-wheel displays
- **Phase 6 assets:** Carbon twill (12 px) albedo with a normal map and anisotropic sheen for the ring band; brushed titanium for the inner rim; the brand logo dial variant; MSDF atlases for B612 Mono, Saira Condensed, Barlow Semi Condensed and Titillium Web. Procedural fallbacks: a 2×2 twill and conic banding.
- **Risk:** v1 drew a hatched grey plate with empty boxes. Here, a rotating rev scale loses some reading accuracy and can feel unsettling at full lock. Cap the rotation at ±32° on a non-overshooting spring and keep ranks 1–2 (gear, speed and the shift shelf) level. Offer a 'fixed ring' toggle in the OSD. Roll trust is unverified: if the golden packets show it unusable, derive the horizon tilt from lateral G with a clamp and label it as derived. Performance: SDF only, est. 0.15 ms.

#### RattrapanteSector — build #61 · cockpit+hood
Category **Hypercar** · target `concept` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 36 · new.

**Category note.** Hypercar through watchmaking: a split-seconds chronograph movement opened up and seen working. It has no named car.

A split-seconds chronograph opened into a sector. Two long lance hands rise from a hidden pivot 640 px below the anchor and sweep a 110° fan whose chapter arc (R 880) spans the binnacle, giving about 15 px per 100 rpm. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 210, in a sapphire lens (alpha 44, 1 px rim, crescent) on X = 1920 that the hands pass behind; 15 lume batons lie on a straight chord above the arc, 30 × 760 px. Rank 2: tabular Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 100, in the fan's right wing, which no hand reaches. Rank 3: a primary-colour main hand plus an accent hairline split hand. Idle→shift spans −55°→0°, and the top 15% of the range opens out to +20°. Rank 4: a 'shot group' of the last five shifts under the speed. Rank 5: a titanium arc rail and the Michroma name. Hood view: the same sector at R 1000 inside the 1760 × 400 envelope.

- **Signature moment:** Catch-up. On every upshift the split hand freezes at the rpm where the gear changed. The arc between it and the 12 o'clock index fills in three steps: go within ±100 rpm, caution within ±300, warning beyond. The arc's length carries the same message, so colour is never the only cue. After 1.2 s the split hand catches up to the main hand on the fast spring. It is a shift coach for a manual-gearbox driver, built from a horology complication. At the limiter only the baton chord strobes, and the main hand's lume core burns a steady shift colour. At idle both hands sit superimposed and still.
- **Telemetry:** rpm drives the main hand (needle spring with feed-forward) and the baton thresholds. A gear-increment edge, with the rpm from the frame before the drop, captures the split hand; the clutch byte confirms a real shift. Downshifts are optional: they capture the blip peak against a rev-match target from per-gear rpm/speed ratios learned live into a fixed [f32; 10] table. The car-profile shift rpm sets 12 o'clock (fallback: limiter × 0.97). speed drives the tabular readout. The last five shift deltas live in a fixed [f32; 5] ring buffer for the shot group. Lateral G drives the light on the rail and the hand ridges. All of these fields are already decoded.
- **References:** Split-seconds (rattrapante) chronographs; 1930s sector ('fan') speedometers and pressure gauges; Porsche 992.2 shift-at-12 rev counter; AiM/MoTeC per-gear shift thresholds
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: SDF hands, arc, batons and lens; MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed, Barlow Semi Condensed and Michroma. An optional brushed-titanium tangential map can be added for the arc rail.
- **Risk:** Hands about 900 px long sweep across the game scene and can cut through the road view. Clip them to the envelope, fade the roots behind the gear lens, keep them ≤14 px wide, and end the sector at ±55° so the tips stay inside 1440 px (1760 px on the hood). The coach must never be mistaken for the live reading: the split hand is a 2 px accent hairline with no bloom. Performance: SDF only, est. 0.12 ms.

#### BionicSpaceframe — build #68 · cockpit
Category **Hypercar** · target `inspired` · family Race Dash · catalog row 39 · new.

**Category note.** Inspired by the Czinger 21C's printed node chassis: the frame is the dash. The 21C is not in the game.

No panel, no bezel ring: the cluster hangs in a generative titanium spaceframe grown along stress lines from two column mounts (±420, +210) to a top beam and a central hub ring. At most 48 brushed struts (tangent-aligned anisotropic GGX, base ≤12% luminance) hold every module in a machined node boss: 10 pods in beam nodes, the `race` 800 gear at cap 240 inside the hub, speed (`race` 700, cap 112) in a left sub-node, corner-ticked lap tiles between right struts, four tyre nodes where the lower struts meet. Rank 3 is a 12 px LED channel milled along the beam. One `light` slides highlights along every strut through a corner; rims 1 px; only ranks 1–3 glow.

- **Signature moment:** Load path: on each upshift a specular glint (non-emissive, 60% white peak) leaves the hub ring and runs down the strut chains to both column mounts at 4000 px/s, as if the shift torque flowed through the frame into the car; on a downshift it runs mount to hub. At the limiter the 10 pods strobe inside the 36 px beam strip while their node bosses hold a steady `shift` reflection. Idle is still: constant G means frozen highlights.
- **Telemetry:** acceleration_x → light azimuth (strut highlights) and ±4 px frame parallax; rpm + car-profile thresholds → pods, strobe and beam LED channel; gear up/down edge → load-path glint direction; speed_ms → speed; tyre temps ×4 → wheel nodes in thermal steps, combined slip → their 1 px slip outline; lap fields → right tiles (free roam: power_watts, boost_psi, distance); LIM, FUEL and synthesised TC/ABS → two lamps per side node.
- **References:** Czinger 21C additively manufactured node chassis; topology-optimised (generative-design) aerospace brackets; Bugatti Tourbillon skeleton stack (depth without boxes); Apple Liquid Glass highlights that travel with geometry
- **Phase 6 assets:** Seeded lattice from tools/asset-forge (Blender geometry-nodes stress-line recipe) as a ≤20k-tri glTF plus a baked 1440×560 RG16F distance+tangent field; brushed-titanium anisotropy/normal tile; procedural fallback: ≤48 SDF capsules from the same seed, baked to the same field at load.
- **Risk:** Design: the lattice turns into greeble and pulls the eye from ranks 1–3. Avoid: every strut must carry a module (stress-line recipe, ≤48 struts), static luminance ≤12%, the zen tier fades everything but beam and hub. Perf: one pass over the envelope sampling the baked field plus anisotropic specular ≈0.03 ms; the glint is a 1D parameter along precomputed strut arc length (no extra pass); bloom on beam, pods and gear ≈0.12 ms; ≈0.2 ms total.

### Motorsport

#### ★ Gt7HoodPro — build #3 · hood
Category **Motorsport** · target `recreation` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 3 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Its rebuild recreates the Bonnet View meter of Gran Turismo 7. TJX, 2026-11-28: "its just clean". It leads the Motorsport category, and `Gt7CockpitPro` is its cockpit sibling.

Design board (recorded before the re-cut): Penpot `07 · Gt7HoodPro` (`6fe75c5e-bb22-8084-8008-fd908bb325c8`) · `mockups/Gt7HoodPro/index.html`.

Frameless 'gull-wing horizon' hung from one 1 px hairline (alpha 46, ±820 px). Twin mirrored ribbon tachs (7→24 px, power 1.6, primary→white core, comet orb) sweep from the wingtips (±820, −20) up to a crest at (0, −150). Idle sits at the tips and the car's shiftRpm at the crest. Titillium Web 600 numerals appear on the left wing only, with modern11 diamonds (28 px) 40 px above the crest. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 150, in a 250×210 oledBlack squircle with a light-driven 1 px rim and a feathered dimming pad (the hood scrim is 0%). Rank 2: Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 84, below it. Rank 4: thermal-step slip capsules on the pod's four corners make the pod read as the car; G puck on the right, THR/BRK on the left. Only ribbons, diamonds and gear glow.

- **Signature moment:** Apex fuse: as revs build the two comet orbs race inward. At the car's own shiftRpm they meet at the crest directly above the gear and smooth-union (k 10 px) into one steady 6×36 px vertical blade in `shift`, joining the ribbon to the centre diamond. This is the family's 'ribbon locks vertical' moment, timed with the outside-in diamond fill. At the limiter only the 28 px diamond strip strobes (10 Hz, 50% duty); the blade and the pod rim stay a steady `shift`. On an upshift the blade splits and both comets drop back outward to the new rpm on the `ribbon` spring with feed-forward, while the gear does the standard 1.12→1.0 entry.
- **Telemetry:** rpm current/idle/max plus car-profile shift and per-gear thresholds → twin ribbon positions, diamond stages, apex fuse, blade colour; gear → pod glyph (N at 72% ink, R in warning); speed → 3-cell tabular speed with ghost 8s and 0.6 hysteresis; tyre temps FL/FR/RL/RR → capsule thermal steps (shy °C numerals only outside the optimal band); tyre slip ratio / combined slip ×4 → capsule outline amber >0.08, red >0.18; acceleration x/z → G puck (felt force −a, gBall spring, 500 ms dot trail); throttle/brake → pedal capsules; lateral G → light azimuth on every rim and 0/2/4 px parallax; lap number/current/best → shy lap line above the pedal capsules, only when lap > 0.
- **References:** Gran Turismo 7 race-screen meter (Polyphony Digital); F1 2026 McLaren MCL40 progressive shift LEDs; Porsche 992.2 shift-at-12 track mode; Lucid Gravity bezel-free floating OLED
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (SDF ribbons, diamonds, squircle, capsules, G rings). Uses the shared MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600/800 and Titillium Web 600 and the 64×64 blue-noise dither tile. Retire assets/gt7_hood_pro_plate.png (the v1 opaque plate).
- **Risk:** Design: in the hood view (scrim 0%) the cluster sits over bright road and sky, so thin ribbons and the speed can fail the blur gate, and two converging tachs could read as duplicates. Fix: a feathered dimming pad (alpha ≤140) behind gear and speed only; the right-wing ribbon at 70% intensity with ticks but no numerals; diamonds kept 40 px clear of the crest. Perf: about 20 SDF draws in the 1760×400 clip plus half-res bloom of the emissive buffer ≈0.12 ms, with no full-screen pass.

#### ★ BoschDdu — build #4 · cockpit
Category **Motorsport** · target `recreation` · family Race Dash · catalog row 12 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Its rebuild recreates the Bosch Motorsport DDU 10 race display.

Design board (recorded before the re-cut): Penpot `07 · BoschDdu` `b568ed3f-64cd-806f-8008-fd94834f5850`.

Fixes the hero's faults: dead lamps, boxed tiles, a fixed 0–9 scale. An open-bottom U of 2×2 carbon twill, 14 px wide, 1400×540, with a deep 28 px 45° chamfer whose lit flank (55%) and shadow flank (18%) follow `light`; four Allen bolts. Inside: a true-black cavity under bonded AR cover glass, an 18/255 crescent, 1 px `glass` AR rim tint. Rank 1: ten 36 px microprism pods over a `race` 800 gear at cap 240. Rank 2: speed, cap 112, left. Rank 3: 45-segment bar numbered from the car's max rpm. Rank 4: signed long-G bar above the gear, thermal tyre quad below. Corner-ticked tiles, Titillium Web labels; three data-bound alarm lamps per side (16 LEDs in all, the CARTEK spirit), lit without bloom.

- **Signature moment:** The family signature, owned here: pods light in the same frame each per-car, per-gear threshold is crossed, green → amber → red, outer pair first, the last landing on the centre pod above the gear; at shift rpm all ten take `shift` (#2F6BFF default) and at the limiter only the 36 px pod strip strobes at 10 Hz, 50% duty. BoschDdu's one steady cue: the upper carbon chamfer takes a non-emissive `shift` reflection ramped 0→60% over 150 ms, as if the pods spill onto the bevel; it never flashes. Event page: crossing the line swaps the right tiles to LAST / Δ BEST for 4 s with a stepped 150 ms crossfade.
- **Telemetry:** current_engine_rpm + engine_max_rpm + car-profile shift/limiter rpm → pods, strobe, 45-segment bar and its numerals; gear → hero glyph; speed_ms → tabular mph/km/h with 0.6 hysteresis; acceleration_z → signed long-G bar; acceleration_x → light azimuth and 0/2/4 px parallax; tyre temps ×4 → thermal steps; lap number, current/last/best lap, race position → tiles (free roam: distance, boost_psi, power_watts); lamps: LIM (rpm ≥ limiter), FUEL (<10%), TYRE (any tyre overheated), SLIP (combined slip), and TC / ABS synthesised from driven-wheel slip ratio per drivetrain_type with throttle >0.2 or brake >0.3 (thresholds prop., labelled derived).
- **References:** Bosch Motorsport DDU 10/11 (10 top + side RGB lamps, 6-step brightness, event pages); Cosworth CCW Mk3 (10 shift + 6 alarm); MoTeC SLM-C (warnings outrank shift stages); Porsche 963 top light bar
- **Phase 6 assets:** Carbon twill albedo+normal tile (12 px weave, 512², seamless) with the 2×2 procedural fallback; microprism pod-lens normal map 128² (procedural concentric-ridge fallback); Allen bolt normal sprite 64² (SDF fallback); 128² blue-noise dither tile; MSDF Saira Condensed 700/800 and Titillium Web 600 with a tnum check.
- **Risk:** Design: sliding back into the hero's dense boxed rectangle where ranks 2–5 share one weight. Avoid: corner-tick tiles, 3 lamps per side, static structure ≤20% luminance, race/zen tiers. Perf: bloom only on pods, gear and bar inside a 1400×600 scissor (8 levels from 256 px) ≈0.12 ms; twill + chamfer + glass in one SDF pass ≈0.03 ms; ≈0.25 ms total. Blockers: FH6TelemetryData today lacks tyre temps, slip ratio, combined slip, fuel and lap fields (extend the decoder against golden packets); gear_string() allocates a String, so use a static glyph table; COMPONENTS §14 lets lit alarms glow, but the rank rule wins (no bloom).

#### DriftGlass — build #13 · cockpit
Category **Motorsport** · target `concept` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 5 · rebuild.

**Category note.** A drift scoring tool. The helmet visor is its housing and stays, built by absorption on the kit's glass and not by a flat alpha. From the Motorsport category it takes exact data and shift lights that behave like the real hardware; it keeps its own family's materials.

'Smoked visor': one 1240×300 Tinted glass visor (alpha 56–64, glassTint) with a helmet-curved top and tapered flanks; its lower 40 px feather to zero. Two 1 px creases split it into three facets, so the G-driven light swaps brightness between them in corners. It has a polished 1 px top rim and a 2 px edge-glow lip. Rank 1: outsideIn15 pills (24 px) above the visor, and a Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 200, in a faceted hexagonal smoked lozenge at X 1920. Rank 2: Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 112, 300 px right. Rank 3: a 12 px light pipe inside the visor's top edge, primary→white meniscus. Rank 4: a 180° drift fan under the gear. Shy Titillium labels.

- **Signature moment:** Slip wedge with peak scribe: above 8° slip, a smoked wedge snaps open in the fan from the travel-direction axis to the body heading. Its colour steps by band: 8–20° primary, 20–40° accent, >40° caution, with a 150 ms crossfade. A 1 px steer tick on the fan rim shows counter-steer against the wedge, and the G comet rides the same pivot. The slide's peak angle leaves a scribed tick held for 2 s; once the slide ends (β < 4° for 300 ms), 'PEAK 41°' shows for 1.5 s. At the limiter only the pill strip strobes, and the visor lip warms to a steady `shift`.
- **Telemetry:** local velocity x/z → β = atan2(v_lat, v_long), hidden below 5 m/s, with 8°/4° hysteresis; angular velocity y → wedge feed-forward; steer (normalised, no wheel-angle claim) → counter-steer tick; acceleration x/z → G comet direction and length (gBall spring); rear tyre combined slip → wedge edge amber; handbrake flag → shy HB label; rpm/idle/max + shift → light pipe and pills; gear; speed; throttle → meniscus intensity; lateral G → light azimuth (facet swap) and 0/2/4 px parallax.
- **References:** D1 Grand Prix DOSS drift-angle scoring overlays; tinted race-helmet visors; Apple iOS 26.1 Tinted Liquid Glass; Mercedes AR-HUD sparse symbology
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: faceted visor SDF, tint, frost noise (1.4 px, 3.5%) and the blue-noise dither tile; shared MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600/800 and Titillium Web 600.
- **Risk:** Design: Tinted glass at alpha 64 over a bright scene can read as a grey box (OLED rule 7), and a wedge flickering at slide transitions would distract. Fix: tint only inside the facets, with the 40 px feather and no fill below the visor; no live blur of game pixels (smoke = tint + frost noise + rims); β hysteresis and the `snap` spring with feed-forward. Perf: visor and fan are analytic in one 1240×520 clipped pass plus instanced pills ≈0.1 ms.

#### Gt7CockpitPro — build #22 · cockpit
Category **Motorsport** · target `inspired` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 55 · new.

**The cockpit sibling of `Gt7HoodPro`** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "we already have Gt7HoodPro nearly finished id like some other variants of this as its just clean"). `Gt7HoodPro` exists only in the hood view. This dash is the same clean instrument for the cockpit view, so that pressing RB between cockpit and hood changes the camera and not the language. Start from the finished `Gt7HoodPro` (its `mod.rs`, `RESEARCH.md` and `HANDOVER.md`) and from Gran Turismo 7's own race-screen meter, which step 1 researches again for the cockpit and chase views; the layout below is the starting point.

Starting layout, frameless and detached, on the 1440 × 560 binnacle. Rank 3: the two mirrored ribbon tachs of the hood dash rise from the lower outer corners at (±690, +170) and meet in a pointed arch at (0, −270), idle at the feet and the car's shift rpm at the crest, numerals on the left limb only. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 190) in an `oledBlack` squircle at (0, −70) under the crest, with 11 diamonds (28 px, 760 px span) on a straight line above the arch's shoulders. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 100) under the gear. Rank 4: inside the arch's left flank the pedal capsules, inside its right flank the G puck, each small and without glow; a shy lap line under the speed. Only the ribbons, the diamonds and the gear glow.

- **Signature moment:** The apex fuse, shared with the hood dash: the two comet heads climb the arch and meet at the crest at the car's own shift rpm, where they fuse into one steady vertical blade in `shift` that points down at the gear. On the limiter only the diamond line strobes. On an upshift the blade splits and both comets drop back down their limbs on the `ribbon` spring. What is new here is the handover: on a change of view the gear, the speed and the diamonds hold their size and order, so the eye finds them where the other view left them.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → ribbons, diamonds, fuse and limiter; gear → squircle numeral (N at 72% ink, R in `warning`); speed → tabular speed with ghost digits; throttle and brake → pedal capsules; acceleration → G puck on the `gBall` spring with a 500 ms trail; lateral G → the light on every rim; speed and timestamp → the acceleration timers on the lap line. Tyre slip and tyre temperature are not verified: the corner capsules of the hood dash appear here only after those fields are verified in the real game, and until then the flanks hold pedals and G.
- **References:** Gran Turismo 7 race-screen meter in the cockpit and chase views (Polyphony Digital); `Gt7HoodPro` and `Gt7ModularTripod` in this catalog; Porsche 992.2 shift-at-12 track mode; Formula 1 progressive shift lights
- **Phase 6 assets:** Shares the hood dash's parts: compose them from `common/` and ask the coordinator for a shared helper where one is missing; never copy its module. Blender: the gear squircle's rim and the diamond caps relit for the cockpit, plus the arch's two feet as machined end caps. ComfyUI: the fine matrix under the ribbons that the hood dash uses, re-made at the cockpit scale. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800 and Titillium Web 600.
- **Risk:** A second dash in the same language can be a copy: the arch, the cockpit proportions and the handover between views are its reasons to exist, and the closing review compares the two side by side. A tall arch puts the ribbons close to the gear: keep 60 px of true black between the ribbon and the squircle, and the right limb at 70% intensity so the two limbs do not read as two tachs. In the cockpit the scrim hides the game's dash, so no dimming pad is needed. Perf: the hood dash's draws re-placed, est. 0.12 ms.

#### SimInputTrace — build #31 · cockpit+hood
Category **Motorsport** · target `concept` · family Race Dash · catalog row 59 · new.

**A sim racer's input overlay as a dash** (TJX, 2026-11-28: after the clean Gran Turismo variants, "some more sim like"). Sim racing has its own instrument that no road car carries: the input telemetry overlay that streamers and coaches run over iRacing, Assetto Corsa Competizione and rFactor 2, with scrolling traces of throttle, brake, clutch and steering beside the pedal bars. Step 1 researches the real ones (the common overlay tools, and the trace views of MoTeC i2 and the sims' own telemetry screens) and names the one this dash is benchmarked against.

Starting layout, on a strict grid with tabular figures. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 200) at (0, −110), under a row of 16 shift LEDs (780 × 32) in the green, red, blue order of sim hardware. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 100) to the right of the gear. Rank 3: a segmented rev bar under the LEDs, numbered from `EngineMaxRpm`. Rank 4: the trace window, 1000 × 170 at (0, +110): the last 8 s scroll from right to left at a fixed 125 px a second, with throttle, brake and steering each on the same 0 to 100% height, and at its right edge the live pedal bars and a steering tick. Rank 5: labels `THR`, `BRK`, `STR`, a time grid every second and the logo zone. Hood view: the trace window alone, 1500 × 110, with gear and speed at its right end.

- **Signature moment:** The shift mark. Every gear change drops a thin vertical line into the trace window with the new gear's number above it, so the driver can read the last eight seconds like a lap coach: where the brake came off, where the throttle came back in, and how far apart they were. Time spent on the limiter is hatched on the rev row above the traces, and time with neither pedal pressed is marked `COAST` on the baseline. On the limiter only the LED row strobes, as sim wheels do; nothing in the trace window flashes.
- **Telemetry:** throttle, brake and steer (all verified) → the three traces, sampled once a frame into fixed ring buffers of 512 values each, no allocation; rpm, idle and max with per-gear thresholds → LED row, rev bar and limiter hatching; gear → numeral and shift marks; speed → tabular speed; acceleration → an optional fourth trace, longitudinal G. Clutch and handbrake are decoded but not covered by the golden packets: they join the traces only after the real-game check (97% rule). No ABS or traction-control lamp is drawn: the packet has neither.
- **References:** Input telemetry overlays for iRacing and Assetto Corsa Competizione; MoTeC i2 time-distance graphs; Fanatec and Simucube wheel LED rows; `BoschDdu` in this catalog for the Race Dash grid
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the LED row as a rendered bar of lens caps in a machined carrier, and a slim anodised frame edge for the trace window with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: the anti-glare matrix of the display face and a dark anodised surface for the carrier. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800, Chakra Petch 500 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** Three traces told apart by colour alone fail for colour-blind drivers: each trace has its own weight and style (throttle a solid 3 px line, brake a filled area, steering a 1.5 px line about the centre) and its label at the right edge. Long polylines are expensive in the kit (one 128-point line cost 0.36 ms in the pilot): draw each trace as a height-field ribbon or in chunks of at most 8 segments, and measure before anything else is built. A scrolling window pulls the eye: it scrolls at a constant speed, sits at rank 4 luminance and never blooms. Est. 0.3 ms, the highest of the Motorsport dashes.

#### Gt7ModularTripod — build #40 · cockpit+hood
Category **Motorsport** · target `recreation` · family Race Dash · catalog row 14 · rebuild.

**TJX override (2026-11-23): build the real GT7 in-car multi-function meter, not the capsule/prism concept below.** Reference: `mockups/references/gt7_multifunction_meter.webp` (local only, gitignored; copyrighted, never commit it). It has detached floating elements over the scene with no backing panel: a left round speedometer (thin white scale, light numerals, inset fuel sub-gauge, odometer box); a right round tachometer (red arc from the shift zone, inset boost sub-gauge in ×100 kPa); a central trapezoid pod with a dense tick rev bar (one accent colour) over big squared speed and gear digits with an MT tag; under it a D-pad-paged centre panel (Brake Balance, Radar and other pages, page dots, ◀ ▶) flanked by slim vertical input bars; a tyre-diagram block at the far left; circled telltale icons along the bottom. Monochrome white and grey, one accent, translucent dark panels, thin precise lines. Pages show only decoded telemetry (no brake balance or radar data exists, so pick honest pages: G, inputs, power and torque, trail). The sections below are background only; where they conflict, the real GT7 meter wins.

Three machined black-anodised aluminium capsules: a 600×480 centre module and two 360×280 satellites yawed 9° toward the driver by a homography and set one depth layer back. Each capsule is a thin spun-grain ring with a 3 px polished 45° chamfer lit by `light`, around a true-black cavity. Rank 1: one floating 36 px strip of 11 pods (3+5+3) bridging all three tops, plus the `race` 800 gear at cap 228 centre. Rank 2: speed, cap 112, beneath it. Rank 3: a 60-segment arch tach cresting over the gear. Rank 4: each satellite is a three-faced prism of pages (left TYRES / G / INPUTS, right LAP / POWER / TRIP), with two shy lamps on its outer edge.

- **Signature moment:** Prism page turn: an event rolls a satellite's triangular prism 120° about its horizontal axis on the `default` spring (237 ms); the two visible faces foreshorten by cos θ and shade by dot(n, L) from the cluster light, like a trackside three-sided rotating billboard. Brake >0.5 with combined slip flips the left to TYRES; a completed lap flips the right to LAP; at most one turn per 2.5 s, never on the centre module. The outside-in fill jumps the two capsule gaps to land on the centre pod; at the limiter the strip strobes while the arch tach's last 6 segments hold a steady `shift`.
- **Telemetry:** rpm + car-profile thresholds → 11-pod strip, strobe and arch tach (segments numbered from engine_max_rpm); gear → centre glyph; speed_ms → speed; tyre temps ×4 + combined slip → TYRES face; acceleration_x/z → G face ball (felt force, −acceleration) and light/parallax; accel/brake/clutch/hand_brake → INPUTS face bars; lap fields + synthesised distance-based delta → LAP face; power_watts, torque, boost_psi → POWER face; fuel + distance → TRIP face; brake, slip and the lap-number edge → page-turn events; lamps LIM, FUEL and synthesised TC/ABS.
- **References:** Bugatti Tourbillon (one hero centre dial with two satellites); three-sided rotating trivision billboards; Bosch DDU event-triggered page changes; Pagani Utopia black anodised machined cluster
- **Phase 6 assets:** Spun-grain (tangential) anisotropy map, strength in R and rotation in G, for the capsule rings, plus a 45° chamfer normal profile; procedural anisotropic fallback; MSDF Saira Condensed 700/800 and Titillium Web 600; prism faces carry live vector content, no bitmaps.
- **Risk:** Design: three capsules read as three gadgets, and page turns tug the periphery. Avoid: one shared pod strip and one light tie the trio; satellites carry only ranks 4–5; turns are event-only and rate-limited; the zen tier hides the satellites. Perf: prism faces are live vectors under a vertical scale (no render-to-texture), the homography runs in the vertex stage, rings are one anisotropic pass: ≈0.2 ms. Speed stays in the centre module, so it is always within 420 px of the gear. Hood [4]: satellites move to ±700 px at 6° yaw.

#### SimPitWall — build #49 · cockpit
Category **Motorsport** · target `concept` · family Race Dash · catalog row 65 · new.

**The race engineer's screen as a dash** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "some more sim like"). Where `SimInputTrace` shows what the driver does, this dash shows what the car does, in the look of the analysis screens an engineer watches on the pit wall: a g-g diagram, a power curve and a table of channel maxima. Step 1 researches the real tools (MoTeC i2, Cosworth Pi Toolbox, McLaren ATLAS and the telemetry pages of the sims) and names the one this dash is benchmarked against.

Starting layout, on a strict grid with tabular figures and no ornament. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 200) at (0, −60), under a row of 14 shift LEDs (760 × 32). Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 100) under the gear. Rank 3: the rev bar across the top, drawn over the car's own power curve, which the dash learns as it is driven: the bar's fill shows the rpm, and the curve behind it shows where the power peaks. Rank 4, left: a Ø300 g-g diagram with rings at 0.5 g, the live dot with a 500 ms trail, and the envelope the driver has reached, held in 24 sectors. Rank 4, right: a channel table of six rows, each a label, a live value and a held maximum: lateral G left and right, braking G, acceleration G, top speed and peak power. Rank 5: unit labels and the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** A bigger envelope. When the live dot pushes past the stored envelope in any sector, that edge moves out with it and holds, the new edge stays highlighted for 1.5 s in a steady `accent`, and a small tag shows the gain, for example `+0.04 g`. The driver sees the car's limits grow corner by corner. On an upshift a marker drops on the rev bar from the rpm before the shift to the rpm after it, on top of the power curve, so it is plain whether the next gear lands in the power. On the limiter only the LED row strobes.
- **Telemetry:** acceleration → g-g dot, the 24-sector envelope (a fixed `[f32; 24]`) and the table's four G rows; rpm and power → the learned power curve (peak power per 250 rpm band in a fixed `[f32; 48]`, cleared when the car ordinal changes); rpm, idle and max with per-gear thresholds → rev bar, LED row and limiter; gear → numeral and the shift marker; speed → tabular speed and the top-speed row; boost → an optional seventh row. Every channel is a verified field. Tyre, suspension and temperature channels, which a real pit wall shows, are not verified: they are left out, and four friction circles are added only after the real-game check.
- **References:** MoTeC i2 g-g and histogram views; Cosworth Pi Toolbox; McLaren ATLAS; the engineer's page of endurance and Formula 1 timing screens; `SimInputTrace` and `BoschDdu` in this catalog
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the LED row in a machined carrier, and thin anodised frame edges for the diagram and the table with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: the anti-glare matrix of the display face and a carbon surface for the carrier. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800, Chakra Petch 500 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** An analysis screen is dense by nature and the rank order can drown: gear, speed and the LED row are the only things at full light, the diagram and the table stay under 35% luminance, and the zen tier hides the table. A learned power curve is empty for the first pull: draw the plain rev bar until three bands hold data, and never show a curve from another car. Held maxima must not be read as live values: live values are tabular and bright, held ones are small, dim and marked `MAX`. Perf: the curve is 48 points in chunks of 8, the envelope 24 points, est. 0.2 ms.

#### RackFocus — build #58 · cockpit+hood
Category **Motorsport** · target `concept` · family Race Dash · catalog row 38 · new.

An obsidian monolith with three focal planes and no frame: a 1400×500 slab implied only by a 1 px `light`-driven top rim and an 18/255 crescent along its upper edge. Plane 0 (sharp, no parallax): 10 slim 34 px pods, the `race` 800 gear at cap 250, speed in `display` 600 at cap 112 beside it. Plane 1 (±2 px): a 60-segment bar with per-car numerals. Plane 2 (±4 px): corner-ticked Titillium Web tiles for laps, tyres, G-ball and inputs, plus shy alarm lamps. Depth is optical, not boxed: planes separate by parallax and a 10% dimming step, never by plates. Only ranks 1–3 glow.

- **Signature moment:** The rack focus: when combined /G/ ≥ 0.85 g or brake ≥ 0.35 (release below 0.5 g for 400 ms, minimum hold 1.2 s), plane 2 pulls out of focus (σ 0 → 8 px), scales to 0.97 and dims to 35% over 300 ms on the emphasized-decelerate curve, leaving gear, speed and shift lights razor-sharp through the apex; on corner exit it racks back in over 300 ms. Lit alarms are exempt and stay sharp. The outgoing gear defocuses as it leaves; the incoming one is never blurred.
- **Telemetry:** acceleration_x/z → combined G for the focus trigger, light azimuth and parallax; brake → focus trigger; rpm + car-profile thresholds → pods, strobe and bar; gear, speed_ms → plane 0; tyre temps ×4, combined slip, lap fields with a synthesised distance-based delta, accel/brake/clutch → plane 2 tiles; LIM, FUEL and synthesised TC/ABS → lamps.
- **References:** Cinema rack-focus / follow-focus grammar; Lucid Gravity Zen theme; BMW Panoramic iDrive shy-tech; GT7 'Race Info Only' HUD density setting
- **Phase 6 assets:** procedural only (shared blue-noise dither tile; MSDF Saira Condensed and Titillium Web)
- **Risk:** Design: data vanishing when wanted (mid-corner tyre temps), and the focus change itself drawing the eye. Avoid: user thresholds in the OSD, critical alarms exempt, at most one transition per 1.2 s, ranks 1–3 never touched. Perf: plane 2 renders to a half-res 720×260 target, a separable 9-tap Gaussian with σ animated by mip blend ≈0.04 ms, composite ≈0.01 ms, bloom on ranks 1–3 ≈0.12 ms; ≈0.2 ms total. Hood [4]: the slab widens to 1760×400 and plane 2 tiles move to the wings.

### Retro

#### ★ PorscheAnalog — build #2 · cockpit
Category **Retro** · target `recreation` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 7 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Retro by TJX's word (2026-11-28: "like retro we already have recreation of the Porsche 911 (992) five-tube cluster"). The dash is built; its module holds it as built.

Design board (recorded before the re-cut): Penpot `07 · PorscheAnalog` (`b568ed3f-64cd-806f-8008-fd946dce7013`), boards `b568ed3f-64cd-806f-8008-` + 01–04 `fd946df67f24`, `fd946ee9892c`, `fd946f74f160`, `fd946ff7365f` · `mockups/PorscheAnalog/index.html`.

Pattern-setter (★). A Ø520 centre tach under domed sapphire owns the binnacle. Two Ø330 satellites, speed on the left and a G/lap chrono on the right, tuck behind its titanium ring at −4 px parallax, which puts the speed 400 px from the gear. The faces are oledBlack; their sunburst finish shows only as a G-driven anisotropic lobe at ≤20% luminance. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 200, in a hollow hub that the needle orbits, under 15 lume jewels on a flat 38 × 760 px visor. Rank 2: tabular Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 112, with ghost 8s. Rank 3: a primary-colour dauphine needle over Barlow Semi Condensed numerals. A two-rate Track scale pins shift at 12, magnifies shift→limiter 4× into 12→1:30 and carries the lume operating band. Rank 4: G-ball and lap chrono. Rank 5: brushed titanium, with the crest or Michroma name in the dead quadrant.

- **Signature moment:** Plumb line. The jewels fill outside-in on per-car, per-gear thresholds with 0 ms attack. The last one is the centre jewel on X = 1920, and it lights in the same frame that the needle stands vertical beneath it. That jewel is a second light for the needle only, so its colour runs down the dauphine's polished ridge while the two facets keep their G-lit and shadow split. At the limiter only the 38 px visor strobes (10 Hz, 50% duty). The hollow-hub bearing ring holds a steady shift-colour glow, the one steady cue; it replaces the hero frame's full-face red wash. The gear may pulse at 2 Hz after 0.5 s on the limiter. Gear change: the token motion (old glyph to 0.92 in 50 ms, new glyph from 1.12 with a 24 px slide on the fast spring) plus one 60% rim flash that runs once round the hub ring.
- **Telemetry:** rpm drives the needle (needle spring T 0.07, ζ 0.75, velocity feed-forward) and the jewel thresholds. EngineMaxRpm and EngineIdleRpm, plus the car-profile shift, peak-torque and peak-power rpm, set the Track scale and the lume band. Fallbacks: shift = limiter × 0.97, and the band is learned from a fixed 64-bin rpm→power/torque max table filled from the packet's power and torque fields. gear drives the hub glyph. speed drives the tabular digits and the speed satellite's needle. Lateral G drives the light azimuth (light spring) and the 0/2/4 px parallax. G lat/long drive the G-ball; current, last and best lap drive the chrono sub-hands. IsRaceOn 0→1 (first launch only) plays the sweep. Torque and the lap fields are not yet decoded by fh6-core::telemetry; add them and verify them against tests/golden/packets.
- **References:** Porsche 992.2 Classic cluster with the rev counter rotated to put the shift point at 12; Porsche 550 Spyder tachometer (green operating band); Patek Philippe sunburst dial finishing; Bosch DDU / AiM LED-lens shift pods
- **Phase 6 assets:** Brushed-titanium albedo/roughness with a spun (tangential) anisotropy map (R strength, G rotation) for the rings; a sunburst anisotropy map for the faces; a sapphire-dome normal map; a polished-steel hub matcap; the brand-logo dial variant (mono white); MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed, Barlow Semi Condensed, Titillium Web and Michroma. Every item has a procedural fallback (conic banding, analytic sunburst, analytic dome).
- **Risk:** The hero frame fails three rules. Its jewel arc (≈160 × 530 px) breaks both the ≤42 px strobe strip and the ≥720 px span. Its speed sits 506 px from the gear (Z2 allows ≤420). Its redline floods the whole face red. The flat visor, the tucked satellites and the hub-ring cue fix all three. The remaining risk is that the two-rate scale misreads at mid-range. Mitigation: it is used only when the car profile has a shift rpm, with numerals every 1000 and 100 rpm ticks only inside the magnified wedge; otherwise the symmetric 270° token face is used. Performance: three analytic faces (~0.55 Mpx), ~180 instanced ticks, ~40 MSDF glyphs and a bloom chain clipped to the 1440 × 560 envelope, est. 0.2–0.3 ms.

#### ★ RetroCyber — build #6 · cockpit+hood
Category **Retro** · target `recreation` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 19 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Its rebuild recreates the Panoramic Digital Meter of the Toyota Supra (A70).

Design board (recorded before the re-cut): Penpot `07 · RetroCyber` (`6fe75c5e-bb22-8084-8008-fd924684ddca`) · `mockups/RetroCyber/index.html`.

A vacuum-fluorescent tube in a machined housing: a 10 px gunmetal chamfer45 ring with light-driven 28 px chamfers around a 1380x500 #000000 cavity. Four VFD layers: Seg7 phosphor (0 px); a 6 px hex grid that only modulates lit phosphor; five 1 px filament wires seen as dark lines across lit segments (+1 px); front glass shown only by a light-driven crescent and 1 px rim, never a tint (+3 px). Rank 1: octagon-bay Seg7 gear, cap 220, 45% white core, under a 38 px slanted 15-pod strip. Rank 2: Seg7 speed, cap 112, ghost 8s. Rank 3: a 24-chevron rising arc, 14 primary, 6 secondary, 4 accent. Rank 4: unglowing THR/BRK/BST columns and a 13x5 dot G-scope. Scanlines multiply emission.

- **Signature moment:** Filament ignition boot (the family's sector boot). On the first IsRaceOn 0->1 after launch, the five filaments warm as 1 px lines at 15% luminance for 150 ms. The cavity then ignites in 8 left-to-right sectors of 40 ms as an all-segments self-test that settles to ghost. Total time is 1 s or less, and gear and speed are live from frame 1. Gear change: a 1-frame 60% rim flash laps the octagon's 8 facets in 50 ms. Limiter: only the 38 px strip strobes at 10 Hz in shift, the octagon rim holds steady in accent, and the gear may pulse at 2 Hz after 0.5 s. Segments light in the same frame and release with a 50 ms phosphor decay, so the display never trails telemetry.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm against per-car, per-gear thresholds (EngineMaxRpm fallback) drives the strip and the 24 chevrons. Gear drives the octagon Seg7 (N in ink at 72%, R in warning). Speed drives the Seg7 speed with 0.6 hysteresis. Accel, Brake and Boost drive the THR (go), BRK (warning) and BST (secondary) columns; this fixes the v2 hero's pink brake column. Felt G (-AccelerationX/Z) drives the G-scope dot with a 500 ms trail, the light azimuth for chamfers and crescent, and 0/1/3/4 px parallax. Rear TireSlipAngle drives the SLIP lamp and the DRIFT n deg readout. TireSlipRatio together with Brake or Accel drives derived LOCK and SPIN lamps; these are inferred, because the packet has no ABS or TC flags, and are labelled as such. IsRaceOn drives the boot and the 1-frame hide.
- **References:** Noritake Itron and Futaba multi-segment VFD modules (filament, grid-mesh and phosphor stack); the 1976 Aston Martin Lagonda and 1980s Japanese digital dashboards; Cyberpunk 2077's squared 1980s car numerals (HUDS+GUIS); Oblivion GFX (GMUNK) line and greeble discipline
- **Phase 6 assets:** Chakra Petch MSDF atlas with a recorded tnum check. A 6 px hex-mesh tile with mips (procedural SDF fallback). A chamfer45 bezel normal/roughness map that supersedes assets/cyber_vfd_bezel.png (procedural chamfer fallback). A 64x64 blue-noise tile to dither the glass crescent. Seg7 digits are procedural.
- **Risk:** The mesh, filaments and scanlines could light off pixels or moire on the WRGB subpixels. All three only multiply lit phosphor, are fixed in screen space, and the mesh is mip-mapped, so the cavity stays #000000. Bloom for 24 chevrons plus 15 pods uses one emissive buffer clipped to 1440x560 with the 8-level chain from 256 px (about 0.12 ms). Everything else is SDF in one pass, about 0.3 ms total.

#### SkylineR34Mfd — build #12 · cockpit
Category **Retro** · target `inspired` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 50 · new.

**A twist on the Skyline GT-R R34 and its Multi Function Display** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "for retro i want some nice twists on some nismo / skyline variants some retro and some modern"; in the game as the 2000 Nissan Skyline GT-R V-Spec II). Checked against sources on 2026-11-28 (Jalopnik, evo, Toprank's MFD guide, Japanese Nostalgic Car and paultan.org): the MFD, a 5.8-inch full-colour LCD on top of the centre of the dashboard, opens on a GT-R logo screen and then shows boost, throttle opening, injector duty, oil, water and cabin temperatures and the torque sent to the front axle as numbers, bar graphs and a line graph of the last 30 s of one value; the driver sets a red zone for boost, injector duty and oil and water temperature (and exhaust temperature on the V-spec), and past it the bar turns red; the V-spec adds intake and exhaust temperatures (sources disagree on the exact count of live values); the extremes of a drive are stored; the real shift light is one red LED in the MFD housing that flashes at an rpm the driver sets; a final Nismo version added lap times and G. The stock analogue cluster is not established: the tachometer's scale and red zone and the gauges beside it are to confirm from a cluster photo in step 1 (the engine's redline is 8,000 rpm; Nismo's Heritage Parts reproduction meter reads 11,000 rpm and 320 km/h with orange night lighting). The real car has no gear readout. The twist: the MFD leaves the dashboard top and its pages float around the tachometer as the dash's own tiles.

Starting layout, detached parts. Rank 3: a Ø440 tachometer at (0, −20) as a rendered dial of the period, white numerals on a dark face and a red needle, scaled from `EngineMaxRpm`. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 170) in a period liquid-crystal window at the dial's foot, and above the dial a straight row of 12 small lamps (740 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 96) in its own window to the left of the dial, 400 px from the gear. Rank 4: two MFD tiles, each 330 × 240, at the outer left and right: the left holds three bar graphs with their numbers, the right holds one value as a line graph over the last 30 s, as on the real MFD. Rank 5: the tile captions in the MFD's own style and the GT-R badge as the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The driver's red zones. The real MFD lets the driver set a red zone for boost, injector duty and temperatures; here every bar has one, set in the OSD. When a value passes its mark the end of the bar turns `warning`, a small ▲ appears beside its number and the tile's caption inverts, all steady; when it drops back the bar leaves a peak tick behind. The line graph draws the same crossing as a shaded band, so a pull through the gears leaves a boost history the driver can read afterwards. At the shift rpm the lamp row is full; on the limiter only the lamp row strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → tachometer needle (`needle` spring with feed-forward), lamp row and limiter; gear → the window; speed → tabular speed; boost, throttle and power → the three bars; any one of them, or longitudinal G → the line graph, in a fixed ring buffer of 600 samples (30 s at 20 Hz); brake → an optional fourth bar. The real MFD's injector duty, oil, water, cabin, intake and exhaust temperatures and the oil pressure of a Nismo page are not in the telemetry, and neither is the torque that goes to the front axle: none of them is drawn, and the handover lists them as deliberate changes.
- **References:** Jalopnik on the R34 GT-R's MFD (https://jalopnik.com/the-r34-gt-rs-multifunction-display-was-secretly-its-co-1845687935); evo, "Art of speed" (https://cdn2.evo.co.uk/nissan/gt-r/203810/nissan-r34-skyline-gt-r-multifunction-display-art-of-speed); Toprank's MFD guide (https://toprankglobal.jp/news/92/How%20to%20use%20the%20R34%20Nissan%20Skyline%20GT-R%20MFD%20:%20Part%201); Nismo's reproduction combination meters (https://japanesenostalgiccar.com/nismo-reproduction-silvia-s15-and-skyline-r34-gauge-clusters , https://paultan.org/2022/08/24/gismo-heritage-parts-r34-skyline-gtr/); engine figures (https://www.jbskyline.net/r34/gtr/index2.php); still needed in step 1: the BNR34 owner's manual pages for the MFD and the meters, and a clear photo of the stock cluster; `RetroCyber` in this catalog for period display materials
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the tachometer (ring, face, needle and hub cap) and the two tile housings as period plastics with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: the grain of a late-1990s liquid-crystal panel with its backlight bleed, and the dial-face print. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800, Barlow Semi Condensed 500 and a period-style label face from the OFL pack. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** The MFD's real look cannot be rebuilt from memory: no tile is designed before its real page is in `RESEARCH.md`. A low-resolution period screen drawn at 4K can look like a bug: keep the pixel grid as a visible, deliberate texture and the numbers crisp above it. The real car has no gear readout: the gear window is this dash's addition, must reach cap 150 or more and win the glance, and the handover lists it, as it lists the 12-lamp row in place of the real single red shift LED. Red zones use `warning`, so the dial's own red needle and band stay within the red budget. Perf: one dial plate, two tiles and a 600-point line in chunks of 8, est. 0.25 ms.

#### GlyphDotMatrix — build #21 · cockpit
Category **Retro** · target `concept` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 6 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Retro through the LED dot-matrix displays and scoreboards of the 1980s. The dot optics of this brief stay; the phone in its references is a benchmark for the dots, not the subject.

'Micro-LED field': every element is made of round SDF dots (2-stop core 40% toward white, 1 px rim at 35%, a sheen moved by the virtual light). Unlit dots exist only inside glyph cells and bar tracks at inkGhost, never as a full-field grid. Rank 1: 11 outside-in super-dots (30 px), and the gear as an original procedural 5×7 dot glyph on a 30 px pitch (cap 210) at X 1920. Rank 2: speed in three 5×7 cells on a 16 px pitch (cap 112), 280 px right, with ghost 8s as dim dots. Rank 3: a 64×3 dot bar (18 px pitch, 1152 px). Rank 4: a live dyno dot plot under gear and speed at ≤30% luminance. Titillium Web micro-labels.

- **Signature moment:** Self-drawing dyno: at full throttle (≥0.95), each of 64 rpm bins stores the maximum power seen (filled dots) and torque (hollow ring dots), so the car's real wide-open-throttle curve draws itself during the first pulls. A 2×2 hot operating-point cluster rides the curve, and the learned peak-power column gets a 3-dot crown. Once rpm passes it, the tach dots beyond dim to 60% as a live shift hint. Gear changes re-raster the glyph top to bottom in 50 ms (row dots pop 0.6→1.0; the new value is present from frame 1). At the limiter only the super-dot row strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm/idle/max + per-car shift → dot bar, super-dots, operating point; power (W) and torque (N·m) at throttle ≥0.95 → dyno bins (fixed [f32; 64] ×2, reset when the car ordinal changes); gear → dot glyph; speed → dot cells; boost → optional shy column; car ordinal / class / PI → dyno reset and a shy class badge; lateral G → dot sheen azimuth and 0/2/4 px parallax.
- **References:** Nothing Phone (3) dot-matrix Glyph Matrix and Ndot typography; stadium micro-LED scoreboards; chassis-dyno power/torque sheets; Oblivion dot-lattice layout (GMUNK)
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: SDF dots with original 5×7 glyph bitmaps stored as Rust consts (no font licence); shared MSDF Titillium Web 600 for micro-labels.
- **Risk:** Design: v1 failed on rank inversion (rings and a canvas-wide grid dominated, the gear was tiny), and dense dot fields can moiré on the WRGB subpixels. Fix: gear dots ≥24 px and the only bloom; dyno ≤30% luminance with no glow; even-pixel pitches with fwidth AA; no grid outside cells. Perf: about 1,500 instanced dot quads in one draw ≈0.05 ms; dyno storage is fixed arrays, with no per-frame allocation.

#### GtrNismoMfd — build #30 · cockpit
Category **Retro** · target `inspired` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 58 · new.

**A twist on the Nissan GT-R Nismo (R35) and its Multi Function Display** (TJX, 2026-11-28: Skyline and Nismo twists, "some retro and some modern"; in the game as the 2020 and 2024 Nissan GT-R Nismo). Checked against sources on 2026-11-28 (Nissan's releases of October 2007 and November 2013, the 2017 and 2024 US press kits, the 2015 Multi Function Display manual and the 2009 owner's manual): an analogue cluster of speedometer, a large centre tachometer with the gear display at its upper right, and fuel meter, all at one height with the multi-function meter; a dial plate whose design evokes engaged gears inside a three-dimensional metal-like ring; upshift indicator lamps in green, yellow and red; on the Nismo a red tachometer design, a carbon-like meter finish and an Alcantara meter hood; and the multi-function meter on the centre-stack screen (7-inch to 2016, an 8-inch touch screen from the 2017 model), designed with Polyphony Digital, the Gran Turismo studio, where the driver fills a Custom view of three or six panels with gauges from the car's list and can record and play back driving data. The redline is about 7,000 to 7,100 rpm (Wikipedia, from the earlier check). The tachometer's scale end and the way each gauge is drawn are to confirm in step 1 from the manual's gauge chapter and cabin photos. The twist: the driver's own page is the dash. The tachometer stays in the middle and six slots around it, three on each side as on the real Custom view, take whichever gauges the driver assigns.

Starting layout, detached parts. Rank 3: a Ø460 tachometer at (0, −20) as a rendered dial with a machined, chronograph-style ring, scaled from `EngineMaxRpm`. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 170) in a window at the dial's upper right, as on the car, with a straight row of 12 lamps above the dial (740 × 28), filling outside-in (the car's own upshift lamps are green, yellow and red). Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 96) inside the dial's lower half. Rank 4: six gauge slots, each 240 × 150, three stacked on each side of the dial; by default boost, the G plot and longitudinal G on the left, throttle, brake and steering on the right. Each slot draws its value as a small round virtual dial, a horizontal bar, a cross-hair plot or a scrolling time graph (an interpretation until the manual's gauge chapter shows the real styles). Rank 5: the page name and the Nismo logo.

- **Signature moment:** The custom page. The six slots are the driver's: in the OSD each takes any gauge from the list, and the dash keeps the page. On screen the slots share one light and one grid, so a page always looks designed. At the shift rpm the tachometer's ring takes a steady `shift` colour and the gear window's frame lights; on the limiter only the lamp row strobes. On an upshift the gear digit changes at once, with a 1.12 → 1.0 entry and no roll (the real car's transition is not confirmed).
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → tachometer needle, ring, lamp row and limiter; gear → the window; speed → tabular speed. The gauge list holds only what the packet carries, and much of it is on the real list: boost, accelerator and brake position, steering position, longitudinal and lateral G (the real list's G-force reading; the cross-hair plot is this dash's drawing), speed, and a stopwatch from the timestamp (the real one runs from the MRK and START/STOP switches on the wheel). Power and acceleration timers are this dash's additions. The slot choices are `config.extra` keys named `GtrNismoMfdSlot1` to `GtrNismoMfdSlot6`, parsed when the config changes. Water, engine oil and transmission oil temperatures and pressures, the torque split, fuel level, flow and economy and the tyre pressures are on the real list and not in the telemetry: they are not offered.
- **References:** Nissan's GT-R launch release of 24 October 2007 (https://global.nissannews.com/en/releases/071024-01-e); the GT-R Nismo release of 19 November 2013 (https://global.nissannews.com/en/releases/131119-02-e); the 2017 and 2024 US press kits (https://usa.nissannews.com/en-US/releases/us-2017-nissan-gt-r-press-kit , https://usa.nissannews.com/en-US/releases/2024-nissan-gt-r-press-kit); the 2015 Multi Function Display manual (https://owners.nissanusa.com/content/techpub/ManualsAndGuides/GTR/2015/2015-GTR-MultiFD.pdf); the 2009 owner's manual, upshift indicator (https://www.manualsdir.com/manuals/192484/nissan-r35-d-2009-gt-r.html?page=85); Gizmodo on the Polyphony Digital display (https://gizmodo.com/nissan-gt-r-multifunction-display-looks-like-a-playstat-313871); still needed in step 1: the manual's gauge chapter and cabin photos for the drawing styles and the tachometer's scale end; `Gt7ModularTripod` in this catalog for the paged-panel idea
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the tachometer with its machined ring and the gear window, and one shared slot frame, with three light azimuths. ComfyUI: a carbon-look meter surround as on the Nismo, and the fine pattern of the display face. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800, Barlow Semi Condensed 500 and Titillium Web 600. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** A gear at the upper right of a dial is off the centreline, against the rule that the hero gear leads on X = 1920: either the window is large and bright enough to win the glance where the car has it, or the gear moves to the dial centre and the handover says why. Six free slots can produce an ugly page: every gauge is drawn on the same 240 × 150 grid and none blooms. The real graphics must come from the manuals and frames, not from memory. Perf: one dial plate and six small gauges, est. 0.22 ms.

#### SynthwaveOutrun — build #39 · cockpit+hood
Category **Retro** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 20 · rebuild.

A night-drive horizon in letterbox: 1700x400 on the hood, 1400x520 in the cockpit, split by a 1 px horizon line at y -40. Rank 3: a sliced half-sun (radius 170) behind the gear, its 14 horizontal slats lighting bottom-up with rpm, gaps narrowing toward the crown, accent at the base to secondary at the top, at 70% alpha. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear (cap 210, 10 deg MSDF shear) in ink over a feathered dimming pad, under a 36 px 16-pod crown strip. Rank 2: chrome-banded italic speed (cap 104) to the right, with a hard horizon cut inside each glyph aligned to the scene horizon. Rank 4: a perspective hairline grid below, at 20% luminance or less, unglowing. Rank 5: a wireframe ridge on the horizon.

- **Signature moment:** Sunrise to the redline. Each slat is an rpm stage that lights in the same frame, so the sun rises with the revs and closes into a full disc at the shift rpm while the crown strip strobes at 10 Hz. The steady limiter cue is the horizon line heating to accent; it never flashes. Upshift: as the sun sinks with the rpm drop, a 1-frame bright pulse runs outward along the horizon from X=1920 at 4000 px/s. Boot: the grid draws in from the horizon in 4 sectors, then the sun rises to live rpm.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm with per-car thresholds drives the sun slats and the strip. Gear drives the hero glyph and Speed the chrome speed. DistanceTraveled sets the grid scroll phase, so a parked car's grid is still. AngularVelocityY / max(v, 3 m/s) sets the grid curvature kappa; this is a synthesised road plane, not map data. PositionY over the 120-point trail draws the horizon ridge as the car's own recent elevation profile, labelled as such. Felt lateral G sets the light azimuth that slides the chrome bands' highlight. Brake fades the grid's near edge toward caution as a steady ramp.
- **References:** Generic 1980s synthwave/outrun poster and album art (sliced sun, chrome type, wire grid); Tron (1982) and Tron: Legacy line work (GMUNK, Digital Domain); 1980s arcade racing cabinets' horizon framing; Oblivion GFX restraint
- **Phase 6 assets:** Saira Condensed 800 MSDF atlas (tnum check). The chrome band ramp as a 256x1 LUT baked at load. An optional 4K chrome-glyph bevel normal map (procedural bevel fallback). The sun, grid and ridge are procedural.
- **Risk:** The large lit sun threatens the colour budget and burn-in. At idle only one slat is lit (20% luminance or less), slats sit at 70% alpha with 4 px gaps, warning red is never a slat colour, and the sun never flashes. The chrome cut must not cost legibility: speed keeps a minimum band L so it passes the 4.5:1 blur gate, and the gear stays solid ink on a pad. The grid is drawn as analytic SDF lines in one pass clipped to the lower 160 px band (about 0.05 ms). Total is about 0.2 ms.

#### HakosukaS20 — build #48 · cockpit
Category **Retro** · target `inspired` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 64 · new.

**A twist on the first Skyline GT-R, the "Hakosuka"** (TJX, 2026-11-28: Skyline twists, "some retro and some modern"; in the game as the 1971 Nissan Skyline 2000 GT-R). Checked against sources on 2026-11-28 (Nissan's Heritage Collection pages for the PGC10 and KPGC10, Supercar Nostalgia, and the Gooding and Sports Car Market lots): the KPGC10 hardtop (October 1970 to September 1972, 1,197 built, all right-hand drive) has one six-gauge binnacle, two large round dials for road and engine speed with four small gauges (water temperature, oil pressure, fuel and ammeter) stacked two high on either side of the pair; the 1969 PGC10 sedan put water and oil between the large dials and the ammeter and fuel on the console. The PGC10 GT-R's tachometer reads to 10,000 rpm in place of the 2000GT's 8,000 rpm unit (to confirm on the KPGC10 dial in step 1). The S20 is a 1,989 cc four-valve DOHC straight-six on three Mikuni-Solex twin-choke carburettors, 160 PS (118 kW) at 7,000 rpm and 176 Nm at 5,600. The cabin is spartan, without amenities (on the PGC10 the audio could be deleted to save weight), in black vinyl with wooden inserts in the dash and centre console that match the gear knob, and a three-spoke wheel with fluted alloy spokes. Not established, and to read from a close photograph of an original dial in step 1: where the red zone starts, the speedometer's range and odometer, the colours of face, numerals and needles, and the depth of the tubes. The twist: the dials stay mechanical, and two period racing devices do the modern jobs. A tell-tale needle records each pull, and number drums show the gear and the speed.

Starting layout, detached parts on true black. Rank 3: the tachometer, Ø420 at (+290, −20) in a deep tube with a thin bright ring. Rank 1: the gear on a single white-on-black number drum (Barlow Semi Condensed 700, cap 170) in a window at (0, −40) between the two dials, under a row of 10 small jewelled lamps on a straight line (740 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 2: the speed on three number drums (cap 96) in the face of the left dial, Ø420 at (−290, −20), which keeps a speedometer needle around them. Rank 4: four Ø150 dials stacked two high, a pair outboard of each large dial as in the KPGC10's binnacle, for boost, throttle, longitudinal G and lateral G. Rank 5: engraved labels in a period face and the badge.

- **Signature moment:** The tell-tale. Racing tachometers of the period carried a second, thin needle that the main needle pushed up and left behind, to show the highest revs reached. Here the tell-tale rides up with every pull and stays at the peak when the driver shifts; it drops back on the `light` spring when the next pull passes 3,000 rpm. A glance after a corner shows exactly where the last shift happened. The number drums turn like the real thing, one clean roll per change on the `snap` spring, and are never left between two numbers. On the limiter only the lamp row strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → tachometer needle (`needle` spring with feed-forward), the tell-tale's peak hold, lamp row and limiter; gear → the gear drum; speed → the three speed drums, held with hysteresis so the last drum does not flutter, and the speedometer needle; boost, throttle, longitudinal G and lateral G → the four small dials. Fuel, water temperature, oil pressure and the ammeter have no verified telemetry, so their four dials show these values instead, and the handover lists that as a deliberate change.
- **References:** Nissan Heritage Collection, Skyline 2000GT-R PGC10 (https://www.nissan-global.com/EN/HERITAGE_COLLECTION/skyline_2000gt_r.html) and Skyline Hardtop 2000GT-R KPGC10 (https://www.nissan-global.com/EN/HERITAGE_COLLECTION/skyline_hardtop_2000gt-r.html); Supercar Nostalgia on the PGC10 (https://supercarnostalgia.com/blog/nissan-pgc-10-skyline-2000-gt-r) and KPGC10 (https://supercarnostalgia.com/blog/nissan-kpgc-10-skyline-2000-gt-r); Gooding lot KPGC10-000641 (https://www.goodingco.com/lot/1971-nissan-skyline-h-t-2000gt-r-hakosuka); Sports Car Market, KPGC10000667 (https://www.sportscarmarket.com/profile/1971-nissan-skyline-2000gt-r); still needed in step 1: a close photograph of an original dial for the red zone, the speedometer, the odometer and the colours; chronometric racing tachometers with a tell-tale needle; mechanical drum counters; `PorscheAnalog` in this catalog for rendered dials and tubes
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: both dial tubes with their rings, faces, needles and hub caps, the number drums as real cylinders with printed numerals (glTF for the kit's mesh pass, with a flipbook fallback), and the jewelled lamp caps. ComfyUI: the black panel with its wooden insert, the dial-face print with its period numerals, and the wheel's fluted alloy spokes and the wooden dash insert for the environment the rings reflect. MSDF atlases for Barlow Semi Condensed 500 and 700.
- **Risk:** Two equal dials and a small centre window can lose the gear: the drum window is the brightest part of the dash and the dial faces stay under 20% luminance. A rolling drum is slower to read than a digit that snaps: the roll takes at most 120 ms and the new number is fully in place before the eye arrives. `PorscheAnalog` is also a row of tubes, so this dash must read as a 1971 Japanese panel: black with the wooden insert, bright thin rings, plain numerals, no carbon and no chrome flourish. Perf: two dial plates, four small dial plates, four drum meshes of about 400 triangles each, est. 0.25 ms.

#### ZNismoSport — build #57 · cockpit
Category **Retro** · target `inspired` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 68 · new.

**A twist on the Nissan Z Nismo, old pods over a new screen** (TJX, 2026-11-28: Skyline and Nismo twists, "some retro and some modern"; in the game as the 2024 Nissan Z Nismo). Found in Nissan's press kits by the check of 2026-11-28 (read them again in step 1): a 12.3-inch screen with Normal, Enhanced and Sport modes, the redline shift point at 12 o'clock, and a Sport mode that puts the tachometer in the centre with a shift-up light at the top; a red outline round the Nismo's tachometer; and three analogue pod gauges on top of the dash for boost, turbo speed and voltage, which quote the three pods of the 1969 240Z. The twist: the two eras are built as what they are. The screen is a screen, and the three pods are rendered as physical 1969 hardware standing above it.

Starting layout, detached parts. Rank 3: the Sport-mode tachometer, a Ø440 drawn dial at (0, +10) turned so that the red zone ends at 12 o'clock, with a red outline, scaled from `EngineMaxRpm`. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 180) in the dial centre and a shift-up bar of 10 cells on a straight line at the top of the screen part (740 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira Condensed 600, cap 96) to the left of the dial, 380 px from the gear. Rank 4: three Ø170 pods in deep hooded tubes on a line above the dial's shoulders at y −230, turned toward the driver, for boost, power and G, with real needles. Rank 5: the Nismo wordmark with its red final letter as the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** Redline at noon. In Sport mode the needle climbs clockwise to the top, so the moment to shift is always straight up, where the eye already is. At the shift rpm the shift-up bar is full and the dial's red outline brightens to a steady `shift`; on the limiter only the bar strobes. The three pods answer in their own time, as mechanical needles do: the boost needle swings up on the `needle` spring and overshoots a little on a hard pull, while the screen below it is instant. Two eras, two motions, one dash.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max → tachometer needle, shift-up bar and limiter; gear → centre numeral; speed → tabular speed; boost → the first pod; power → the second; acceleration (the larger of lateral and longitudinal, signed) → the third. Turbo speed and voltage, which the real pods show, are not in the telemetry: the two pods are re-labelled for power and G, and the handover lists that as a deliberate change. The display mode is fixed to Sport; Normal and Enhanced are not built.
- **References:** Nissan Z and Z Nismo (RZ34) press kits and owner videos of the three display modes; the 240Z's three dash-top gauges; the Nismo logotype in `assets/logos/`; `HakosukaS20` in this catalog, which shares the period-pod method
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the three pods as real hardware (hooded tubes, faces, needles, hub caps and glass) with three light azimuths, and the screen's thin frame. ComfyUI: the pods' crackle-black finish and face print in the 240Z's style, and a stitched dash-top surface for them to stand on. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 600 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500. Procedural fallback for every part.
- **Risk:** Three pods above the gear can crowd the shift-up bar: the bar sits on the screen part under the pods' line, and the pods stay at rank 4 luminance with no glow. A turned scale is unfamiliar: number it clearly and keep idle at the lower left. A deliberate lag on the pod needles must stay small (the kit's `needle` spring, nothing slower), so no reading is ever late enough to mislead. Red is the Nismo colour and also the warning colour: the outline and the wordmark count against the red budget, and nothing red flashes. Perf: one dial, three pod plates and needles, est. 0.2 ms.

### Liquid Glass

#### ★ LiquidGlass — build #1 · cockpit+hood
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 1 · rebuild.
