Design board (recorded before the re-cut): Penpot `07 · LiquidGlass` (`b568ed3f-64cd-806f-8008-fd9deaa27e48`), boards `791061e3-930c-8090-8009-` + cockpit 01–04 `02957d24347b`, `02960076ab73`, `029629233a70`, `02965b083da7`, hood 01–04 `02968ca40fbd`, `0296a85d0c9f`, `0296ae2e1f90`, `0296b582d923` · `mockups/LiquidGlass/index.html`.

Rebuilt on 2026-11-27 on the kit's optical glass; TJX rejected the first pilot's single capsule ("a dark slab with blobs"). Separate drops of clear synthetic sapphire (`fh6_kit::OpticalGlass`, n_d 1.768) float over OLED black, one per instrument; every light is drawn under the glass and bent by it, and there is no backing panel. Rank 1: 16 glass cabochons over the rev rod (the shift lights), and the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 156) under a dome lens that magnifies it about 1.15 times. Rank 2: the speed (Saira 500, cap 100, tabular) in a capsule left of the gear lens. Rank 3: a graduated rev rod with a ray-traced liquid-light caustic, ending in a meniscus bead. Rank 4: a small G lens that focuses a spot of light. Power and boost sit in the right capsule; the rpm readout (under the left capsule) and the car's logo or the custom name (under the right) sit on one line, drawn after the glass so no rim bends them. The zone table is at the top of the module's `mod.rs`.

- **Signature moments:** from the shift point only the cabochons strobe (10 Hz, starting lit; a breath in reduced-flash mode), and on the limiter they fuse into one bar of glass and the bead turns white with a warning halo. On an upshift a ripple runs out through the gear lens's rim while the numeral stays still. Under hard braking the side capsules reach for the gear lens and fuse with it on the slosh spring. The studio light swings up to 25° with lateral G, so every rim's highlight travels together. Showing grows the glass's thickness over 200 ms; hiding takes 1 frame.
- **Telemetry:** rpm (current, idle, max) with the shift point (the tokens' 97%, or `ShiftPointPct` set from the OSD) → the rod, the bead and the 16 cabochons. Gear → the hero numeral and the upshift ripple. Speed → 3 tabular cells (0.6 hysteresis). Acceleration → the studio's swing and the G spot (felt force, gBall spring). Body slip from velocity → the spot defocuses from 3° to a full ring by 18° (grip is focus). Braking → the fuse. Power and boost → the right capsule; rpm, power and boost hold their digits by hysteresis on the limiter. IsRaceOn → materialise. All are native fields; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** Apple Liquid Glass, WWDC25 sessions 219, 323 and 356 (lensing, the travelling highlight, materialise); Apple CarPlay Ultra; Lucid Gravity Clearview's floating OLED; *Light: Science & Magic* chapter 7 on lighting glass (dark-field). Sources in the module's `RESEARCH.md`.
- **Assets:** the Blender studio environment (`studio_env.png`, with a ComfyUI panorama on its cyclorama) and a ray-traced liquid-light caustic (`liquid_light.png`), both 4096² (`assets/clusters/liquid_glass/ASSETS.md`). Fallbacks: the kit's analytic studio and a plain ribbon. MSDF atlases: Saira, Saira Condensed, Titillium Web.
- **Performance and lessons:** 0.32 to 0.46 ms GPU per state at 4K (0.33 to 0.49 ms since the host CPU phase's compute bloom), about 0.01 ms of CPU for its own update and draw, and +0.06 ms per frame over the scaffold on the live bench (the handover's "Verification output" and "Live bench"); the module's `HANDOVER.md` and `docs/kit/README.md` §13 carry the lessons for sibling dashes. Hood: the scrim is 0%, so the black cores keep the numerals readable over a bright bonnet, and the G lens moves beside the gear.

#### VernierLoupe — build #17 · cockpit+hood
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 31 · new.

Frameless and horizontal. A 1200 px vernier rail runs across the crown with ticks every 100 rpm (2 px minor, 5 px major) and Barlow Semi Condensed 500 numerals at cap 20, all ≤20% luminance, plus a 4 px primary fill from the left. The precise reading is a 170×110 glass loupe that glides to the current rpm and magnifies the scale ×2.4 by self-lensing, with the lensed ticks lit in primary. Rank 1: 13 round cabochon pods in a 38 px strip above the rail. Below: the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 200) in a fixed 230 px lens, and the speed on the left in Saira 200 cap 112, ghost 8s. Rank 4: a 120 px G-lens roving over a 24 px dot lattice under the gear. Optional Michroma logo zone at the rail's left end.

- **Signature moment:** Lens whip: as rpm falls on an upshift, the loupe slides back on the ribbon spring (feed-forward, no lag). The ticks inside it smear radially with a 3-tap blur scaled by the loupe's velocity, then snap sharp when it stops, and a glint runs once round its rim. In the last 300 rpm before the shift point, magnification swells from ×2.4 to ×3.2, so the final ticks visibly grow. At the limiter only the cabochon strip strobes at 10 Hz; the loupe rim holds a steady ring in the shift colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, max rpm and the car profile (shift rpm, redline, peak-torque → peak-power operating band) → rail fill, loupe position and magnification, the 13 pods, and a faint operating-band tint on the rail. Gear and speed → the fixed lens and the tabular field. Acceleration x/z → the G-lens position over the dot lattice (felt force); the dots under it are magnified, so load reads as dot size. Tyre combined slip → G-lens rim steps from neutral to caution to warning. Lateral G → light azimuth on all three lens rims. Native fields only; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** Watchmaker's loupe and vernier caliper scales; the iOS text-selection magnifier as precedent for a lens that is the readout; Porsche 550 Spyder tachometer operating band; Oblivion's dot lattice (GMUNK).
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: SDF rail and ticks, a closed-form magnifier UV warp and the dot lattice. Optional: the shared sapphire dome normal map for the lenses, and a mono-white brand logo for the logo zone. MSDF atlases: Saira, Saira Condensed, Barlow Semi Condensed, Titillium Web, Michroma.
- **Risk:** Design risk: a sliding magnifier is slower to read in peripheral vision than a filled bar, and lensing a hairline scale can shimmer. To avoid it: the 4 px fill ribbon carries the coarse reading and the loupe adds precision. The scale and lattice render once (again on a scale change) into clipped, mip-mapped targets, so the warp samples filtered texels. Performance: three one-tap lens warps plus bloom on ranks 1–3 only. Estimate ≈0.18 ms. Hood: the rail widens to 1500 px with a legibility halo of alpha ≤0.55.

#### ShibuyaChromaticPrisma — build #26 · cockpit
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 26 · rebuild.

**Category note.** In the Liquid Glass category the prism is clear `OpticalGlass`, and the colour split is the prism deviation of `docs/specs/optics.md` section 10, with body slip turning the prism (yaw is not verified). Tint comes from absorption only. The neon of this brief lives in the beam and in the wet-road mirror, never as tubes on the glass. Its family stays Sci-Fi.

A rain-night optics bench. A 280 px equilateral glass prism, point-up at X=1920 (tint alpha 44, 1 px rim, fresnel band, neon-tube edges), holds the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 180) on a dimming pad. A white beam enters from the left, with the speed (Saira Condensed 700, cap 104) above it. Rank 3: from the right face a three-band fan in primary, secondary and accent exits; its length and opening grow with rpm against a 1 px rpm ruler. Rank 1: a 36 px 15-pod strip across the crown. Rank 4: under the prism, four short unglowing tyre beams that split with each tyre's slip. Rank 5: a wet-road mirror appears only when puddles are present.

- **Signature moment:** Dispersion is slip. With grip, the three bands are drawn coincident as one ink-white ray. As the rear slip angle grows they split by up to 18 px per band, so a drift literally breaks the light into colour. Aberration is driven by telemetry and is never an always-on effect. At the shift rpm the fan reaches the end of the ruler and takes the shift colour, steady, while only the strip strobes at 10 Hz. When WheelInPuddleDepth > 0, the mirror fades in over 200 ms, rippled by SurfaceRumble.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm drives the fan length and opening and the strip. Gear drives the prism glyph and Speed the speed. Accel sets the incoming beam's brightness. Rear TireSlipAngle and AngularVelocityY set the band separation (yaw x 6 + beta x 18 px), which is 0 with grip. TireCombinedSlip x4 splits the four mini beams. WheelInPuddleDepth x4 drives the mirror opacity and SurfaceRumble x4 its ripple amplitude. Felt lateral G sets the light azimuth for the prism rim and fresnel band.
- **References:** The white-light prism dispersion experiment; night photography of Shibuya and Shinjuku neon on wet asphalt; Blade Runner 2049's 'degradation as register' (Territory Studio); Cyberpunk 2077's glitch-as-event rule
- **Phase 6 assets:** An optional 2K wet-asphalt normal map for the mirror ripple (procedural sine-ripple fallback). A prism thickness/normal map (analytic fallback). Saira Condensed MSDF atlas. Neon tubes and bands are procedural.
- **Risk:** Chromatic splitting could smear the digits, and a multicolour fan could break the colour budget. Splitting applies only to the fan and the mini beams, never to digits, and snaps back to 0 within a frame at grip. Bands sit at 70% alpha, white at 15% of lit area or less, and warning red is never used. Cost: prism self-lensing over a 300 px region, 3 bands, and a mirror built as a flipped, blurred re-draw of the emissive buffer clipped to 1400x120, about 0.25 ms; the mirror is off when dry.

#### SapphireMystery — build #35 · cockpit
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Classic Analog · catalog row 37 · new.

**Category note.** In the Liquid Glass category (moved from Apple Vision on 2026-11-28 to make room for `CarplayIsland`) the invisible sapphire disc and the domed cabochon are the instrument: both are the kit's clear optical glass, and the cabochon's lensing follows its row in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths). Hands, lume and indices stay Classic Analog. This brief's own warning stands: no frost and no light running round the rims, so it is never mistaken for `LiquidGlass`.

A mystery dial made for a display that is itself transparent: no face, no ring and no visible pivot. Lume indices hang over the live cockpit on three parallax layers. The rpm hand floats, its root lost in an invisible Ø520 sapphire disc (alpha 28) that shows only as a 1 px fresnel crescent where the G-driven light grazes it. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 700 gear, cap 190, in a domed sapphire cabochon (Ø230, alpha 44) that lenses the indices behind it; 13 floating lume dots form the shift strip, 24 × 740 px. Rank 2: tabular Saira 300 speed, cap 100, floating under the cabochon on a dimming pad. Rank 3: a primary-colour lance with a lume core and a counterweight tail; numerals appear only at the quarter marks and at the shift rpm, which sits at 12. Rank 4: a ringless G-dot under the speed. Rank 5: one Michroma name, cap 12.

- **Signature moment:** Lume wake. The floating hand charges every index it passes. Each one glows lume green and decays with τ = 400 ms, so a pull leaves a phosphor wake up the dial and a lift-off leaves one coming down. At constant rpm the wake dies and only the two indices bracketing the tip stay lit, so idle is still. At the shift rpm the 12 o'clock index charges in the shift colour instead. At the limiter only the floating dot strip strobes. The invisible disc's 1 px rim then lights steady in the shift colour, the only moment the whole crystal can be seen.
- **Telemetry:** rpm drives the floating hand (needle spring with feed-forward), the per-index charge (a fixed [f32; 48] array with exponential decay from FrameInput dt, no allocation) and the dot thresholds. gear and speed drive the cabochon glyph and the floating digits. Lateral G drives the light azimuth (crescent position, fresnel arc) and the 0/2/4 px parallax of the index layers. G lat/long drive the G-dot. OemDashOpaquePct sets the dimming pad behind gear and speed (alpha ≤140 × (1 − scrim)). All of these fields are already decoded.
- **References:** Mystery clocks (hands carried on transparent rotating crystal discs); AR-coated domed sapphire crystals; Lucid Gravity's floating bezel-free OLED; Super-LumiNova phosphorescence
- **Phase 6 assets:** A sapphire-dome normal/refraction pair and a soft caustic crescent texture, with an analytic dome and crescent as the procedural fallback; a 64×64 blue-noise dither tile for the alpha-28 disc; MSDF atlases for Saira 300, Saira Condensed 700, Barlow Semi Condensed and Michroma.
- **Risk:** With no face or ring, the dial can lose its frame of reference over a busy scene at 0% scrim. Keep the quarter numerals and the dimming pad, cap the wake at 3 lit indices behind the hand, and require the blur gate on a C1 capture (gear ≥7:1, speed ≥4.5:1). It must also stay clearly apart from Liquid Glass: no frost, no light running round the rims, and glass used only as the invisible carrier. Performance: one disc with a 40 px lensing band, one cabochon and 48 instanced indices, est. 0.15–0.2 ms, with dither against banding.

#### DichroicLouvre — build #44 · cockpit
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 32 · new.

A kinetic arch of 28 vertical glass fins (18×96 px) on a 200° arc (semi-axes 560×200) around the gear. Each fin turns on its own axis. Edge-on it is a single 1 px rim line, so OLED pixels stay off; face-on it is a lit dichroic pane in primary, shading to accent only in a grazing fresnel sheen. Rank 3: fins turn face-on in order as rpm passes each fin's threshold. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 200) on a dimming pad at the arch's centre, plus a 760×40 strip of 15 pods above the apex. Rank 2: speed left of the gear, Saira 300 cap 104, ghost 8s. Rank 4: four tyre fins in a car footprint under the gear and speed. Titillium Web labels.

- **Signature moment:** Shift-point snap: when the last fin turns face-on at the shift rpm, the whole arch steps in one frame from primary to the shift colour. It becomes a steady dichroic wall that never flashes, while the 15-pod strip strobes at 10 Hz at the limiter. As rpm drops on an upshift, the fins roll back edge-on from the right in a wave. The wave is only the rpm falling (needle spring, no added delay). In a corner, the accent sheen slides across the fin faces with the G-driven light.
- **Telemetry:** rpm + per-gear thresholds → each fin's angle θ = 90°·(1 − clamp((rpm − r_i)/band)) on the ribbon spring with feed-forward, and the pod strip. Gear and speed → hero glyphs. Lateral G → light azimuth, which sets where the accent sheen sits on the fins. Throttle → face brightness 70→100%. Per-wheel slip angle → each tyre fin's yaw; tyre temps → its thermal step; slip ratio → amber/red fin rim. Brake → caution rim on the front tyre fins. Native fields only; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** Architectural dichroic-glass fin installations that transmit and reflect two colours by angle; kinetic louvre facades; flip-disc and split-flap displays as rotating-element readouts; Apple Liquid Glass specular following geometry (WWDC25).
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only. Fins are analytic oriented quads (projected width w·/cos θ/) with a two-role thin-film shader, and a 256-entry dichroic ramp LUT is baked from the palette at load. MSDF atlases: Saira Condensed, Saira, Titillium Web.
- **Risk:** Design risk: at high rpm, 28 face-on fins make a wall of light that out-shouts the gear and breaks the colour budget. To avoid it: fin faces at ≤45% intensity with bloom on fin edges only, accent limited to a ≤10% fresnel sheen, and a dimming pad that keeps the gear at ≥7:1. Edge-on fins stay at hairline alpha. Performance: 32 instanced quads in one pass, plus bloom. Estimate ≈0.12 ms.

#### ApertureNine — build #53 · cockpit
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 34 · new.

**Category note.** In the Liquid Glass category the iris is seen through glass: lens elements of the kit's clear optical glass sit in front of the blades and refract them, and the blades stay black metal. Its family stays Modern Digital 2027. The iris geometry is in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths).

A nine-blade camera iris at X 1920 is the tach. Its black-anodised titanium SDF blades are filled oledBlack and read only through 1 px machined edges and a brushed anisotropic lobe (≤18% luminance) that follow the G-driven light. Rpm rotates the blades: the aperture closes from r 200 at idle to r 128 at the car's shift rpm, and blade 1's tip points to the current rpm on a fixed 300° ring numbered in Barlow Semi Condensed 600 (cap 34). Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 200, always clear inside the aperture, under 15 outside-in pills (24 px) crowning the ring. Rank 2: Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 112, at +420. Rank 4: a camera light-meter ladder for longitudinal G directly beneath the speed.

- **Signature moment:** Shutter lock: at the car's shift rpm the nine blade edges close into one perfect circle around the gear numeral. Their rims step to a steady `shift` while only the pill crown strobes at 10 Hz. On an upshift the iris snaps open to the new rpm on the `ribbon` spring, with a 1-frame 60% edge flash, like a leaf shutter firing. In corners the highlight travels blade to blade around the ring.
- **Telemetry:** rpm/idle/max + per-car shift → blade rotation (linear in rpm), aperture radius, ring index, stage pills; rpm above shift → blade rim colour; gear → aperture glyph; speed → tabular speed; acceleration z (longitudinal G) → light-meter ladder (right = acceleration in go, left = braking in warning, 0 at centre); boost → shy '+0.8 BAR' readout styled as exposure compensation; lateral G → light azimuth (highlight runs edge to edge) and parallax (gear 0, blades −2, ring −4 px).
- **References:** Leica Summilux 9-blade iris diaphragms; Hasselblad leaf shutters; Gossen Lunasix exposure meter; Porsche 992.2 shift-at-12 track mode
- **Phase 6 assets:** A brushed anisotropic tangent map for the blades (strength in R, rotation in G) with an analytic fallback; shared MSDF Saira Condensed 600/800, Barlow Semi Condensed 600 and Titillium Web 600; everything else procedural.
- **Risk:** Design: a large rotating structure in the periphery could compete with the gear, and closing blades could clip the numeral. Fix: the blades are black with only their 1 px rims lit; the aperture is clamped at r ≥128 (the gear's half-diagonal is ≈119 px at cap 200); blade motion is direct telemetry on the ribbon spring with no overshoot, and idle is still. Perf: nine analytic blade SDFs in one 640×640 quad plus the ring ≈0.07 ms.

#### LumenEtch — build #62 · cockpit
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 30 · new.

A frameless, untinted 1200×500 crystal slab, seen only through two 1 px polished face edges offset 6 px to show its thickness. Every graphic is laser-etched frost lit by a virtual LED bar in the bottom edge. Etch brightness therefore falls with height, I = exp(−h/λ), with a 70% floor inside the gear and speed. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 210) as solid frosted etch with a crisp 1 px cut line, plus a 40 px strip of 17 etched dots fed by a top-edge LED bar. Rank 2: speed on the left, Saira 300 cap 112, ghost 8s. Rank 3: 40 radial etched lines on a 200° arch, one LED each. Rank 4: an etched tyre plan under the gear. Titillium Web labels.

- **Signature moment:** Light pours in. On show, the LED bars ramp the light reach λ from 0 to 600 px over 200 ms, so the cluster fills from the bottom up instead of fading in. On an upshift the new gear lights at 70% in the same frame and fills bottom-up to 100% in 50 ms. Its surplus light escapes sideways along two etched hairlines and runs up the slab's polished side edges as a 1 px glint at 4000 px/s. At the limiter only the top dot strip strobes at 10 Hz; the polished face edges hold a steady, non-emissive exit line in the shift colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm + per-gear thresholds → which of the 40 edge LEDs feed the etched arch and the 17-dot strip (outside-in). Gear and speed → etched glyphs. Throttle → light reach λ 480→640 px, so the slab glows deeper under load; this is bounded and never goes below the 70% floor on ranks 1–2. Acceleration x/z → face-edge thickness parallax and the light azimuth on the polished edges. Tyre temps (4 thermal steps) and per-wheel slip ratio/angle → tyre-plan etch colour and amber/red slip outlines. Brake → a caution tick on the tyre plan's front axle. Native fields only; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** Edge-lit laser-engraved acrylic signage and awards; Oblivion (2013) Bubbleship UI as a hologram embedded in a glass cockpit (GMUNK); Lucid Gravity's bezel-free floating OLED; Tron: Legacy's layered relief light.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only. Etch masks are SDF/MSDF (Saira Condensed, Saira and Titillium Web atlases), and the frost uses the shared 128² blue-noise tile at ≤4% amplitude. No plate or bezel textures.
- **Risk:** Design risk: the exponential falloff dims the top of the gear below the blur gate, and dense etched lines moiré on the WRGB subpixels. To avoid it: a 70% floor inside the rank 1–2 masks, etched lines ≥3 px with SDF AA, frost amplitude ≤4%, and nothing lit outside the etch, so off pixels stay black. Performance: one SDF pass over the 1200×500 clip, plus bloom on the gear, arch and strip only. Estimate ≈0.15 ms.

#### FibreOpticCorona — build #69 · cockpit
Category **Liquid Glass** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 33 · new.

96 drawn-glass fibres fan out of a polished ferrule that holds the gear. The 2 px cores sit unlit at alpha 30 and curve up and out as cubic Béziers to tips on a 210° elliptical arc (600×260). Rank 3: fibres light in rpm order, carrying light visibly from the ferrule to a polished bead tip (r 6); white is reserved for the tips. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 200) on the 240 px ferrule face, a sapphire disc with a 1 px rim, plus a 15-pod strip (40 px) above the arc apex. Rank 2: speed left of the ferrule, Saira 300 cap 112, ghost 8s. Rank 4: a 37-cell close-packed fibre faceplate under the ferrule. Titillium Web labels.

- **Signature moment:** Upshift pulse: a bright packet leaves the ferrule rim and runs out along every lit fibre at 4000 px/s. The fibres have equal optical length, so all the tips flash together for one frame at 60%. A downshift sends the packet inward, landing on the gear. At the limiter only the pod strip strobes at 10 Hz; the lit fibres stay steady and their tips warm to the shift colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm + per-gear thresholds → number of lit fibres and the pod strip. Throttle → brightness gradient along each lit fibre (core 60→100%). Gear → ferrule glyph and pulse direction. Speed → tabular field. Acceleration x/z (felt force) → the lit faceplate cell, with its neighbours at 30%. Tyre combined slip → the lit cell spreads to its 6 neighbours as grip goes. Lateral G → light azimuth on the ferrule rim. Brake → caution on the faceplate rim. Native fields only; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** Fibre-optic faceplates and fibre-optic lamps; Tron: Legacy's procedural line work under a strict rulebook (GMUNK); Bugatti Tourbillon centre dial with satellites; the WWDC25 Liquid Glass glow that starts at a contact point and spreads.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only. The 96 Bézier fibres are baked at load into a distance + arc-length texture (RG16F over the clip), so drawing them costs one fetch per pixel. Also a faceplate SDF. MSDF atlases: Saira, Saira Condensed, Titillium Web.
- **Risk:** Design risk: 96 hairlines read as greeble and moiré on WRGB, and lit fibres break the colour budget. To avoid it: tip spacing ≥14 px, 2 px cores with SDF AA, unlit fibres at ≤12% luminance, and lit fibres in primary only with white on the tips; the texture is rebaked only on a scale change. Performance: one fetch pass over the clip, plus bloom on ranks 1–3. Estimate ≈0.16 ms.

### Apple Vision

#### VisionSpatialGlass — build #10 · cockpit
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 2 · rebuild.

**Category note.** It leads the Apple Vision category. Its panes are the kit's clear optical glass: where this brief gives a pane a tint, `docs/specs/optics.md` wins.

A frameless spatial triptych at four depths. Front: a 480×330 squircle pane (tint 36) holding the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 180) above the speed (Saira 200, cap 96, ghost 8s). Behind it, two 420×200 wing panes are toed in 14° by a true homography, so their far edges are 12% shorter. Rank 3: a 10 px light ribbon crosses both wings left to right and passes behind the front pane, where it is visibly refracted (14 px offset, 1.5 px chromatic split at the rim). Rank 1: a 900×38 glass blade floats frontmost above the stack, with 15 pods filling outside-in. Rank 4: a hovering attitude puck under the front pane. The wings carry only Titillium Web nano labels. Every rim takes the one G-driven light.

- **Signature moment:** Z-hop upshift: a 1-frame 60% glint lights on the far wing's rim and hops forward one layer at a time (wing, front pane, shift blade), 40 ms per layer. At the same time the front pane recedes 6 px in depth on the fast spring and the new gear pops forward from 1.12. Lateral G shears the stack by 0/2/4/6 px per g (parallax spring ω 12 rad/s), so depth is something you read, not something drawn. At the limiter only the 38 px blade strobes at 10 Hz; the wings' outer rims hold a steady shift-colour line. On show, the panes materialise back to front over 200 ms.
- **Telemetry:** rpm + car-profile thresholds → wing ribbon length and the 15 blade pods. Gear and speed → front pane. Acceleration x/z (felt force) → layer parallax, the one light's azimuth on every rim, and the bead rolling on the puck. Pitch and roll → puck tilt drawn as a perspective ellipse. Verify both fields against tests/golden/packets before use (97% rule); the fallback is tilt from smoothed G. Yaw rate → wing toe-in ±2°. Brake → caution step on the puck rim. Tyre combined slip → the bead's halo spreads. Native fields only; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** visionOS window depth stacking with ornaments floating in front; Apple Liquid Glass lensing (WWDC25); BMW Panoramic iDrive upper-layer/lower-layer choreography; Porsche Taycan curved cluster with angled side zones.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: SDF panes and blade, homography-projected wings and an analytic puck. It shares the LiquidGlass sapphire normal map (optional) and the blue-noise tile. MSDF atlases: Saira, Saira Condensed, Titillium Web.
- **Risk:** Design risk: four translucent layers overlapping into a muddy grey stack (the v1 build read as three unrelated boxes), and digits on tilted panes failing the blur gate. To avoid it: every digit sits on the untilted front pane, the wings carry only the ribbon and nano labels, pane overlap is ≤12% of the area, and summed glass alpha is clamped to 64 where panes overlap. Performance: the refraction pass samples only the ribbon target inside the 480×330 front-pane footprint, the panes are 4 SDF draws over about 0.5 Mpx, and bloom covers ranks 1–3 only. Estimate ≈0.30 ms.

#### CarplayIsland — build #18 · cockpit
Category **Apple Vision** · target `inspired` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 53 · new.

**Inspired by Apple's CarPlay Ultra** (TJX, 2026-11-28: "apple vision style, carplay inspired"). Checked against Apple's, Aston Martin's and the press's own pages on 2026-11-28 (sources under References). CarPlay Ultra began rolling out on 15 May 2025, first for new Aston Martin orders in the US and Canada (DBX, Vantage, DB12 and Vanquish); in August 2026 Aston Martin was still the only maker shipping it. It fills every driver screen, but the car draws the gauges itself, from an asset package the iPhone sends at pairing, so they stay live if the phone link drops; maps and media stream from the phone, and camera and driver-assistance views stay the car's. The driver picks from a curated set of themes that Apple and the maker designed together, and can change their colours and wallpaper but not their layout. Aston Martin has three layouts: twin round dials with a square between them, a full-screen map with speed and revs moved to the bottom, and one of coloured horizontal bars. The space between the dials cycles through map, audio, tyre pressure and trip information, and takes widgets and notifications. Speed and fuel are mandatory gauges and the rev counter is optional; a cruise-control target shows as a dot on the dial and a speed limiter as a line, and the centre of a round gauge shows the transmission state or a brand image. Each maker sets the dial geometry: where the arc starts and ends, the count, length and style of the ticks, the needles, gradients and stroke width. No shift light is documented: the rev gauge takes a value and a state, and the car sets the state past the engine's limit. iOS 26 (announced 9 June 2025) brings the new design, Liquid Glass, into CarPlay and CarPlay Ultra. Apple's type is its own SF family; this dash uses open-licence stand-ins. The dash takes the twin-dial layout with glass widgets between the dials, and adds one part the real display does not have: the island.

Starting layout, at three depths. Far: two round dials on true black, Ø400 at (±390, −10). The left dial is the speed, with its tabular digits (Saira 300, cap 90) at its centre, 391 px from the gear (rank 2). The right dial is the rev counter (rank 3): a thin tick ring, numerals inside it and a lit arc. Middle: a clear glass squircle, 440 × 300 at (0, −40), that overlaps the inner edge of both dials by 30 px and holds the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 170); under it two glass widgets, 200 × 110 at (±110, +175). Near: the island, a glass capsule 40 px tall at y −290 over ten lamp seats that span 760 px (rank 1). Rank 4: the left widget holds three rings in the manner of Apple's activity rings (power as a share of the highest power seen this session, boost, brake), and the right widget holds a G-dot. Rank 5: Titillium Web labels on the dials, and the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The island. At rest the capsule is a pill 200 px wide round the two centre seats, and the other eight seats show as dim dots, so the strip's place never moves. As the revs enter the shift window the glass stretches outward from the centre, one seat a side at a time, and each seat lights as the glass reaches it; at the shift point the island is 800 px wide and all ten are lit. It moves on a critically damped spring with ω = 14 rad/s: from rest, x(t) = x_T + (x₀ − x_T)(1 + ωt)e^(−ωt), so it never overshoots and is within 5% after 0.34 s. When the target moves while the glass is still moving (seat by seat, or on letting go), the spring keeps its speed, x(t) = x_T + (c₁ + c₂t)e^(−ωt) with c₁ = x₀ − x_T and c₂ = v₀ + ωc₁, and a fast reversal can pass its target once, so the glass edge is clamped between the pill's 200 px and the 800 px it reaches at the shift point. The seats follow the glass, never the other way round. On an upshift it lets go and returns to the pill in one motion. On the limiter only the lamps inside the island strobe, and the arcs of both dials hold a steady `shift` colour. Lateral G shears the glass of the three depths by 0, 2 and 4 px per g, and the dial ticks seen through the rim of the middle pane are refracted by it; the numerals and the ten seats stay where they are, and only the glass over them moves.
- **Telemetry:** speed → the left dial's arc and digits; rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the right dial, the island and the limiter; gear → the numeral in the pane; power → the outer ring, as a share of the session's highest power (one stored `f32`); boost → the middle ring (it shows throttle, and its label says so, while boost has stayed under 0.05 bar since the start); brake → the inner ring; acceleration → the G-dot on the `gBall` spring and the parallax of the three depths. Every one of these is a verified field. No map, no media and no phone data: the game sends none.
- **References:** Apple Newsroom, "CarPlay Ultra, the next generation of CarPlay, begins rolling out today" (15 May 2025, https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2025/05/carplay-ultra-the-next-generation-of-carplay-begins-rolling-out-today/); Aston Martin, "Aston Martin debuts Apple CarPlay Ultra for iPhone users" (https://www.automotiveworld.com/news-releases/aston-martin-debuts-apple-carplay-ultra-for-iphone-users/); WWDC24 "Say hello to the next generation of CarPlay design system" (gauges, ticks, arcs, the content area, https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2024/10112/) and "Meet the next generation of CarPlay architecture" (who draws what, the speed and rpm signals, https://developer.apple.com/wwdc24/10111/); Top Gear's hands-on with the three Aston Martin layouts and the thumb-pad swipes (https://www.topgear.com/car-news/tech/new-apple-carplay-ultra-tested-hands-every-new-feature); Apple Newsroom on iOS 26 and the new design in the car (9 June 2025, https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2025/06/apple-elevates-the-iphone-experience-with-ios-26/); the iPhone's Dynamic Island, for the way the island stretches; Apple Watch activity rings; `LiquidGlass` and `VisionSpatialGlass` in this catalog for the glass
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural: the kit's clear optical glass for the pane, the widgets and the island; the dial ticks and arcs as shapes; the `LiquidGlass` sapphire normal map and the blue-noise tile, shared. ComfyUI: the studio panorama the glass rims reflect. Blender: none, unless step 1 finds that the real dials have a rendered bezel. MSDF atlases for Saira 300, Saira Condensed 700 and Titillium Web 600.
- **Risk:** Two round dials are what `AmgMbuxClassic` has: the glass pane between the dials, the island and the rings are what make it this dash, and the sibling sheet puts the two side by side. A resting island is narrower than the 720 px the strip must span: the ten seats always span 760 px, and only the glass grows. No Apple logo, no app icons, no symbols from Apple's set, and the word CarPlay appears nowhere on the dash; the logo zone shows the driven car's logo. Clear glass over black can read as a grey box: tint by absorption only, and every figure keeps a black core. Perf: two dials with instanced ticks, four glass pieces and ten lamps, est. 0.25 ms.

#### WindshieldHud — build #27 · raised HUD
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family ADAS & HUD · catalog row 16 · rebuild.

**Category note.** The category trait is the virtual image placed 10 m out in the world. The collimated strokes stay; no glass panes.

Civil AR-HUD virtual image at (1920,1595), focused 10 m out: no plate, only a feathered #000 dimming pad (alpha ≤150, 48 px feather) hugging the symbology so it reads against bright sky. Vertical stack on X=1920: Z1 crown of 21 slender 8×22 px staves on a 16 px-sag arc spanning 960 px; beneath, a full-width 1 px horizon whose 3 px rpm core (rank 3) grows centre-out. Gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 160) sits in a 1 px four-tick reticle, speed beneath in B612 Mono (cap 96), then Z4: a flat perspective map ribbon with the trail behind and a dashed κ prediction 3 s ahead, stepped accent/caution/warning braking zones and an ego chevron. Near symbols take ±4 px G parallax; the ribbon stays fixed.

- **Signature moment:** The crown closes. Staves fill outside-in on per-gear thresholds while the horizon rpm core grows centre-out; at the shift rpm the two meet at both ends, the crown becomes one closed arc in the shift colour, and the reticle's four ticks snap 8 px inward. At the limiter only the 38 px-tall stave row strobes 10 Hz; the horizon and reticle hold steady. In a drift the ribbon's ego chevron yaws to the nose heading while a 2 px slip arrow continues along the velocity, with β labelled in B612 Mono.
- **Telemetry:** rpm → staves and horizon core; gear → reticle; speed → readout and lookahead length (3 s × v); world position x/z (bytes 244..255) → 120-point trail; brake sampled into each trail node → stepped zones (accent < 20%, caution < 60%, warning ≥ 60%); angular velocity y / max(v, 3 m/s) → κ arc ahead; local accel z + speed → a predicted braking zone ahead at v²/(2/a/); local velocity x/z → β slip arrow; roll → ribbon counter-roll so it stays level; lateral G → light azimuth and parallax. There is no map or route in the packet: the road ahead is a constant-curvature guess, drawn dashed and faded out by 1.5 s so it never reads as map data.
- **References:** BMW Neue Klasse Panoramic Vision plus 3D HUD (upper alert layer over a black band); Mercedes S-Class AR-HUD (10°×5° window, image at about 10 m); Garmin G1000 synthetic-vision flight-path symbology; F/A-18 collimated stroke HUD
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (SDF staves, reticle and horizon, a ribbon triangle strip); MSDF atlases B612 Mono, Saira Condensed 700, Titillium Web 600; the shared blue-noise tile to dither the dimming-pad feather
- **Risk:** It floats over the windscreen, so contrast against sky and road is the failure mode (v1 even drew an opaque white box). The dimming pad must let the gear pass 7:1 and the speed 4.5:1 in the blur gate at its alpha cap; lit area stays ≤8% of the 1280×480 window, nothing below y +230 so the sightline stays clear, and the user height offset is honoured. `fh6-core::trail` stores only x/z today, so it needs a brake byte per node in its fixed array (no allocation). Cost: a ≈400-triangle ribbon plus SDF strokes, <0.1 ms.

#### ArNavWayfinder — build #36 · cockpit+hood
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family ADAS & HUD · catalog row 17 · rebuild.

**Category note.** Gates, chevrons and the road edges are placed in the world, which is the category's trait; no glass panes.

A first-person 'through the glass' road inside the binnacle (eye 1.1 m, FOV 40°, no ego car), its vanishing point pinned on X=1920 at y −140 exactly where the hero gear stands (Saira Condensed 800, cap 170), so every line leads to the gear. Z1: ten 48×38 px wayfinder chevrons at y −292, five per side pointing inward, filling outside-in. Rank 3: the two road edges are the tach, 5 px light rails filling from the bottom corners toward the gear, parametrised by screen length. Speed sits on the horizon at left (Saira Light, cap 112), lap delta and next-gate distance at right (Titillium). Z4 between the rails: the remembered road as a 1.6 m driving-line ribbon with 1 px brake-gate arches and apex diamonds; uncharted road shows only a fading κ arc.

- **Signature moment:** The target-gear ghost. 250 m before a remembered brake gate, the gear you used there on your best pass appears as a ghost numeral (inkGhost, cap 90) just beyond the hero gear at the vanishing point, with one tick per downshift still needed. Each downshift steps the hero toward it, and when they match the two merge with a 1-frame 60% rim flash. Passing a gate drops its arch flat onto the ribbon, and the next arch rises from the vanishing point over 200 ms. The limiter strobes only the chevron strip, while the rails hold a steady full shift-colour fill.
- **Telemetry:** Road-ahead knowledge is self-mapped from our own history, never from the game: a preallocated 65,536-sample memory (x, y, z, speed, brake, throttle, gear every 2 m, ≈1 MB, spatially hashed in 32 m cells) of roads already driven this session plus the best lap. World position + yaw → memory writes and nearest-sample localisation (±64-sample window, heading match ±30°); local velocity + angular velocity y → κ arc when uncharted; speed → ribbon scroll and gate distance; recorded brake onset → gate placement; recorded throttle/brake → ribbon tint (accent / caution); recorded gear vs live gear → target-gear ghost and downshift ticks; rpm → chevrons and rails; lap number, current/best lap time and distance travelled → best-lap reference swap and delta (Saira Condensed tabular, go/warning by sign); roll → horizon counter-tilt.
- **References:** Mercedes MBUX AR navigation arrows (judged slow, so ours are never delayed); highway-in-the-sky tunnel-gate avionics displays; road chevron-alignment signs; circuit brake-marker boards at 100/50 m
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (SDF rails, chevrons, gate arches, apex diamonds, a ribbon triangle strip); MSDF atlases Saira Condensed 800, Saira Light, Titillium Web 600; the shared blue-noise tile
- **Risk:** The risk is memory and localisation, not pixels. Preallocate the 1 MB ring and spatial hash at start (no per-frame allocation), bound the nearest-sample search, and require a heading match so a crossing road is never taken for the one ahead. Gates are dashed after one pass and turn solid only when a second pass agrees within 4 m, so a mis-registered gate never reads as truth. Lap number, lap times and distance travelled are not decoded yet: add them against tests/golden/packets (97% rule) or integrate speed·dt. GPU <0.08 ms.

#### GeodesicWell — build #45 · cockpit+hood
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family ADAS & HUD · catalog row 40 · new.

**Category note.** The lattice floor and the well are geometry placed in the world under the car; no glass panes. The floor is a Gaussian well ([Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths)), and the tyre slip and suspension travel this brief reads are not verified yet.

Spacetime instead of scenery. A perspective lattice floor (24 px pitch at the near edge, 2 px hairlines at alpha 46, dissolving to black over 120 px) is bent into a Gaussian well beneath the gear by the force the driver feels. The car is a 28 px light puck at the well's centre, and its predicted path is a geodesic rolling across the warped grid. Z1: twelve 20 px round photon pods on a straight strip at y −292, filling outside-in. Gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 180) floats above the well at (0, −160), speed left (Saira Light, cap 112), boost and lap right (Titillium). Rank 3 is an event-horizon ring, the well's rim on the floor, filling from its far point toward the near point on both sides.

- **Signature moment:** The grip horizon. The well's depth tracks /G/ and its centre slides toward the felt force (half a σ per g). When any tyre's combined slip exceeds 1.0, the rim tears: grid lines crossing it break open on the sliding side for as long as the slide lasts, then heal on the fast spring when grip returns. At the shift rpm the ring closes at its near point in the shift colour. At the limiter only the pod strip strobes 10 Hz while the closed ring holds steady.
- **Telemetry:** Local accel x/z (felt force = −a) → well offset and depth on the gBall spring; combined tyre slip ×4 → rim-tear side and length; angular velocity y / max(v, 3 m/s) → geodesic curvature; local velocity → β notch on the puck; speed → grid lines passing at true ground speed (still when stopped); normalised suspension travel ×4 → puck tilt; rpm → pods and event-horizon ring; gear and speed → hero readouts; boost and lap → right readouts. Everything is derived from the packet; no world is synthesised.
- **References:** Flamm's paraboloid and rubber-sheet embedding diagrams of curved spacetime; Oblivion (2013) GMUNK lattice discipline; Tesla FSD ego-centred chase framing
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (a 64×32 vertex grid displaced in the vertex shader, SDF puck, pods and ring); MSDF atlases Saira Condensed 800, Saira Light, Titillium Web 600
- **Risk:** Constant grid motion and warping can feel busy or nauseating and pull the eye from ranks 1–2. Keep the grid at hairline alpha ≤46, cap the well depth at 60 px on screen, fade the lattice beyond 1.5 s of travel, and never write the floor to bloom. The ring is rank 3 but in perspective, so parametrise its fill by angle on the ground ellipse and taper it from 6 px near to 3 px far. Fine scrolling lines can moiré on WOLED: use analytic fwidth AA and keep the 24 px minimum pitch. GPU <0.07 ms.

#### Lightpainter — build #54 · cockpit+hood
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family ADAS & HUD · catalog row 41 · new.

**Category note.** The light trail is a volume floating in space, seen from a fixed camera; no glass panes.

A long-exposure photograph whose shutter never closes. A fixed high three-quarter camera (pitch 38°) looks down on black, and the last 2 km of your path glows as a 3D light streak with true elevation: width ∝ speed, brightness ∝ throttle, and a warning taillight core wherever you braked. It fades by distance driven, never by time. The car is the streak's bright head. Z1: a 40 px shutter strip of nine 64 px blade pods, filling outside-in. Below it sits the rank-3 exposure-meter tach: 1/3-stop ticks from −3 to +3 EV, the shift rpm at 0 EV, the redline as overexposure, and a glowing cursor. Gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 170) and speed (Saira Light, cap 112) form a centred couplet over the image, inside 1 px viewfinder corner marks (rank 5).

- **Signature moment:** The axle split. In a drift the single streak splits into front- and rear-axle trails, offset by wheelbase·sin β, and a faint caution veil fills the gap between them, so the drift angle is written into the road as a widening ribbon. When grip returns the trails re-merge on the fast spring. At the shift rpm the meter cursor lands on 0 EV and the shutter blades close inward in the shift colour. At the limiter only the shutter strip strobes 10 Hz.
- **Telemetry:** World position x/y/z → a dedicated preallocated 1024-node trail at 2 m spacing (2 km) with real elevation from y; speed → streak width; throttle → streak brightness; brake → warning taillight core; local velocity x/z → β and the axle-split offset; rpm → EV meter and shutter blades; gear and speed → couplet; lateral G → light azimuth on the streak's 1 px rim. No map exists: only roads you have driven appear, and the fade is by distance, so idle is still.
- **References:** Night long-exposure light-trail photography of mountain passes; the ±3 EV, 1/3-stop exposure scale in mirrorless-camera viewfinders; GPS altitude-profile curtain charts; Ryoji Ikeda's data-light installations
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (a ribbon triangle strip built from the trail, SDF shutter blades, meter and corner marks); MSDF atlases Saira Condensed 800, Saira Light, Titillium Web 600; the shared blue-noise tile to dither the streak's low-alpha tail
- **Risk:** Self-overlapping streaks can blow out into a white blob and break the rank order. The streak is rank 4: cap alpha at ≤160, use max-blend rather than additive where it crosses itself, and never write it to emissive; only the cursor, blades and gear glow. Auto-framing 2 km must not constantly re-zoom: step the scale per speed band with a 300 ms ease. Overlap with WindshieldHud's map ribbon is avoided by viewpoint (high orbit, past only) and by encoding (width = speed, exposure = throttle). GPU <0.06 ms.

#### TreadSeismograph — build #63 · cockpit+hood
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family ADAS & HUD · catalog row 42 · new.

**Category note.** The four road ribbons are graphics anchored in the world behind the car, which is the category's trait; no glass panes. The per-wheel fields this brief calls real are not verified yet: see its row in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths).

The car writes the road. A low chase camera looks forward over a 1 px chassis footprint, and from each contact patch a strip-chart ribbon runs back toward the viewer: a 256-sample seismograph of what that wheel felt. Height is suspension travel (≤40 px), fine teeth are surface rumble, a mirror-glass segment with a fresnel rim is puddle depth, and lateral smear is slip angle, so a slide literally draws sideways. Z1: ten 48×14 px stylus pods at y −292, filling outside-in. Gear centre (Saira Condensed 800, cap 170), speed left (Saira Light, cap 112). Rank 3 is a horizontal rpm scale whose glowing stylus head trails a 1.5 s ink trace that scrolls up like chart paper. Tracks are hairline neutral ink; colour appears only for slip and lock.

- **Signature moment:** The kerb strike. A rumble-strip hit spikes only the touched wheels' tracks in a sharp burst of teeth (inkMuted, no glow) that scrolls back at road speed. Wheelspin widens the driven tracks into caution slip bands; lock-up turns the front pair into flat warning dashes. At the shift rpm the stylus reaches the shift mark and every pod takes the shift colour. At the limiter only the pod strip strobes 10 Hz.
- **Telemetry:** Normalised suspension travel ×4 → track height; surface rumble ×4 → teeth; puddle depth ×4 → glass segments; tyre slip angle ×4 → lateral smear; slip ratio ×4 → caution spin bands and warning lock dashes; tyre temps ×4 → contact-patch thermal step, shown only outside the optimal window; speed → scroll rate (one sample per 0.5 m, so the tracks are still when stopped); rpm → pods, stylus and trace; gear and speed → hero readouts. All per-wheel data is real; nothing is synthesised.
- **References:** Seismograph and polygraph strip-chart recorders; Harold Craft's 1970 stacked plots of pulsar CP 1919 (the ridgeline as a data form); MoTeC i2 suspension and kerb traces used by race engineers; Mercedes Magic Body Control road-surface scanning
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (4 ring-buffer triangle strips of 256 samples, updated in place by ring offset; SDF footprint, pods and stylus; a small fresnel glass segment); MSDF atlases Saira Condensed 800, Saira Light, Titillium Web 600
- **Risk:** Four busy traces could steal attention from gear and speed. Clamp amplitude at 40 px, keep 2 px hairline weight and alpha ≤140, never bloom the tracks, and fade them over the last 40% of their length; at idle they are flat and still. Rumble, puddle depth and wheel rotation speed are not decoded yet: add them and verify against tests/golden/packets (97% rule), or ship with suspension and slip only. GPU <0.05 ms (≈2k vertices rewritten in place, no reallocation).

#### StarSailOrigami — build #70 · cockpit+hood
Category **Apple Vision** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 43 · new.

**Category note.** Placed here by the category review of 2026-11-28: the sail's panels tilt at real depths behind a glass capsule, with parallax, in both views. The capsule is the kit's clear optical glass, and the fold follows its row in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths). Its family stays Sci-Fi.

A deployable light-sail. Behind the gear, a pleated parallelogram-fold sheet (2 rows x 24 panels) extends symmetrically from a 180 px folded stack to 1360 px wide. Its span is the rank-3 tach, over a hairline rpm scale. Each facet is a 1 px-rimmed quad shaded only by the G-driven light, so panels swap brightness as they open (fill at 35% luminance or less, rims up to 70% white). Rank 1: Saira 600 gear (cap 200) on the spacecraft bus, a squircle glass capsule (alpha 44, 1 px rim, crescent), under a 36 px 16-pod strip. Rank 2: Saira 300 tabular speed (cap 96) under the bus. Rank 4: an unglowing four-quadrant sun-sensor G meter under the speed.

- **Signature moment:** Light from geometry, then deploy lock. As rpm rises the folds open, and each facet flips from dark to lit as its normal crosses the light vector; in a corner the moving light makes the highlights roll across the sail. At the shift rpm the sail reaches full span and the fold angle goes flat. All 48 facets then face the light together, and one steady specular sheet in the shift colour crosses the sail while only the strip strobes at 10 Hz. Upshift: the sail retracts with the rpm on the ribbon spring, rippling dark-to-light inward. Boot: the bus capsule materialises in 200 ms, then the sail unfolds to live rpm.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm sets the sail span and fold angle (rpm / shift rpm -> 0..90 deg, ribbon spring with feed-forward) and the strip. Gear drives the glyph and Speed the speed. Felt G (-AccelerationX/Z) sets the light vector (azimuth 135 + 25 x gLat / 1.5 g) that shades every facet, and places the sun-sensor spot with a 500 ms trail. Pitch and Roll tilt the sail's parallax layer by up to 3 px. Boost drives the bus crescent intensity.
- **References:** Origami-folded deployable solar arrays and the IKAROS solar-sail demonstrator (Miura-ori fold); spacecraft quadrant sun sensors; Apple Liquid Glass 'highlights follow geometry' (WWDC25); the dauphine facet swap from the research's horology section
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: 48 quads with analytic normals. An optional 1K brushed-film anisotropic normal map for facet sheen (procedural anisotropic fallback). Saira MSDF atlas.
- **Risk:** A wide reflective sheet could break the colour budget and make rank 3 glow everywhere. Facet fill stays at 35% luminance or less, only the sail's two outer edges and the leading lit facet are emissive, and panels stay within glass alpha 64. Span and fold come straight from rpm through the ribbon spring with feed-forward, so the sail never lags. Cost: 48 quads plus the bus glass, about 0.1 ms.

### Psychedelic

Each figure follows its row in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths), which corrects these older briefs where the two differ, and the [colour law](#psychedelic-and-abstract-pattern-first-colour-from-the-model) applies to all eight.

#### ★ KineticLiquidMetal — build #7 · cockpit
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 27 · rebuild.

**Category note.** The first Psychedelic dash to be built: its thread settles how a figure is tested against its row in the maths table. The shared `common::liquid` tilts the surface by atan(gLat ÷ (1 + |gLong|)); hydrostatics puts vertical g there, not longitudinal g, so its handover asks for that fix to the shared code.

Sealed borosilicate, not a drawing: a 240° glass channel (R 225, bore 40, open at the bottom) wraps a domed ampoule (R 140) at (0,−20), and the live mercury column inside is the tach (rank 3), its meniscus the exact reading. Mercury is an SDF smooth-union surface, normals from a 1D height field, shaded by `liquidMetal` env bands that rotate with the G-light, tinted 0.18 toward primary. Gear: Exo 2 800, cap 200, floating above a mercury pool tilting on `fluidSlosh`. Eleven Ø26 droplet beads (≤14 px sag) form the shift strip; speed (Exo 2 300, cap 112) fills the bottom gap above a 520×28 G spirit-level vial. Glass alpha 28–44, 1 px rims, nothing filled on black.

- **Signature moment:** Limiter (family signature): the meniscus swells and warms steadily to the `shift` hue (never flashes) while droplets pinch off the column tip at 5 Hz, run down a 1 px capillary and merge into the ampoule pool by smooth union (k 10 px). The pool level visibly rises the longer you sit on the limiter, then drains back over 300 ms after the shift. Only the bead strip strobes at 10 Hz. Upshift flourish: the old glyph sinks into the pool (0.92, out in 50 ms) and the new one surfaces through the meniscus from 1.12 with the 24 px upward slide on the `fast` spring. The surface closes over it with one under-damped slosh.
- **Telemetry:** current/idle/max rpm → mercury column length on the `ribbon` spring with velocity feed-forward (the meniscus tip is exact). Per-car, per-gear thresholds → 11 beads, outside-in, 0 ms attack, 120 rpm off-hysteresis. gear → ampoule glyph plus an upshift slosh impulse. speed → Exo 2 tabular field with ghost 8s. gLat/gLong → pool tilt atan(−gLat/(1+/gLong/)) on `fluidSlosh`, vial slug position (gLat) and slug length (gLong), and the light azimuth that rotates the env bands. throttle/brake → twin test tubes at x −560 (`go` / `warning`). Car slip angle → right micro-readout, stepping to `caution` above 8°. Surface rumble (a packet field the decoder does not expose yet) and suspension-travel velocity → ripple impulses on the column that decay flat. The Phase 8 ω = rpm·0.08 idle ripple is dropped, so idle is still.
- **References:** Family hero frames in docs/design/heroes/fluid-generative/ (channel, ampoule, beads); laboratory mercury manometers and clinical thermometers; the liquid-metal register of Terminator 2 (1991), material only; the Lexus LFA meter (an instrument faster than any mechanism).
- **Phase 6 assets:** Liquid-metal flipbook (64 frames, 512² atlas) with matching flow and normal maps as the budget fallback. A 256×1 HDR studio env-band LUT baked in Blender (procedural fallback: the token `liquidMetal` env bands). A 128² blue-noise tile for low-alpha glass gradients. Exo 2, Titillium Web and Barlow Semi Condensed MSDF atlases. Brand crest dial variant etched on the lower-left channel cap (the logo zone).
- **Risk:** The hero's bead arc is about 90 px tall at 4K, which breaks the ≤42 px strobe strip: flatten it to ≤14 px sag (40 px band). Mercury reflections around the gear can fail the blur gate: add a feathered dimming pad (alpha ≤140×(1−scrim)) and hold the pool below the glyph baseline. The v1 build shipped a white full-canvas wash with no readable gear; nothing may fill black. Perf: the column and pool are 1D shallow-water height fields (128 + 64 cells) on one tiny compute dispatch, drawn by one SDF pass clipped to about 700×600, roughly 0.06 ms. Switch to the flipbook if bench-render exceeds 0.3 ms.

#### CymaticResonanceField — build #14 · cockpit+hood
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 28 · rebuild.

An acoustic-levitation bench. A polished black-chrome circular plate seen at a 62° tilt (ellipse 1100×300, centre (0,+90)) carries 20k GPU sand grains that gather on the nodal lines of circular-plate modes J_m(k_mn·r)·cos mθ. Its near rim, 9 o'clock round to 3 o'clock, is the tach (rank 3): a 6 px lit ribbon with Barlow Semi Condensed numerals inside; the far rim is a 1 px hairline. The gear (Exo 2 800, cap 200) levitates above the plate centre at (0,−130) on a dimming pad; speed (Exo 2 300, cap 112) at (+330,−120). Fifteen 10×36 tuning-fork tines form the shift strip at y −296. Grains are rank 4: inkMuted tinted 18% primary, no bloom; the centre clamp carries the crest.

- **Signature moment:** An upshift re-tunes the plate. The grains are thrown off in one impulse (about 120 ms of airtime along the plate normal, foreshortened by the tilt) and land on the nodal lines of the new, lower mode, so the whole figure re-forms in about 300 ms. The gear glyph itself changes in frame 1. Modes step through 9 discrete (m, n) figures across the car's idle → shift range with 120 rpm hysteresis, never a continuous morph, so each rpm band has its own recognisable figure. Limiter: the plate holds the highest mode and the grains warm steadily to `shift`; only the tine strip strobes at 10 Hz.
- **Telemetry:** rpm → near-rim tach ribbon (`ribbon` spring with feed-forward, exact) and the mode index (9 bands from the car profile's idle → shift rpm). Per-gear thresholds → tines, outside-in. gear → glyph plus the re-tune impulse. speed → tabular. throttle → drive amplitude: on a lift the lines are soft and fuzzy, at full throttle they are razor-thin. steer + gLat → figure rotation θ0 on the `light` spring (±20°). gLong → grains creep along the tilt under braking. Surface rumble (decoder extension needed; verify the offset on tests/golden/packets) and suspension-travel velocity → grain-hop impulses that are zero on smooth tarmac, so idle is still. The Phase 8 continuous mapping n = rpm/1200, m = V/40 is replaced by stepped modes; the square-plate Chladni equation stays as the procedural fallback figure.
- **References:** Ernst Chladni's sand-figure plates (1787) and modern Chladni-plate demonstrations; Hans Jenny, Cymatics (1967); Bessel-mode visualisations of a vibrating circular membrane; Bang & Olufsen sculptural black-aluminium audio hardware for the plate finish.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only. Bessel J_0..J_6 root table and 512-sample radial profiles computed at load into an RGBA16F LUT; the plate rim bevel normal is analytic. Optional: a polished black-chrome rim normal map. 128² blue-noise for grain seeding. Exo 2, Barlow Semi Condensed and Titillium Web MSDF. Brand crest dial variant for the centre clamp.
- **Risk:** The v1 build was a white full-canvas wash with a square pattern and no gear; 20k grains could also read as noise behind the readouts. Keep the plate wholly in Z4 (only its far rim rises behind the gear), grains at alpha ≤0.5 and ≤20% luminance when settled, no bloom, and dimming pads so gear and speed pass the blur gate. The grains must never be read as the tach; the rim ribbon is the exact reading. Perf: grain gradient descent on −∇(u²) via LUT for 20k points ≈0.02 ms, instanced 2 px sprites ≈0.04 ms, rim and tach SDF ≈0.02 ms. Hood [4] fit (1760×400): plate 1500×220, gear at gearCompact cap 150, speed to its right.

#### ChronoMultiverse — build #23 · cockpit+hood
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 29 · rebuild.

Ferrofluid is black, so on OLED only its highlights exist: bodies are true #000 and form is drawn purely by G-moved specular env-band reflections and 1 px rims. A ferrofluid pool runs along a 1300 px crown arc (rise 50, apex y −200); Rosensweig spikes stand up along it from the left to the rpm front (rank 3), the lead spike tallest with a primary-tinted ridge. The gear (Exo 2 800, cap 200, ink) is a polished pole piece at (0,−20) whose outline bristles with micro-spikes; speed (Exo 2 300, cap 112) at (+290,−10). Twelve 30 px pole caps are the shift strip at y −292. Under gear and speed, four ferro-drops in a car footprint read tyre slip.

- **Signature moment:** A gear change is a field-collapse wave. A single dip travels left to right through the spike row: each spike slumps to 30% and recovers on the `fast` spring. At the same time the micro-spike fringe crawls off the old gear outline onto the new one by smooth-union migration over 168 ms. The glyph swaps in frame 1 and the rpm front stays exact throughout. The spike lattice warps, compressing toward the front like a field gradient. Limiter: the lead spikes fuse into one tall crown spike at the redline end, lit steadily in `shift`; only the pole-cap strip strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm → spike-front position (`ribbon` spring with feed-forward; the front is exact) and lattice compression toward it. throttle → field current = spike height (40–100%, through the `snap` spring). boost → spike sharpness (apex angle 38° → 18°). gLat → spike lean (shear on `fluidSlosh`); gLong → foreshortening under braking. Per-gear thresholds → pole caps. gear → pole-piece glyph plus the collapse wave. speed → tabular. Per-wheel combined slip (packet field, decoder extension needed; tyre slip angles, which are decoded, as the interim input) → spikes erupting on each ferro-drop (0 at grip, up to 7), with `caution`/`warning` outline steps at 0.08/0.18. Tyre temperatures (decoder extension) → 4-step thermal tint on each drop's 1 px rim.
- **References:** Sachiko Kodama's ferrofluid sculptures ('Protrude, Flow', 2001); the Rosensweig normal-field instability lab demonstration; spec-only photography of piano-black automotive trim; the family hero frames for the env-band language.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Ferrofluid spike flipbook (32 frames, 512²) with a normal map as the budget fallback. A dark-studio 256×1 env LUT with two softbox highlights that move with the G-light (procedural fallback: token `liquidMetal` env bands × 0.6). 128² blue-noise. Exo 2 MSDF. Spikes themselves are procedural: cone SDF lattice smooth-unioned with the pool.
- **Risk:** Black on black: spec-only shading vanishes when the light leaves the softboxes. Every cone keeps a 1 px rim at alpha ≥60, and the gear is an ink-filled pole piece, never a black ferro glyph (v1 shipped an opaque black box with a tiny gear). Spikes boil on noisy throttle, so throttle passes the `snap` spring with a 2% deadband. Perf: 64 cone SDFs smooth-unioned in a 1300×160 clip plus 4 drop SDFs, about 0.04 ms. Hood [4] fit: the crown widens to 1600 px and flattens to rise 24, with gear compact cap 150.

#### AuroraVeil — build #32 · cockpit
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 45 · new.

**Category note.** Placed here by the category review of 2026-11-28: a living curtain computed from the physics of an aurora. Under the colour law its rays may take the colours of the real emission lines. The heading tape of this brief needs yaw, which is not verified: until it is, the tape is left out. Its family stays Sci-Fi.

A polar-night sky over the binnacle. A 1 px field arc (1300 px span, 100 px sagitta) crosses the crown. From it, 64 vertical rays rise as the rank-3 tach: an aurora curtain whose ray heights grow with rpm from both ends inward, up to 160 px. Each ray is a primary-to-transparent gradient at alpha 64 or less, so the sky stays black. Rank 1: Saira 600 gear (cap 200) under the arc's apex, with a straight 34 px 16-pod strip just beneath the arc. Rank 2: Saira 300 speed (cap 96) beside the gear. Rank 4: an unglowing heading tape from Yaw under speed and gear, with ticks every 15 deg. Labels are Chakra Petch.

- **Signature moment:** Solar wind. Lateral G shears the whole curtain sideways (rays lean up to 12 deg), and longitudinal G compresses or lifts it, on the under-damped fluidSlosh spring (T 0.62 s, zeta 0.35). The aurora sways with the car and settles perfectly still at constant input. At the shift rpm the curtain closes at the centre and the ray tips take the shift colour, steady, while only the strip strobes at 10 Hz. Upshift: the curtain drops with the rpm, and a single 1 px fold travels along the base from the centre outward.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm drives the ray heights (outside-in) and the strip. Felt G (-AccelerationX/Z) drives the curtain shear and lift and the light azimuth. Gear drives the glyph and Speed the speed. Yaw drives the heading tape; this is heading only, not navigation. Boost drives ray brightness. DistanceTraveled drives the slow fold drift, so a parked car's curtain is still.
- **References:** All-sky auroral time-lapse photography (curtain and corona forms); magnetosphere field-line diagrams; Lucid Gravity's floating black-glass restraint; BMW Panoramic Vision's single dark band
- **Phase 6 assets:** An optional 1x256 ray-noise texture baked at load (procedural fallback). Saira and Chakra Petch MSDF atlases. The curtain, arc and tape are procedural.
- **Risk:** A large luminous curtain costs bloom, can pull the eye off the gear, and could turn into constant motion. Rays are gradients that fade to 0, only the lower 30% of each ray is emissive, and the curtain is capped at 1300x160. Sway comes only from G through the spring, and fold drift only from distance. Cost: one 1300x200 ray pass plus bloom, about 0.12 ms.

#### HarmonographDrive — build #41 · cockpit
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 48 · new.

A pendulum drawing machine that reads the drivetrain. Two mirrored 90° arcs bracket the gear like parentheses (R 300 around (0,−60)) and fill bottom-to-top together with rpm (rank 3), Barlow Semi Condensed numerals outboard. Gear (Exo 2 800, cap 200) at (0,−60); speed (Exo 2 300, cap 112) at (0,+95). Fifteen Ø22 pendulum bobs form the shift strip at y −300. Under the speed, a 640×80 drawing table carries a 2 px harmonograph figure in primary whose frequency ratio is the current gear's learned overall ratio, so every gear draws its own figure; the stroke decays along its length like ink running dry. Arcs are G-lit steel channels; Michroma wordmark between the arc feet.

- **Signature moment:** Grip is stillness. With the drivetrain locked, the figure is frozen. The instant the driven wheels spin, it precesses (rotates through its own lobes) at a rate proportional to the slip. It reverses direction under brake lock-up and smears visibly during clutch slip, so traction reads as motion in the corner of the eye. Each gear owns a figure (1st a dense 5-lobe rosette, top gear a near-ellipse). On a shift the pen redraws the new figure over 200 ms while the glyph swaps in frame 1. Limiter: the arcs are full and the figure tints steady `shift`; only the bob strip strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm and driven-wheel rotation speeds (packet fields, decoder extension needed; verify offsets on tests/golden/packets per the 97% rule). drivetrain_type selects the driven axle; the live ratio is r = ω_engine/ω_wheel. A per-gear ratio is learned when /r − r̄/ < 1% for 0.5 s with clutch at 0, and stored in the car profile. Frequencies (1, map(r_gear) into 1.5–5) give the figure; phase precession dφ/dt = k·(r/r_gear − 1) with a 1.5% deadband. throttle → damping (lift = decaying spiral, full = crisp closed figure). rpm → parenthesis arcs (`ribbon` spring with feed-forward). Per-gear thresholds → bobs. gear → figure swap. speed → tabular. The ratio is synthesised from these fields; the game sends no gear-ratio data.
- **References:** Blackburn-pendulum harmonographs (19th-century drawing machines); rose-engine guilloché geometry from watch-dial finishing; X-Y oscilloscope vector art; a chassis-dyno printout's per-gear ratio curves.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: a static 4096-vertex polyline whose positions are evaluated in the vertex shader from 6 uniforms, drawn as an SDF-antialiased 2 px stroke with a length-wise alpha falloff. 128² blue-noise. Exo 2, Barlow Semi Condensed and Michroma MSDF. Brand wordmark (crest optional at ≤96 px).
- **Risk:** It depends on wheel rotation speeds the decoder does not expose yet, and on per-car ratio learning. Until a gear is learned, the figure uses rpm/speed with a nominal 0.33 m tyre radius and shows no precession, so it can never fake slip. Precession must stay subtle at cruise, or the figure jitters on bumps: use the deadband plus the `fast` spring on r. The figure is rank 4 and never blooms. Perf: one 4k-vertex line draw plus two arc SDFs, about 0.03 ms.

#### IsochromaticLoadPath — build #50 · cockpit
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 46 · new.

A clear polycarbonate single-seater seen from above, set between crossed circular polarisers: unstressed material is optically black (off pixels), so light exists only where the car is loaded. Its front wing (1100×60, y −235) is the tach: a point-load puck rides it to the rpm position (rank 3, emissive) while a fan of fringes radiates beneath. Thirteen pills on the wing's leading edge form the shift strip (y −292). Gear (Exo 2 800, cap 200) sits in the tub at (0,−30) on a dimming pad; speed (Exo 2 300, cap 84) on the engine cover at (0,+165). Contact patches at (±400,−170) and (±400,+175) show load as monochrome isochromatics, sin²(πN) in primary only; chassis drawn in 1 px rims.

- **Signature moment:** Load transfer becomes light. Trail-brake into a corner and the outside-front contact patch blooms into 6–8 tight concentric fringe loops (Flamant circles tangent at the patch) while the unloaded inside-rear goes black. Throttle out of a hairpin lights the driven axle's patches. Lateral G rotates the analyser ±10°, so dark isoclinic bands sweep across the chassis as you turn, which is a real polariscope effect. Gear change: the wing's point load releases, and its fringe fan collapses and re-forms at the new rpm on the `snap` spring while the puck moves instantly. Limiter: the wing fringes reach order 6 and freeze in a steady `shift` tint; only the pill strip strobes.
- **Telemetry:** normalized_suspension_travel FL/FR/RL/RR (decoded) plus gLat/gLong load transfer → point-load magnitude at each contact patch (`fast` spring, 2% deadband). drivetrain_type → which axle shows torque-reaction fringes under throttle (RWD rear, FWD front, AWD both), scaled by throttle. Per-wheel combined slip (packet field, decoder extension needed; verify against golden packets) and tyre slip angles → patch smear direction, with `caution`/`warning` outline steps at 0.08/0.18. rpm → puck position (`ribbon` spring with feed-forward) and wing fringe order N ∝ rpm/shiftRpm (≤6). Per-gear thresholds → pills, outside-in. gear → tub glyph. speed → tabular. gLat → analyser angle on the `light` spring.
- **References:** Photoelastic stress analysis (Brewster, 1816; Frocht, Photoelasticity, 1941); Flamant's half-plane point-load solution (1892), whose isochromatics are circles tangent at the load; engineering polariscope demonstrations of loaded discs and hooks; photoelastic granular force-chain experiments.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: a closed-form superposition of four Flamant point-load stress tensors per pixel, principal stress difference → fringe order N → sin²(πN). This is art-correct, not FEA. Optional car-class plan silhouettes as SVG through resvg (single-seater, GT, SUV, truck) with an SDF capsule-and-box fallback. 128² blue-noise for low-alpha fringe tails. Exo 2 MSDF.
- **Risk:** Fringe moiré and shimmer on the WRGB subpixels at high N: cap N at 8, antialias each fringe with fwidth, and fade fringes thinner than 3 px. Noisy suspension data would make the loops boil; the spring and deadband keep idle still. A plan-view car can read as a toy, so draw it with 1 px rims only, at ≤20% luminance, with no fills. Perf: four tensor sums per pixel over two clips (about 0.25 MP), one pass, about 0.03 ms, the cheapest live visual in the family.

#### AeroSmokeTunnel — build #59 · cockpit
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Modern Digital 2027 · catalog row 35 · new.

**Category note.** Psychedelic through a closed-form flow. Its family stays Modern Digital 2027.

A calm top-down wind tunnel sits under the gear and speed. A brand-tinted 1 px plan silhouette (sedan, SUV or supercar by class, 180 px, heading up) sits in a 900×200 flow window with mask-dissolved edges. Smoke streamlines wrap it, drawn as contour lines of an analytic stream function: Joukowski potential flow round an ellipse, rotated by slip β, plus a yaw-rate vortex. They are 2 px, ink at ≤22% luminance, with dashes that drift with distance travelled. Above: Saira Condensed 800 gear, cap 200, at X 1920; Saira Condensed 600 speed, cap 112, at +320; a thin 1100 px crown ribbon tach (7→14 px) under modern11 diamonds. Titillium labels and a shy FLOW · MODEL tag.

- **Signature moment:** Crossflow: in a drift, the incoming smoke swings to the car's true direction of travel while the silhouette keeps its heading, so you see the air hit the flank at angle β. The wake sheds a counter-rotating vortex pair whose strength follows yaw rate. Heavy braking pinches the streamlines into a pale amber stagnation fan at the nose (steady, ≤30%). At standstill the dashes stop; at the limiter only the diamond strip strobes.
- **Telemetry:** local velocity x/z → β and free-stream direction (hidden below 5 m/s); distance travelled → dash phase (still at rest); speed → streamline spacing; angular velocity y → vortex circulation Γ; brake + longitudinal G → stagnation pinch; car class/ordinal → silhouette choice; rpm/idle/max + shift → ribbon and diamonds; gear; speed; lateral G → light azimuth and parallax. The flow is an idealised model built from velocity and yaw, not aero data or CFD, and is labelled that way.
- **References:** F1 wind-tunnel smoke-rake visualisation; Joukowski potential-flow plots from classic aerodynamics texts; Tesla FSD ego state shown on the ego model; Lucid Gravity Zen-theme restraint
- **Phase 6 assets:** Three plan-view car silhouettes as SVG (sedan, SUV, supercar), rasterised once through resvg, with a procedural capsule fallback; shared MSDF Saira Condensed 600/800 and Titillium Web 600.
- **Risk:** Design: constantly flowing dashes in peripheral vision can pull attention from ranks 1–3 and be mistaken for real aero data. Fix: ≤22% luminance and no emissive; contours stay still at constant β (only the dashes move, and they stop at rest); hidden in the race and zen density tiers; an explicit MODEL tag. Perf: one analytic fragment pass (a complex sqrt and log per pixel) over 900×200 ≈0.03 ms, with no particles and no simulation.

#### AbyssalShearBloom — build #66 · cockpit
Category **Psychedelic** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 47 · new.

A black lagoon, dark until disturbed: light is emitted only where water shears, like dinoflagellates. Two mirrored wake channels sweep up and out from the gear like gull wings (each 600×180, 1 px glass rims at alpha 28); a forcing jet runs centre-out in both to the rpm point, its tip the exact reading (rank 3, emissive), while shear along its flanks lights plankton in primary. Gear (Exo 2 800, cap 200) in a clear lens at (0,−80) on a dimming pad; speed (Exo 2 300, cap 112) at (0,+95). Thirteen 12×30 photophore lozenges form the shift strip at y −296. Beneath, a 900×80 lagoon holds four wheel paddles stirred by tyre slip; its light never blooms.

- **Signature moment:** Shear is the only light source. Hold a constant speed on smooth tarmac and the lagoon goes black while the wings hold a steady glowing jet. Slide a tyre and its paddle tears a glowing vortex pair through the lagoon, which spirals and fades over 400 ms, so you see slip at the corner where it happens. Each upshift fires one pressure ring outward from the gear lens through both wings: a single expanding band of plankton light. Limiter: the jet tips curl into steady glowing eddies at the wing ends in `shift`; only the photophore strip strobes.
- **Telemetry:** rpm → jet length centre-out in both wings (exact tip, `ribbon` spring with feed-forward). throttle → jet velocity and flank-shear brightness. Per-gear thresholds → photophores, outside-in. gear → upshift pressure ring (a single splat impulse). speed → tabular. Per-wheel combined slip (decoder extension needed) and tyre slip angles (decoded) → paddle stirring force at four footprint positions. Surface rumble (decoder extension) → small stir impulses at the paddles. Puddle depth (decoder extension) → plankton density in 3 steps (a wet track gives a brighter sea). gLat/gLong → lagoon body force (slosh). All forcing at zero → the lagoon dissipates to black. The plankton are a synthetic visual, not sensor data.
- **References:** Noctiluca scintillans 'blue tears' bioluminescent coastlines; Pavel Dobryakov's MIT-licensed WebGL Fluid Simulation (solver defaults and relief shading); teamLab's interactive light-on-water installations; deep-sea photophore anatomy for the shift lozenges.
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural: a stable-fluids solver clipped to two 600×180 wing rects and a 900×80 lagoon (sim 160×48 and 224×20, RG16F velocity, R16F emission at half resolution, warm-started pressure). 128² blue-noise for plankton sparkle seeding. Optional 32-frame sparkle-and-eddy flipbook as the budget fallback. Exo 2 MSDF.
- **Risk:** The 2D solver is the costliest item in the family. Clip every pass to the three rects (about 0.3 MP at full resolution), run 12 warm-started Jacobi iterations at sim resolution and emission at half resolution: about 0.12–0.18 ms. Switch to the flipbook if bench-render exceeds 0.3 ms. Lagoon light is rank 4 and must never feed the emissive buffer. The sea must settle to black: dissipation exp(−d·dt), emission decay τ 250 ms, and a strain threshold S0 so numerical noise never sparkles at idle.
