A 6 px phosphor halo on ranks 1–3 only.

- **Signature moment:** Lock and SHIFT cue. As the lock line fills outside-in, at the shift rpm the four corner brackets snap into a closed box round the gear and a boxed 'SHIFT' cue (B612 Mono, shift colour) lights steady above it. At the limiter only the lock line and the box's 4 px top bar strobe 10 Hz. In a drift the flight-path marker slides off the boresight by β (14 px/°, clamped ±35°), a 1 px leash joins them, and β in degrees hangs off the marker's wing. Below 5 m/s the marker fades out over 120 ms.
- **Telemetry:** rpm → lock-line dashes (per-car, per-gear thresholds), rpm tape, SHIFT cue, top-bar strobe; gear → hero; speed → speed tape and box; roll → ladder rotation (×2, spring); pitch minus a 2 s baseline → ladder offset (×4); local-frame velocity → FPM, with β = atan2(v_x, v_z) laterally and atan2(v_y, v_z) ×4 vertically (axes and signs verified against golden packets); local accel x/z → G readout and session max-G latch (B612 Mono, cap 20, rank 5); current lap time → bottom-right clock; yaw → an optional shy 3-digit heading box.
- **References:** F/A-18 and F-16 HUD conventions (fixed tapes, flight-path marker against boresight, asymmetric pitch ladder); NASA NTRS flight-path-marker HUD studies; the Airbus B612 cockpit typeface; Oblivion's hologram-in-glass line discipline
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only (SDF strokes, tapes, ladder, brackets); MSDF atlases B612 Mono and Saira Condensed 700
- **Risk:** Symbology density can eclipse the rank order, and a car's attitude is too small to read. Hold the ladder at alpha 140 with only ±2 rungs drawn, no halo below rank 3, and exaggerated, sprung roll and pitch with the baseline removed. A single mid-green hue must still give the gear 7:1, so lift it to OKLCH L ≥ 0.80. v1 drew a filled green box and a full-canvas white wash, both banned. Draw only generic HUD conventions, never an exact copy of a real aircraft's format. Hood [4]: tapes move to ±560 and the attitude window shrinks to 760×150. Cost ≈300 SDF segments, <0.06 ms.

#### NeuralCyberSynapse — build #37 · cockpit
Category **Sci-Fi** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 23 · rebuild.

A living neuron. The gear (Saira Condensed 800, cap 210) is the soma: a 260 px cell body with a 1 px membrane rim lit by the G light. A dendritic tree branches upward 1 -> 2 -> 4 -> 8 -> 16, Bezier fibres tapering 10 -> 2 px across 1300 px. Rank 3: the trunk and four branch levels are the tach, lighting root-outward up to 75% of shift rpm; dark fibres sit at alpha 22. Rank 1: the 16 terminal boutons form a straight 30 px shift strip on the crown, filling outside-in. Rank 2: Saira 200 speed (cap 100) right of the soma, beside the trunk. Rank 4: an unglowing three-channel EEG raster (throttle, brake, steer) under speed and soma. Fibres are primary; the signal front is primary lifted to white.

- **Signature moment:** The action potential climbs the tree. Rpm drives a depolarisation front from the soma up through the branch levels in the same frame, with a 2-node bright spike at the front whose height scales with throttle. From 75% the signal lands in the boutons outside-in, so tach and shift lights are one continuous gesture ending above the gear. Upshift: 12 vesicle particles burst from the soma rim for 150 ms (event only). Limiter: the boutons strobe at 10 Hz and the soma membrane thickens to 3 px in accent, steady, replacing the old flashing 'lightning' idea.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm sets the front height in the tree and the bouton fill (per-car, per-gear thresholds). Gear drives the soma glyph; this replaces the v1 hardcoded '7'. Speed drives the speed. Accel sets the spike amplitude and EEG channel 1, Brake EEG channel 2 (warning), and Steer EEG channel 3 (signed). Clutch opens a gap in the soma rim. Felt lateral G sets the light azimuth and a 2 px lean of the tree. The EEG traces scroll in time but stay flat under constant input, so idle is visually still.
- **References:** Confocal micrographs of Purkinje neurons and Cajal's public-domain ink drawings; textbook saltatory-conduction diagrams; Tron: Legacy procedural line work (Digital Domain); Oblivion GFX rationed greeble (GMUNK)
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: the tree is a fixed, symmetric Bezier layout computed once at load and drawn as SDF capsules. An optional 256x256 membrane noise for the soma interior (gradient fallback). Saira and Chakra Petch MSDF atlases.
- **Risk:** Organic branching can read as noise at 220 mph. The tree is strictly symmetric with fixed levels, the 16 boutons stay a straight 30 px strip, and fibres beyond the front sit at alpha 22. Cost: 31 fibres and 16 pods instanced as SDF capsules plus three 512-point polylines, about 0.15 ms, with bloom on the front, boutons and gear only.

#### SolarisWarpCore — build #46 · cockpit
Category **Sci-Fi** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 24 · rebuild.

A fusion containment rig. Two vertical magnetic-bottle columns, 380 px tall, 90 px wide and 520 px apart, flank the gear. Each is ringed by 10 D-shaped coil hoops drawn as 1 px titanium rims lit by the G light; the space between them stays black. Rank 3: plasma rises in both columns in step, a soft-edged filament with a primary-to-white core whose waist pinches with boost. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear (cap 220) between the columns, under a 36 px 12-pod strip spanning the column caps. Rank 2: Saira Condensed 700 speed (cap 96) under the gear. Rank 4: four unglowing tyre-thermal cells at the column bases in the 4-step thermal colours. Power and torque sit in Chakra Petch micro-labels.

- **Signature moment:** Arc bridge. At the shift rpm both plasma columns reach their caps and a steady plasma arc (6 px core, shift colour) bridges over the gear between them; the arc never flickers, and only the strip strobes at 10 Hz. Boost compresses each filament from 40 to 22 px, and lateral G pushes the plasma toward one wall by up to 18 px. Upshift: each coil the plasma drops through gives a 1-frame 60% rim flash, top to bottom, within 50 ms. Boot: the coils energise in pairs from the base in 5 steps.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm drives the plasma height and the strip (per-car thresholds). Boost drives the filament pinch and core brightness. Felt lateral G drives the plasma deflection and the light azimuth. Gear drives the glyph and Speed the speed. TireTemp FL/FR/RL/RR drive the thermal cells (cold / optimal / hot / overheated, 150 ms crossfade). Power and Torque drive the readouts under the right and left columns. Fuel sets a 1 px level tick on the left base. DistanceTraveled advects the plasma noise, so a parked car is still.
- **References:** Tokamak and stellarator coil-hall photography (JET, Wendelstein 7-X); Z-pinch plasma physics diagrams; Tron: Legacy three-term neon; Oblivion GFX restraint
- **Phase 6 assets:** An optional 16-frame plasma-filament flipbook with a flow map (procedural curl-noise fallback). A coil-hoop normal strip (procedural SDF torus-section fallback). Saira Condensed and Chakra Petch MSDF atlases.
- **Risk:** Two tall glowing columns could out-shout the gear, and clock-driven plasma would break 'idle is still'. Column plasma peaks at 70% luminance while the gear is 100% ink on a dimming pad, and the noise advects by distance and boost, never by the clock. Cost: two 90x380 noise columns plus bloom, about 0.12 ms; the arc is a single SDF capsule.

#### AeroPlasmaBlade — build #55 · hood
Category **Sci-Fi** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 25 · rebuild.

A delta-wing planform seen from above, 1700x380 on the hood: apex up at X=1920, 55 deg leading-edge sweep, tips low on both sides. The skin is never filled; 1 px rims and three spar hairlines draw it, lit by the G light. Rank 3: both leading edges carry a dielectric-discharge line (6 px core, neon halo) that ignites from the apex to the tips in 20 steps per side, with 1000-rpm ticks along the edge. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear (cap 190) in the fuselage under the apex, with a 34 px 16-pod crown strip. Rank 2: B612 Mono speed (cap 96) on the spine below. Rank 4: nine unglowing hairline streamlines across the chord.

- **Signature moment:** Leading-edge ignition. Rpm lights the discharge from the apex to the tips. At the shift rpm both tips spin up steady wingtip-vortex spirals in the shift colour, while only the crown strip strobes at 10 Hz. In a slide the nine streamlines swing to the car's real sideslip angle beta and peel off the leeward tip as dashed separated flow, so a drift reads as airflow. Upshift: the discharge falls back to the new rpm and the apex chevron gives a 1-frame 60% rim flash.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm drives the discharge length and the strip. Gear drives the glyph and Speed the B612 speed. VelocityX/Z give beta = atan2(v_lat, v_long) for the streamline angle, hidden below 5 m/s with a 120 ms fade. DistanceTraveled advects the streamline dashes. NormalizedSuspensionTravel left and right pairs droop each tip by up to 12 px as an aero-load proxy, labelled as such. Front versus rear TireSlipAngle picks which side separates (understeer or oversteer). Boost drives the discharge core brightness. Felt lateral G sets the light azimuth.
- **References:** Dielectric-barrier-discharge plasma-actuator flow-control research footage; wind-tunnel smoke-streamline photography; 1960s ogival-delta supersonic planform drawings; aviation HUD stroke conventions (2 px core, 1 px AA)
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: planform, discharge, streamlines and vortex spirals are SDF. B612 Mono and Saira Condensed MSDF atlases.
- **Risk:** A wide planform can read as decoration, and a diagonal tach is slower to read than an arc. The gear stays on X=1920, the skin is never filled, the discharge steps are discrete, and 1000-rpm ticks sit on each edge. Hood-only placement keeps the planform from crowding the cockpit binnacle. Cost: up to 60 SDF segments plus 9 polylines, with bloom on the discharge, gear and strip only, about 0.1 ms.

#### EvaBioSync — build #64 · cockpit
Category **Sci-Fi** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 21 · rebuild.

A pilot-to-machine synchronisation monitor grown on the family's 24 px lattice. Two honeycomb wings, three cells deep (44 px cells, 4 px gaps, 1 px rims), sweep up from a 300 px pointy-top hexagonal gear capsule like a bioreactor membrane. Rank 3: cells ignite from the wingtips inward along one fixed spine path, mirroring the shift row, filled primary with a 2-stop nucleus gradient; unlit cells are neutral rims at alpha 22. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear (cap 210) in the capsule, with a 36 px 14-pod strip capping the wings. Rank 2: Saira Condensed 700 speed (cap 100) under the capsule. Rank 4: a hex-framed dual-trace sync scope under the speed, unglowing. The capsule rim is lit by the G light.

- **Signature moment:** Phase-lock. The scope draws the driver's demand (Steer) and the car's response (yaw rate) as two traces. With grip they fuse into one bright line and SYNC reads 100%. In a slide they split, and the gap between them shades caution, so oversteer and understeer read as a visible gap. Under braking below -0.2 g, 1 px octagonal barrier rims expand from the capsule in caution at 3 Hz or less and alpha 40 or less, settling at zero decel. Upshift: one hex ring ripples outward through the lattice in 150 ms. Limiter: the strip strobes at 10 Hz and the capsule rim holds steady in accent.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm drives the wing cells and the strip. Gear drives the capsule glyph and Speed the speed. Steer is compared with AngularVelocityY scaled by speed for the two traces; this is a bicycle-model proxy with a nominal 2.7 m wheelbase and 14:1 ratio, labelled as an estimate. TireCombinedSlip x4 gives SYNC % = 100 x (1 - mean combined slip, clamped). AccelerationZ (longitudinal G) drives the braking barrier rims. Felt lateral G sets the light azimuth. Accel and Brake drive two shy input ticks on the capsule base.
- **References:** Bioreactor and cell-culture monitoring displays; aerospace honeycomb sandwich panels; Oblivion GFX single-lattice discipline (GMUNK); The Expanse's faction-colour restraint (Pushing Pixels interview)
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: an instanced hex SDF lattice, with the traces as polyline SDFs. An optional 512x512 cell-nucleus noise for the lit-cell gradient (gradient fallback). Saira Condensed and Chakra Petch MSDF atlases.
- **Risk:** The old brief echoed a well-known anime UI. This direction drops every borrowed name, glyph and styling; the lattice, scope and capsule are original. Some 84 cells read slower than a bar, so the fill follows one fixed mirrored spine and rank 1 is carried by the strip. Cost: about 90 hex SDFs in one instanced draw plus two 256-point polylines, about 0.2 ms with bloom on the lit cells only.

#### ArcLadderCascade — build #71 · cockpit
Category **Sci-Fi** · target `concept` · family Sci-Fi · catalog row 44 · new.

A high-voltage impulse generator. Twelve capacitor stages, each two 1 px plates with a spark gap, form a 220 deg horseshoe ladder (radius 260) over the gear; insulators are hairlines and nothing is filled except charge. Rank 3: stages charge in sequence with rpm, each gap filling with a primary glow, joined by a thin bus. Rank 1: Saira Condensed 800 gear (cap 210) at the discharge electrode, a 1 px polished sphere rim, under a straight 36 px 15-pod crown strip. Rank 2: Saira Condensed 700 speed (cap 100) right of the horseshoe foot. Rank 4: an unglowing shift-timing log under speed and gear, showing the last 12 upshifts as ticks against the car's shift-rpm line.

- **Signature moment:** Cascade on upshift. When the gear rises, the charged stages fire top-down through the gaps into the gear electrode as a 2 px arc per gap, 50 ms in total, once per shift rather than a repeated flash. The ladder then recharges from the new rpm. The log gains a tick showing how close that shift was to the target: go within 150 rpm, caution if early, warning if at the limiter. At the limiter only the strip strobes at 10 Hz, and the gaps hold a steady corona in the shift colour.
- **Telemetry:** CurrentEngineRpm against per-car thresholds drives the stage charge and the strip. A rising edge on Gear, together with the rpm in the frame before the change, triggers the cascade and the log tick against the car_profiles.json shift rpm. Gear drives the glyph and Speed the speed. Boost drives the charge-glow intensity. Clutch dims the gaps while it is in (open circuit). Felt lateral G sets the light azimuth on the electrode sphere and plates.
- **References:** Multi-stage impulse-generator test halls (spark-gap ladders); high-voltage laboratory photography; AiM and MoTeC per-gear shift-light threshold tables; Tron: Legacy three-term neon
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural only: plates, gaps and arcs are SDF, with one jagged polyline per gap generated once at load. An optional 512x512 ceramic-insulator normal strip (SDF fallback). Saira Condensed MSDF atlas.
- **Risk:** Spark arcs are flashes. They are limited to one 50 ms cascade per upshift with 2 px cores and under 3,000 px2 of total arc area, and reduced-flash mode swaps them for a 150 ms charge drain. A 260 px horseshoe keeps 90 px clear of the gear cap. Cost: about 0.1 ms, with bloom on the charge, gear and strip only.

### Abstract

Each figure follows its row in [Real geometry and maths](#real-geometry-and-maths), and the [colour law](#psychedelic-and-abstract-pattern-first-colour-from-the-model) applies to all eight. These briefs, and `CarplayIsland`'s, were checked against sources on 2026-11-28 and their numbers re-computed; the sources are in each References line. Step 1 of each dash still reads its sources itself and records them in `RESEARCH.md`.

#### VisionPetals — build #15 · cockpit
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 51 · new.

**A flower of clear glass petals, one petal for each gear.** The figure is taken from Apple's breathing flower, checked against Apple's own pages and the press on 2026-11-28. On Apple Watch it is the Breathe session of the Mindfulness app (the Breathe app from watchOS 3 in 2016 until watchOS 8 renamed it in 2021): several overlapping circles that grow as the user inhales and shrink as they exhale, turning about half a turn as they open. Apple publishes no circle count; the well-known rebuilds use six. The circles are opaque teal and green that add up where they overlap, on black, and the default pace is 7 breaths a minute (4 to 10 can be set). On Apple Vision Pro the Mindfulness flower is a 3D orb of petals that parts on the inhale and can float out round the viewer while the room dims. Nothing in either is glass: the clear glass, and a petal for each gear, are this dash's own. Here the revs do the breathing, and the flower has as many petals as the gear that is engaged.

Starting layout. Rank 4, the flower: N equal discs of clear optical glass, radius R = 110, whose centres sit on a circle of radius d round (0, −10), at the angles 90° + 360° × i ÷ N, measured anticlockwise from the right with up positive, so the first petal points up. N is the gear. The even petals sit one depth behind the odd ones. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 150) on a true-black core, Ø190, in front of the flower's middle, and ten clear beads on a straight line at y −296 (760 × 28) as the shift lights, filling outside-in. Rank 3: a thin arc of radius 246 round the flower, 240° over the top from 8 o'clock to 4 o'clock, lit from the left, numerals outside it. Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira 300, cap 90) on true black to the right, at (+370, −10). To the left, at (−370, −10), a small G-dot. Rank 5: Titillium Web labels and the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The bloom. The opening follows the revs: d = R × (rpm − idle) ÷ (shift rpm − idle), clamped to [0, R]. At idle every petal lies on the same disc and the flower is closed. As the revs climb the petals part, and the radius of the circle round the whole flower grows from 110 to 220 (d + R). In first and second gear the flower is not centred: in first at d = R it is one disc above the core, touching the middle. In sixth at d = R neighbouring petals sit R apart, the six-circle rosette of the Seed of Life without its middle circle. As the flower opens it turns by 360° ÷ N, so it rotates as Apple's does and ends where it started. At the shift point d = R, every petal passes exactly through the middle, and the rim of each petal lights in the `shift` colour, steadily. On an upshift one more petal unfolds out of its neighbour while the revs fall and the flower half closes: the angles go from 360° ÷ N to 360° ÷ (N + 1) on the `snap` spring. Where petals overlap the glass is deeper, so the rosette shows how many petals cover each point: k petals pass T^k of the light under them, where T counts both of a petal's faces (about 4% lost at each, for n = 1.5) and its absorption. On the limiter only the beads strobe. Lateral G shears the two petal depths by 2 and 4 px per g; the core and the gear stay still.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the opening d, the arc, the beads and the limiter; gear → the numeral and the petal count (neutral is the closed disc; reverse is one petal that points down); speed → the tabular speed; throttle → the brightness of the light under the flower; acceleration → the G-dot on the `gBall` spring and the parallax. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** Apple Newsroom, "Apple previews watchOS 3" (13 June 2016, the Breathe app, https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2016/06/apple-previews-watchos-3/) and the watchOS 8 release (7 June 2021, Breathe becomes Mindfulness, https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2021/06/watchos-8-brings-new-access-connectivity-and-mindfulness-features-to-apple-watch); Apple Support, "Start a Reflect or Breathe session on Apple Watch" (https://support.apple.com/en-jo/guide/watch/apd371dfe3d7/26/watchos/26) and "Practice mindfulness on Apple Vision Pro" (https://support.apple.com/guide/apple-vision-pro/devae0eb1c57/visionos); TechCrunch on Mindfulness in Vision Pro (31 January 2024, https://techcrunch.com/2024/01/31/apple-vision-pros-secret-weapon-mindfulness); MacRumors on the 7-a-minute default (2016, https://www.macrumors.com/2016/06/20/watchos-3-apple-watch-breathe-app/); a six-circle rebuild of the Watch animation on CSS-Tricks (https://css-tricks.com/?p=262519); the Seed of Life rosette; `VisionSpatialGlass` and `LiquidGlass` in this catalog for the glass and the handover format; `docs/specs/optics.md` for Beer–Lambert, Snell and Fresnel
- **Phase 6 assets:** The petals are the kit's clear optical glass, each a shallow lens: Blender bakes one petal's normal and thickness maps at 4K from a modelled blank, and the same pair serves every petal. ComfyUI: the studio panorama the rims reflect. Penpot: the board with the flower at gears 1, 3, 6 and 10. No texture is painted inside the glass. MSDF atlases for Saira 300, Saira Condensed 700 and Titillium Web 600. The kit's analytic studio is the fallback.
- **Risk:** Blades round a centre are what `ApertureNine` has: these are full circles of clear glass, they overlap, and their count is the gear. In first and second gear the flower is one petal and then two: that is the cue working, not a fault, and the direction check looks at gears 1 and 2 first. Ten overlapping panes can go grey: the tint comes from absorption only, the summed depth is capped at six petals' worth, and the gear keeps its black core. Glass is the costly paint: if ten discs pass 0.3 ms, the far ring of petals is drawn as rims only. Perf: at most ten glass discs in one glass layer, one arc and ten beads, est. 0.3 ms.

#### LiquidMorph — build #24 · cockpit
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Liquid Glass · catalog row 56 · new.

**Drops of clear glass that run together and pull apart.** Apple introduced Liquid Glass on 9 June 2025 (checked against Apple's newsroom and developer pages on 2026-11-28). In Apple's words, glass shapes in one container "morph" into one another: "as shapes near one another, their paths start to blend into one another", and a container's spacing sets how soon the blending begins; in transitions, shapes morph into and out of each other and small ones are "re-absorbed". The glass bends and concentrates the light behind it rather than scattering it, appears by turning its lensing up rather than by fading in, and carries highlights that travel round its edge. Apple's Clear variant, with no adaptive tint, is the one this dash follows. This dash is made of nothing else: every part is a drop, and the drops join and part with the car. Nothing here is a plate, which is what sets it apart from `LiquidGlass`. The kit already has the tool: pieces in one group share one outline, the smooth union of `docs/specs/optics.md` section 2.

Starting layout, three drops in a row over OLED black, with every light under the glass. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 160) under a round drop, Ø250 at (0, −30), and above it the shift lights: ten drops, Ø34, on a straight line at y −292 with 81 px between centres (763 px over all). Rank 2: tabular speed (Saira 300, cap 90) under a long drop, 280 × 150 at (+330, −30). Rank 4: the same drop mirrored at (−330, −30) shows power, with the peak of the current pull held. Rank 3: a clear channel, 1100 × 36 at y +170, with the rev light running under it from the left and etched ticks. Rank 5: Titillium Web labels etched into the channel, and the logo zone. The numerals never move: each stands on a fixed black core, and only the glass round it flows.

- **Signature moment:** The thread. As each shift stage lights, the lit drops run together from both ends, and at the shift point the ten have become one liquid thread 18 px thick. On an upshift the thread breaks back into ten drops, and the sizes are those of a real liquid thread (Rayleigh–Plateau): the fastest-growing ripple on a thread of radius 9 px has kR = 0.697, a wavelength of 9.01 radii, 81 px, and one wavelength's volume makes a drop of 1.89 radii, 17 px. That is the ideal of a thin, inviscid thread; a real one also sheds small satellite drops, which this dash leaves out. New drops appear by their lensing growing from nothing, never by a fade. On the limiter only the thread strobes. The three large drops do the same thing slowly: the blend length of their smooth union follows the car's longitudinal G, from 20 px under braking of 0.5 g, through 30 px at a steady cruise, to 60 px under acceleration of 0.3 g, after a low-pass of the force, so the row joins at +0.17 g and a cruise is clearly three drops, never flickering at the join. Their surfaces are 65 px apart, and with the kit's smooth union (the natural-log exponential smooth minimum, its k a length) two surfaces join when the gap is 2 ln 2 = 1.386 times the blend length, so under power the row is one body and at a cruise or under braking it is three. The figure belongs to that formula: another smooth minimum joins at another gap.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the channel's light, the drops, the thread and the limiter; gear → the numeral; speed → the tabular speed; power → the left drop, with the peak held until the next pull begins; longitudinal acceleration → the blend length of the three drops; lateral acceleration → the studio light that swings across every rim, and a sideways lean of the two outer drops' glass of at most 12 px. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** Apple Newsroom, "Apple introduces a delightful and elegant new software design" (9 June 2025, https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2025/06/apple-introduces-a-delightful-and-elegant-new-software-design/); WWDC25 session 219, "Meet Liquid Glass" (lensing, materialising, Regular and Clear, https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2025/219/) and session 323 (GlassEffectContainer and glassEffectID morphing, https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2025/323/); SwiftUI's GlassEffectContainer documentation (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/swiftui/glasseffectcontainer); MIT 18.357 Interfacial Phenomena, lecture 11 (Rayleigh–Plateau, kR = 0.697, λ ≈ 9.02 R, https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-357-interfacial-phenomena-fall-2010/d77da8d5f1bb8b69a62682bc0dbc5845_MIT18_357F10_Lecture11.pdf); Eggers and Villermaux, "Physics of liquid jets", Rep. Prog. Phys. 71 (2008) 036601, for satellite drops (not yet opened); I. Quilez, "Smooth minimum" (https://iquilezles.org/articles/smin); `LiquidGlass` in this catalog for the optics, and `KineticLiquidMetal` for a liquid's free surface; `docs/specs/optics.md` section 2 for the smooth union
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural: the kit's clear optical glass with merged outlines; no texture is painted inside the glass. Blender: a normal and thickness pair for a sessile drop at 4K, for the three large drops. ComfyUI: the studio panorama the drops reflect, and a soft caustic for the light each drop throws on the black beneath it. Penpot: the board with the row as one body and as three. MSDF atlases for Saira 300, Saira Condensed 700 and Titillium Web 600.
- **Risk:** Glass with no straight edge can read as a grey smudge on an OLED: the rims come from the kit's Fresnel, the light is under the glass, and every numeral keeps its black core and must pass the blur gate through the glass. Glass that flows next to a numeral distracts: the cores stay still, and the necks form between the drops, never over a digit. The thread must keep the strip's span of at least 720 px (763 px as ten drops, about 817 px as the thread) and its height of at most 42 px in both states. If one group cannot take the thirteen pieces this dash needs, stop and report it. Perf: thirteen pieces in two groups and one channel, est. 0.3 ms.

#### LorenzButterfly — build #33 · cockpit+hood
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 60 · new.

**The Lorenz butterfly, with the engine's revs as its one control.** Lorenz's three equations of 1963 have a parameter ρ. Below 1 everything falls to a single point. Above 1 two resting points appear and move apart as ρ rises, and points spiral into them from ρ = 1.35. From ρ ≈ 13.93 a point that starts far away can loop chaotically for a long time before it settles; at ρ ≈ 24.06 the butterfly, the best known picture of chaos, is born beside the two resting points, and which one a point ends on depends on where it is; at ρ_H = 470 ÷ 19 = 24.74 the resting points lose their hold in a subcritical Hopf bifurcation and only the butterfly is left. (Checked against MathWorld, a Harvard course handout and a paper on the Lorenz system's transient chaos on 2026-11-28, and by integration.) Here ρ is 28 × rpm ÷ shift rpm, clamped to [0, 28], so the engine takes the figure through all of it: a still point with the engine off, two calm spirals in the middle of the range, and the butterfly over the last 11.7% of the revs before the shift point, or from 86% if it is already flying.

Starting layout, drawn in the x–z plane of the equations, seen with depth, 30 px for each unit across and 8.9 px for each unit up, with the origin at (0, +215). Rank 3: the two resting points are the tach. They sit at x = ±√(β(ρ − 1)), z = ρ − 1, so both ride the parabola z = x² ÷ β, and their height is linear in the revs: each is a bright eye on a ticked parabola that runs from the origin up to (±255, −25) at the shift point, with Barlow Semi Condensed numerals in a fixed column left of the left limb, each at the height of its rpm (a turn about the vertical axis never changes a height, so they never move). Rank 4: the figure, two glowing trails, one from each side. Rank 1: the gear (Exo 2 800, cap 150) on a true-black pad, Ø200 at (0, −40), the butterfly's body, and ten lamps on a straight line at y −296 (760 × 28). Rank 2: tabular speed (Exo 2 300, cap 84) on a pad under the gear at (0, +130). The hood view uses 40 px across and 7 px up.

- **Signature moment:** Lift-off. Two points, exact mirror images of each other, move by Lorenz's equations, and each leaves a glowing trail. Their clock is the distance driven, one unit of the model's time for every 50 m, with a floor of half a unit per second so that they settle when the car stands. Below the shift window a point near its eye always ends in it: when the revs change the eyes move, the points chase them round in spirals, and at steady revs each point comes to rest in its eye while its trail shrinks into it, so a steady cruise is two still lights. As the revs near the shift window the spirals take more and more turns to settle (about 9 turns for each e-fold at ρ = 20, 67 at ρ = 24), and past ρ = 24.74 they never do: the points loop round both eyes without repeating, and the trails draw the butterfly for as long as the revs stay there. Left alone, a point thrown off the butterfly by an upshift would keep looping for kilometres of driving before an eye caught it (integrated: a median of 1 km at ρ = 20, and most never settle at ρ = 24). So the upshift is the catch: on a gear change, and whenever ρ falls below 24 while a point is more than 3 units from its eye (a lift in the same gear), each point is set back by its eye, 0.5 units off it along the line to where it was, and its trail fades out over 300 ms; from there it spirals in by the equations alone, and the wings fold away. Each point is kept at least 1e−6 off the z axis, which the equations never leave, so that it lifts off when the revs rise. On the limiter only the lamps strobe, and both eyes hold a steady `shift` colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm and the car's shift rpm for the gear → ρ, and with it the eyes, the figure, the lamps and the limiter; speed and the timestamp → distance, the model's clock; gear → the numeral; speed → the tabular speed; lateral acceleration → the view turns about the vertical axis by 12° for each g, up to 1 g, which shows the figure's depth (the butterfly spans ±565 px at rest and at most ±715 px at 12°, ±683 px as integrated, inside the 1440 px envelope); the turn moves the trails, the eyes and the parabola together, and the numerals stay in their column; throttle → the brightness of the curves. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** E. N. Lorenz, "Deterministic Nonperiodic Flow", Journal of the Atmospheric Sciences 20 (1963) 130–141; E. Weisstein, "Lorenz Attractor", MathWorld (the equations, σ = 10, β = 8 ÷ 3, the resting points and their stability bound, https://mathworld.wolfram.com/LorenzAttractor.html); a Harvard Math 118r handout on the Lorenz bifurcations (https://abel.math.harvard.edu/archive/118r_spring_05/handouts/lorentz2.pdf); a study of its transient chaos and bistability (13.926, 24.06 and 24.74, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.11017); S. Strogatz, *Nonlinear Dynamics and Chaos*, chapter 9; `HarmonographDrive` in this catalog, the other curve-drawing dash, which this one must not be mistaken for
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural: both points are integrated on the CPU (fourth-order Runge–Kutta, steps of 0.005 units, a few steps a frame), and each trail is a fixed ring of 1,000 samples, the last 20 units of the model's time, drawn as line chunks sorted far to near. The eyes and the parabola come from the closed form, never from the integration. ComfyUI: a soft glow sprite for the eyes and a fine grain for the black pads' rims. Blender: none. Penpot: the board at ρ = 0.5, 10, 20 and 28. MSDF atlases for Exo 2 300 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** The figure is busiest exactly when the driver must shift: the lamps and the gear carry that moment, no part of the figure is brighter than 30% of the gear, and its change is the message, not something to be read. Two dots on a parabola can look empty in the middle of the range: the spirals round the eyes are the figure there, and the direction check looks at ρ = 10 and ρ = 20 first. The trail crosses behind the gear pad (about 14% of it at ρ = 28) and its lowest crossings come near the speed pad: both pads are true black and drawn over the trail. The mirrored points stay mirror images bit for bit only if the arithmetic is sign-symmetric, so the integrator does not use fused multiply-adds. A trail redrawn from scratch would shimmer in chaos, which is why the figure is a trail with a clock and never a fresh curve each frame; the trails are soft lines of at least 2 px. Perf: a few integration steps a frame and 250 line chunks, est. 0.2 ms.

#### SuminagashiMarble — build #42 · cockpit
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 62 · new.

**Japanese floating ink, for a game set in Japan.** Suminagashi is the earliest marbling that survives: the oldest known sample is in the early-12th-century *Sanjūrokuninshū* at Nishi Honganji in Kyoto, and the Hiroba family of Echizen say they have made it since 1151. Two brushes touch ink and a clear surfactant to still water in turn, so dark rings alternate with clear ones and each drop pushes the earlier ones outward; a breath or a fan bends the rings, and a sheet of washi laid on the water lifts the print. Besides black sumi the old colours are indigo and safflower red. (Checked against the Met, JAANUS and the published papers on 2026-11-28; sources under References.) Its motion has a closed-form description, mathematical marbling: every drop and every stroke is a map that keeps areas, as incompressible water does, and the papers present the maps as approximations of the real flow, not exact solutions. Here the ink is light on black water: each gear's ring is a soft light band, and the black between the bands takes the place of the clear rings.

Starting layout: a wide tray of black water, 1240 × 330 at (0, +20), in a lacquered rim. Rank 1: the gear (Exo 2 800, cap 160) on a black stone, Ø200, in the middle of the tray, and ten lamps on a straight line above the tray at y −185 (760 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 4: the ink rings round the stone. Their edges lie at √(100² + k × 90²) px from the middle, so the first is at 135 px and the tenth at 302 px, and every ring holds the same area of ink. The tray's edge cuts the widest rings, as a real tray does, and the tenth ring (302 px) passes under the side stones, which are drawn over it. Rank 2: tabular speed (Exo 2 300, cap 90) on a second stone, 260 × 130 at (+420, +20). A third stone at (−420, +20) shows the felt force as a number. Rank 3: the tray's lower rim is a light bar, 1100 px long, lit from the left, with Barlow Semi Condensed numerals under it.

- **Signature moment:** One ring for each gear. A drop of ink grows at the stone as the revs climb, its area linear in the revs from the rpm at which the gear was entered to the shift point, so every ring already on the water is pushed outward, smoothly and exactly: a drop of radius r at the centre C sends a point P to C + (P − C)·√(1 + r² ÷ ‖P − C‖²), so a point at distance ρ moves to √(ρ² + r²) (Jaffer). Each ring keeps its area and grows thinner as it is pushed out, as Jaffer shows for an annulus. At the shift point the drop is a full ring, and the upshift keeps it (the ring areas follow their targets on the `snap` spring, so a downshift drains the newest ring back into the stone): in sixth gear five full rings and one growing ring lie on the water. The felt force combs them: two strokes, one along the tray's horizontal centre line for lateral force and one along its vertical centre line for longitudinal force, move every point along the force by z·u^s, Jaffer's tine line, where s is the point's distance from the stroke's line, u = 2^(−1 ÷ c) so the shift halves every c = 90 px, and z is 60 px for each g. (Lu and others, 2012, use the other tine form, αλ ÷ (s + λ); this dash uses Jaffer's.) A corner drags the rings into a feather that points the way the car is pushed, and braking drags it forward. With steady revs and no force, nothing moves. On the limiter only the lamps strobe, and the newest ring holds a steady `shift` colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the growing drop, the rim bar, the lamps and the limiter; gear → the numeral and the count of full rings; speed → the tabular speed; acceleration → the two combing strokes (lateral and longitudinal) and the number on the third stone; throttle → the brightness of the newest ring. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** The Met, "Playing with Paper" (how suminagashi is made, https://www.metmuseum.org/perspectives/playing-with-paper); JAANUS, suminagashi (https://projects.mcah.columbia.edu/jaanus/node/934); "Paper marbling" (history, the Sanjūrokuninshū and the Hiroba family, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marbled_paper); S. Lu, A. Jaffer, X. Jin, H. Zhao and X. Mao, "Mathematical Marbling", IEEE Computer Graphics and Applications, 2012 (volume and issue to confirm); A. Jaffer, "Dropping Paint" and "Mathematics" (the drop and tine-line maps and their inverses, https://people.csail.mit.edu/jaffer/Marbling/Dropping-Paint, https://people.csail.mit.edu/jaffer/Marbling/Mathematics); `CymaticResonanceField` in this catalog, which also draws rings and must not be mistaken for this dash; `KineticLiquidMetal` for a still liquid surface
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the tray's rim in black lacquer and the three stones as river pebbles, rendered at 4K with the light variants the G-swing needs. ComfyUI: the grain of the lacquer, the faint sheen of still water as a normal map, and a washi paper fibre texture for the labels. Penpot: the board with the rings at gears 1, 4 and 8, combed and uncombed. The ink is geometry, never a picture: each ring edge is a closed line of 192 points put through the maps on the CPU. Both maps have closed-form inverses (the drop's: C + (P′ − C)·√(1 − r² ÷ ‖P′ − C‖²) for ‖P′ − C‖ ≥ r; the stroke's: P′ − z·u^s along the stroke), so a per-pixel route could pull each pixel back instead. MSDF atlases for Exo 2 300 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** Fine rings can shimmer on the panel: no two edges closer than 12 px at rest, and edges are drawn as soft bands, not hairlines. Rings round a numeral can read as a target: the stones are black and still, and the gear must pass the blur gate with ten rings on the water. The figure must be a function of what the car is doing now: it is rebuilt every frame from the ring areas, the revs and the force, never from a stored picture, so it cannot smear or drift. Perf: at most ten ring edges of 192 points through three maps each, drawn as 240 line chunks, est. 0.15 ms.

#### KaleidoscopeMirror — build #51 · cockpit
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 66 · new.

**A real kaleidoscope, with the mirrors set by the gear.** Two mirrors that meet at an angle θ show 360° ÷ θ − 1 reflected images round the direct view (Brewster); for loose, irregular objects the copies close without a seam only when 360° ÷ θ is an even whole number, θ = 180° ÷ n, which gives 2n sectors. Here n is the gear, so the figure has twice as many sectors as the gear: a mirror image in first, a cross in second, twelve sectors in sixth. Brewster's 1817 patent names 18°, 20° and 22½° as the most pleasing angles, which are exactly tenth, ninth and eighth gear. He conceived the instrument in 1814 to 1816, patented it on 10 July 1817 and published his *Treatise on the Kaleidoscope* in 1819; he set the eye in the plane of both mirrors, as near their meeting edge as possible, which is where this dash's eyepiece looks from. (Checked against Brewster's treatise and other sources on 2026-11-28, listed under References.) The chips in the wedge hang and swing with the force the driver feels, and the mirrors turn that into a figure that breathes through every corner.

Starting layout, stacked on the centre line. Rank 1: ten lamps on a straight line at y −300 (760 × 28), filling outside-in, and the gear (Exo 2 800, cap 150) on a true-black core, Ø190, at the middle of the figure. Rank 4: the figure, Ø380 at (0, −30), seen as through the eyepiece. The wedge between the two mirrors holds eight chips of coloured glass, each on its own pivot. Rank 3: a thin arc round the eyepiece, radius 215, 240° over the top from 8 o'clock to 4 o'clock, lit from the left, with numerals outside it; it sits outside the mirrors and is never copied. Rank 2: tabular speed (Exo 2 300, cap 90) to the right of the figure at (+390, −30); to the left, at (−390, −30), the felt force as a number. Rank 5: a thin knurled eyepiece ring and Barlow Semi Condensed labels.

- **Signature moment:** The turn of the mirrors. On every gear change the mirror angle goes from 180° ÷ n to 180° ÷ (n ± 1): the old figure fades over 150 ms and the new one opens with two more sectors, or two fewer. Between changes the chips do the work. Each chip is a damped pendulum of its own length, from 0.36 m to 1 m, so it swings at its own pace: 0.83 Hz to 0.50 Hz at rest. In a corner the felt gravity is √(g² + a²), a being the whole horizontal force (lateral and longitudinal together), which speeds every chip up by (1 + (a ÷ g)²)^¼: at 1 g the shortest chip swings at 0.988 Hz (undamped; the damped swing is a little slower), and the horizontal force is limited to 1 g in this model so that no chip passes 1 Hz. In a corner every chip leans to the same angle, atan(lateral g), but the short ones get there first (all share one damping ratio), so the figure fans open and settles. Where two chips overlap, their colours multiply as stacked filters do. The mirrors are first-surface aluminium, R = 0.92 a bounce, so the copies dim toward the far side of the figure, as Brewster notes in a real instrument. At the shift point the arc reaches its shift mark and holds a steady `shift` colour. On the limiter only the lamps strobe.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the arc, the lamps and the limiter; gear → the numeral and the mirror angle (neutral and reverse use one flat mirror, n = 1, so they show first gear's figure, and only the numeral tells them apart); speed → the tabular speed; lateral acceleration → the angle the chips hang at; longitudinal acceleration → how far each chip slides along its arm; throttle → the brightness of the light behind the chips. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** D. Brewster, *The Kaleidoscope: Its History, Theory, and Construction* (3rd ed. 1870; first as *A Treatise on the Kaleidoscope*, 1819, https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/74417); the patent of 10 July 1817 and the instrument's history (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaleidoscope); early kaleidoscopes at Kenyon College's apparatus collection (https://physics.kenyon.edu/EarlyApparatus/Optics/Kaleidoscope/Kaleidoscope.html); the dihedral group D_n of order 2n (https://mathworld.wolfram.com/DihedralGroup.html); the three-mirror triangles 60-60-60, 90-45-45 and 90-60-30 (https://en.etudes.ru/etudes/kaleidoscope); first-surface aluminium reflectance (LAYERTEC, https://www.layertec.de/en/coatings/metallic-coatings/aluminum-coatings/); stacked colour filters (Beer–Lambert); `HarmonographDrive` in this catalog for pendulums driven by the car
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: the eight chips as cut glass with bevels, baked to normal and thickness maps at 4K, and the eyepiece ring in knurled black metal. ComfyUI: seeds, bubbles and striae inside the chips as a labelled grain map, and the dim studio the mirrors reflect. Penpot: the board with the figure at gears 1, 2, 3 and 6. The three chip colours are absorptions built from the palette's primary, secondary and accent, so the driver's palette recolours the figure. MSDF atlases for Exo 2 300 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** A bright, symmetric figure round the gear competes with it: no chip is brighter than 35% of the gear, the core is true black, and the gear and the lamps must pass the blur gate with the figure at full light. Nothing in the figure may flicker: the chips swing at 1 Hz or slower in every corner up to the 1 g limit, and the change of mirrors is a fade, never a cut. A figure of sectors is close to `VisionPetals`: this one is made of mirror copies of loose chips, and its sector count is twice the gear. Perf: at most 20 copies of eight chips and one arc, all instanced, est. 0.15 ms.

#### GoldenAngle — build #60 · cockpit+hood
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 69 · new.

**A field of jewelled seeds set out as a sunflower sets them.** Seed number n sits at the distance c√n from the middle and at the angle n × 137.508°, the golden angle. That one rule packs the seeds evenly and makes the spirals of a sunflower head appear: two crossing families whose counts are neighbouring Fibonacci numbers that step up outward, 13 and 21 near the core, 34 and 55 further out, 55 and 89 at the tips of the field. The packing is most even at the golden angle; The Algorithmic Beauty of Plants shows 137.3° and 137.6° already opening gaps and rays. Turn it a twentieth of a degree down and the rim combs into 55 spokes; turn it the same amount up and the field barely changes. Here the car turns the angle. Driving straight, the field is a calm sunflower; in a corner it gathers into arms. (Model and values checked against Vogel's paper, The Algorithmic Beauty of Plants and MathWorld on 2026-11-28, and by computation.)

Starting layout: a wide field of seeds over OLED black, cut to an ellipse 1100 × 400 centred at (0, −10), with c = 20 px: 275 seed centres fall inside it, of which about 200 show round the core and the two pads, and the outermost is seed 754 (843 with c 6% smaller under braking), so the field is built from 850 seeds and culled to the ellipse. Each seed is a domed bead, Ø26, lit from under. Rank 1: the gear (Exo 2 800, cap 160) on a true-black core, Ø200, in the middle of the field, and ten lamps on a straight line at y −296 (760 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Exo 2 300, cap 90) on a black pad, 250 × 130 at (+330, −10). The same pad mirrored at (−330, −10) shows power. Rank 3: a thin light bar under the field, 1100 px long at y +215, lit from the left, with ticks and Barlow Semi Condensed numerals. Rank 4: the field. Seeds light from the middle outward in the order of their numbers, as an echo of the bar and never the reading. The hood view uses an ellipse of 1500 × 300 (279 seeds inside, built from 1,550 to cover the braking case).

- **Signature moment:** The arms. The angle between one seed and the next is the golden angle plus 0.25° for each g of lateral force, positive when the car is pushed to the right. At rest the field is a sunflower head: two families of spirals cross evenly and neither leads. In a corner every seed slides round its own circle and the field gathers into spokes that curl into arms. The fractions that come closest to the golden angle fall on alternate sides of it (8/21 and 21/55 below, 13/34 and 34/89 above), so the two directions look different. Pushed right, the seeds stand in 34 straight spokes across most of the field at +0.56 g; at +0.08 g only the tips of the ellipse begin to comb toward 89, which no one would see. Pushed left, the outer sides comb into 55 spokes at −0.21 g (beyond 310 px from the middle, much of it under the pads) and the whole field stands in 21 spokes at −1.46 g, which a road car reaches only at its limit. Between those the spokes curl, and the other side at the same force shows the Lucas fractions instead (29/76 at −0.56 g, 47/123 at +0.21 g), which is why left and right never look alike. Spokes of q appear only past radius c·q ÷ (2√π), and the direction check is made on the states that read: 34 spokes, 21 spokes and the curled arms between. Back on the straight the arms dissolve into the sunflower. Braking and accelerating breathe the field: c grows by 6% for each g of acceleration and shrinks by 6% for each g of braking, held within ±6%. At steady speed on a straight road nothing moves. On the limiter only the lamps strobe, and the lit seeds hold a steady `shift` colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the light bar, the count of lit seeds, the lamps and the limiter; gear → the numeral; speed → the tabular speed; power → the left pad, with the peak of the current pull held; lateral acceleration → the turn of the angle; longitudinal acceleration → the spacing c; throttle → the brightness of the lit seeds. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** H. Vogel, "A better way to construct the sunflower head", Mathematical Biosciences 44 (1979) 179–189 (https://doi.org/10.1016/0025-5564(79)90080-4); P. Prusinkiewicz and A. Lindenmayer, *The Algorithmic Beauty of Plants*, chapter 4, "Phyllotaxis" (formula 4.1 and figure 4.2, https://algorithmicbotany.org/papers/abop/abop-ch4.pdf); E. Weisstein, "Vogel Spiral", MathWorld (https://mathworld.wolfram.com/VogelSpiral.html); jewelled and faceted dial textures in watchmaking; `CymaticResonanceField` in this catalog for a field of many small parts
- **Phase 6 assets:** Blender: one bead as a domed crystal with a faceted underside, rendered at 4K as a sprite with its normal map and the light variants the G-swing needs; the same sprite serves every seed. ComfyUI: the bead's inner sparkle as a labelled grain map, and the dim studio it reflects. Penpot: the board with the field at rest and at 0.5 g and 1 g. Under the colour law a seed's hue may follow its place in one spiral family (its number modulo 21), through the palette's ramp. MSDF atlases for Exo 2 300 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** Hundreds of bright beads would swamp the gear: unlit seeds sit at 6% and lit ones at no more than 35% of the gear, and the core and the pads are true black. A field that swirls in every corner can distract: the arms form over 300 ms on a spring and never spin on their own. Seed n turns n × 0.25° for each g, so seed 754 turns 188° per g, and noise of 0.02 g would shake the rim by about 36 px: the lateral force is low-passed before the spring, with a deadband of ±0.03 g round zero, and the turn rate of the outermost seed is limited so that a fast transient never reads as a spin. Seeds must not flicker as the lit edge passes them: each fades up over 80 ms. Perf: 850 seed positions a frame (1,550 in the hood view), culled to about 275 instanced sprites, one bar and ten lamps, est. 0.15 ms.

#### HopfFibration — build #67 · cockpit
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 71 · new.

**A torus woven from linked rings, each one an exact circle.** The Hopf fibration fills the three-sphere with circles, one for every point of an ordinary sphere. Projected into ordinary space from a point on the fibre over the far pole, every fibre but that one is an exact circle (that one becomes a straight line), any two of them are linked exactly once, and the circles over one latitude circle on the sphere weave a torus of revolution, each fibre lying on it as a Villarceau circle. Tilting that circle off the latitudes bends the torus into a Dupin cyclide, a ring that swells on one side. (Checked against the sources under References on 2026-11-28, and every number by computation.) The unit of length is the projected fibre over the near pole, the unit circle, which sits just inside the tube. The car drives the two things a driver can read from it: how fat the torus is, and which way it leans.

Starting layout. Rank 1: ten lamps on a straight line at y −300 (760 × 28), filling outside-in, and the gear (Exo 2 800, cap 150) at (0, −180), above the figure on true black. Rank 2: tabular speed (Exo 2 300, cap 84) to the right of the gear at (+260, −180); to the left, at (−260, −180), the felt force as a number. Rank 4: the figure, seen from 25° above its plane, centred at (0, +70) and zoomed so that it always fits a box of 720 × 330: sixteen rings, the fibres over sixteen evenly spaced points of one circle on the sphere. Near parts of a ring are brighter and wider than far parts, and each ring is drawn over a black edge, so the eye sees which ring passes in front at every crossing. Rank 3: a lit arc with ticks on the lower half of a fixed ellipse just outside the 720 × 330 fit box, from 9 o'clock down through 6 to 3 o'clock, with Barlow Semi Condensed numerals inside it; it never follows the figure, so the numerals stay still. Rank 5: micro-labels and the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The lean. The circle's angle from the pole follows the revs, from 40° at idle to 110° at the shift point, so the torus grows from a thin band (tube 0.36, ring 1.06) to a fat one (tube 1.43, ring 1.74), its outer radius from 1.43 to 3.17, and the view zooms out so that it keeps its size; the zoom uses the figure's real extent, tilt included. The felt force tilts the circle on the sphere, 25° for each g: the torus swells on one side and thins on the other, and every ring is still an exact circle. The swollen side is where the circle comes nearest the far pole, and the Hopf twist turns it on screen, so the tilt's direction is computed so that the swell faces the side the car is pushed. With the circle at θ from the pole and tilted by τ, the tube runs from sin(θ ÷ 2) ÷ (cos(θ ÷ 2) + sin(τ ÷ 2)) to sin(θ ÷ 2) ÷ (cos(θ ÷ 2) − sin(τ ÷ 2)); the tilt is capped at 2 asin(cos(θ ÷ 2) ÷ 2), 56° at idle and 33° at the shift point, so the fat side is never more than three times the thin one. With steady revs and no force, nothing moves. On an upshift every ring slides one place round the torus, a turn of 22.5° of the sphere about the circle's own axis, over 300 ms on the `snap` spring: the weave visibly turns and the torus keeps its shape. Untilted, that turn is a rotation of space; tilted, it is a Möbius map, and the rings slide faster on the fat side. The last frame equals the first, so the change shows only while it moves. On the limiter only the lamps strobe, and the rim arc holds a steady `shift` colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the circle's angle from the pole, the rim arc, the lamps and the limiter; gear → the numeral, and the one-place slide on a change; speed → the tabular speed; acceleration → the tilt of the circle (lateral and longitudinal), the number beside the gear and a parallax of 4 px per g between the near and far halves; throttle → the brightness of the rings. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** "Hopf fibration" (fibres, the stereographic picture, nested tori and Villarceau fibres, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hopf_fibration); N. Johnson, "Hopf Fibration Video" (2011, every fibre linked with every other exactly once, https://nilesjohnson.net/hopf); D. W. Lyons, "An Elementary Introduction to the Hopf Fibration", Mathematics Magazine 76(2), 2003, 87–98 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.01642); "Dupin cyclide" (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dupin_cyclide) and "Villarceau circles" (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villarceau_circles); knot diagrams, for the gap that shows which strand passes over; `GeodesicWell` in this catalog for geometry placed in space
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural: every ring is 48 points of the closed form, put through the turn, the projection and the camera on the CPU, and drawn as line chunks sorted far to near. ComfyUI: a soft glow sprite for the near side of a ring and the dim studio it catches. Blender: none. Penpot: the board with the torus thin, fat and leaning. MSDF atlases for Exo 2 300 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** Sixteen thin rings can read as a wire drawing, below the bar of a production display: rings have width, depth shading and a soft glow, and the direction check asks whether it looks made, not plotted. A dense figure under the gear pulls the eye: no ring is brighter than 35% of the gear, and the gear and speed stand clear of it on black. The fibre over the far pole is a straight line that runs off the screen, and rings near it grow without bound: the angle stops at 110°, and the revs-dependent tilt cap keeps every ring at least 36° from that pole. Perf: 16 rings of 48 segments, each drawn twice (edge, then light), 192 line chunks, est. 0.2 ms.

#### HyperbolicTiles — build #72 · cockpit
Category **Abstract** · target `concept` · family Fluid / Generative · catalog row 72 · new.

**An endless tiling inside a circle, shown twice.** In the hyperbolic plane a regular tiling goes on for ever, and the whole of it fits inside a disc: the tiles shrink toward the rim and never reach it, as in Escher's Circle Limit prints. Escher began the series after a figure in Coxeter's "Crystal Symmetry and its Generalizations" (1957), as he wrote to Coxeter in 1958: Circle Limit I (1958) and IV (1960) are built on {6,4}, II and III (1959) on {8,3}, and the white arcs of III are equidistant curves, not edges of a regular tiling, as Coxeter showed in 1979. The same tiling can be drawn in two ways. In Poincaré's disc every edge lies on a circle that meets the rim at a right angle, or on a diameter, and every angle is true. In Klein's disc every edge is a straight chord and angles are not true. The dash shows both, side by side, and the car moves through the tiling: the felt force slides the plane under the disc, by an exact motion that keeps every tile the same size in its own geometry, the Möbius isometry z → (z + a) ÷ (1 + āz) with |a| = tanh(t ÷ 2) along the force, and k = 2z ÷ (1 + |z|²) carries each point to Klein's disc. (Checked against the sources under References on 2026-11-28, and every number by computation.)

Starting layout, mirrored about the centre line. Rank 4: two discs, Ø400 at (±430, −20). The left is Poincaré's and the right is Klein's, and both show the same tiling at the same moment. Rank 1: the gear (Exo 2 800, cap 170) at (0, −70) on true black between the discs, and ten lamps on a straight line at y −296 (760 × 28), filling outside-in. Rank 2: tabular speed (Exo 2 300, cap 96) under the gear at (0, +110). Rank 3: the rim of each disc is a lit arc of 270°, the two mirrored, filling from the bottom up the outer side; Barlow Semi Condensed numerals stand on the left disc only. Rank 5: one small label under each disc naming the model, and the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The slide. At rest the middle tile sits in the middle of each disc. The felt force translates the plane by 1.2 units of hyperbolic length for each g, eased into a limit of 1.8 units above 1.2 g, so at 1 g the tile that was in the middle sits 0.537 of the way to the rim in Poincaré's disc and 0.834 of the way in Klein's: the tiles slide toward the side the car is pushed, shrink as they near the rim, new ones grow out of the opposite rim, and the tiling holds there while the force holds. Nothing ever crosses the rim. The translation is built fresh each frame from the force now, never by stacking one frame's motion on the last, which would make the tiling slowly turn. The gear chooses the tiling: polygons of (gear + 2) sides, triangles in first and one more side for each gear after it, with the fewest meeting at a corner that this geometry allows (seven triangles, five squares, four pentagons, four hexagons, then three). Neutral and reverse show first gear's tiling. On a change the old tiling fades over 150 ms. With a steady force, nothing moves. On the limiter only the lamps strobe, and both rims hold a steady `shift` colour.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with the car's per-gear thresholds → the rim arcs, the lamps and the limiter; gear → the numeral and the tiling; speed → the tabular speed; acceleration → the translation of the plane (direction and length); throttle → the brightness of the tile edges. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** M. C. Escher, Circle Limit I and IV at the National Gallery of Art (https://www.nga.gov/artworks/54281-circle-limit-i, https://www.nga.gov/artworks/61282-circle-limit-iv) and "Circle Limit III" (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle_Limit_III); D. Dunham on the tilings under the four prints (https://www.d.umn.edu/~ddunham/md04paper.pdf); H. S. M. Coxeter, "Crystal Symmetry and its Generalizations" (1957) and "The non-Euclidean symmetry of Escher's picture Circle Limit III", Leonardo 12(1), 1979, 19–25; the Poincaré disc model, its geodesics and the Klein conversion (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poincar%C3%A9_disk_model); Möbius isometries, the {p, q} circumradius and d = 2 artanh|z| (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.10844, equations 13, 17, 18, 22 and 24); `GeodesicWell` in this catalog for geometry driven by the felt force
- **Phase 6 assets:** Procedural: each tiling's corners are computed once at load, out to hyperbolic distance 3.2 plus the tile's circumradius R_h (3.8 to 4.6, by gear) from the middle. A patch cut at 3.2 could not simply be translated: at 1 g its trailing side would end at 0.76 of the radius and leave a blank crescent. So every frame the tiling's own symmetry that brings the tile nearest the moved middle back to the middle is applied before the translation; the picture is the same, the leftover offset is never more than R_h, and the fixed patch always covers the disc out to 3.2. Only edges within 3.2 are drawn: 48 to 182 by gear ({7,3} has the most). The result is drawn as arcs on the left and as chords on the right. Blender: the two rims as thin machined rings, rendered at 4K. ComfyUI: a fine etched-glass grain for the disc faces, used as a labelled sparkle map. Penpot: the board with both discs at rest and at 1 g. MSDF atlases for Exo 2 300 and 800 and Barlow Semi Condensed 500.
- **Risk:** Tiles shrink without end at the rim and would shimmer: no tile narrower than 6 px is drawn, and both discs fade to black over the same band of hyperbolic distance, 2.8 to 3.2 from the middle (0.89 to 0.92 of Poincaré's radius, 0.993 to 0.997 of Klein's), so the two discs show the same tiles. Klein's disc squeezes its outer tiles into thin slivers, so the 6 px rule removes more there. In tenth gear, {12,3}, the middle tile's corners sit at 0.605 of the radius and only that tile is whole. Two discs can read as two dials: neither has a needle or a scale across its face, and the rev arc is on the rims only. A pattern that streams sideways can pull the eye in a corner: the edges are no brighter than 30% of the gear. Perf: at most 182 edges drawn in each disc ({7,3}), drawn as arcs on the left and lines on the right, est. 0.25 ms.

### Spare

A spare brief has no build number, no catalog row and no module. It joins the line-up only by TJX's word, in place of a dash or as a new one.

#### EdoKiriko — spare · cockpit
Written for the Liquid Glass category on 2026-11-28; its seat went to `SapphireMystery` when `CarplayIsland` joined Apple Vision. Family Liquid Glass. Its maths: absorption T = exp(−σ_a · l) in the cased layer and Snell per channel at each cut facet; a turn of 22.5° leaves the 16-ray chrysanthemum unchanged.

**Japanese cut glass, for a game set in Japan.** Edo kiriko is the cut crystal of Tokyo: a thin layer of coloured glass is cased over clear crystal, and the cutter's wheel opens V-shaped grooves through the colour, so the patterns show as bright clear lines in a deep blue or red skin. Its patterns are geometry with names: asanoha (the hemp leaf, a lattice of six-pointed stars), nanako (fish roe, a field of fine crossed cuts), kagome (the woven basket) and the chrysanthemum that is cut into the base of a glass. Step 1 researches the craft from makers' own pages and museum pieces and writes the pattern geometry down as equations.

Starting layout: separate pieces of cut crystal over OLED black, each a real optical object (`fh6_kit::OpticalGlass`), with every light drawn under the glass, as the Liquid Glass family requires. Rank 1: the gear (Saira Condensed 700, cap 190) under a Ø300 tumbler base seen from above at (0, −40), whose polished centre window magnifies the numeral about 1.1 times and whose rim carries a 16-ray chrysanthemum cut; above it seven round cabochons on a straight line (760 px span, 34 px tall) are the shift lights and fill outside-in. Rank 2: the speed (Saira 300, cap 96) under a polished oval drop on the left, 380 px from the gear. Rank 3: a long cut-crystal rod across the lower crown, 1100 × 56, cut in asanoha: the rev light runs under it and each groove catches it in turn. Rank 4: a small dish on the right cut in nanako, where boost and G show as light under the roe. Rank 5: the engraved maker's mark as the logo zone.

- **Signature moment:** The grooves fire. Each V-groove is two facets at 45°, so light under the rod is bent sideways and leaves the groove as a bright line with a thin colour fringe; as the revs climb the lit length grows and the lattice ignites star by star. At the shift rpm the whole lattice is lit and the cased colour deepens at the rim. On the limiter only the cabochons strobe. On an upshift the chrysanthemum under the gear turns by one ray, 22.5°, on the `snap` spring: the refracted light shifts once around the numeral while the numeral itself stays still.
- **Telemetry:** rpm, idle and max with per-gear thresholds → the lit length of the rod, the cabochons and the limiter; gear → numeral and the one-ray turn; speed → the oval drop's numerals; boost and acceleration → the light under the nanako dish; lateral G and steer → the studio light that swings across every facet. Every one of these is a verified field.
- **References:** Edo kiriko tumblers and cased-glass pieces from Tokyo makers and museum collections; the pattern names asanoha, nanako, kagome and kiku; `LiquidGlass` in this catalog for the optics and the handover format; `docs/specs/optics.md` for Fresnel, Snell and Beer-Lambert
- **Phase 6 assets:** The cuts are geometry, not pictures: Blender bakes each piece's normal, thickness and caustic maps at 4K from a modelled blank with real V-grooves, and the case colour is Beer-Lambert absorption through the thickness map, tinted by the palette's primary. ComfyUI: the studio panorama the facets reflect. No texture is painted inside the glass. MSDF atlases for Saira Condensed 700, Saira 300 and Titillium Web 600. The kit's analytic studio is the fallback.
- **Risk:** Cut patterns over a numeral destroy legibility: the gear and the speed sit under polished, uncut windows, and both must pass the blur gate through the glass. Fine nanako cuts can moiré on the panel: no groove pitch under 24 px. A coloured skin can turn murky: the colour comes only from absorption in the thin case layer, and the clear crystal under it stays clear. If the kit's glass cannot take a baked groove normal map, stop and report it. Perf: four glass pieces in one glass layer, est. 0.3 ms.

## Real geometry and maths
TJX, 2026-11-28: "the fluid ones i want real geomatry and maths going on". This table has one row for every dash, in any category, that draws a fluid, field, wave, lens, orbit, fold or generative figure. A dash with no row draws no such figure.

- **What a row binds.** The figure is the named model, driven by telemetry as the brief's Telemetry line says. The dash's unit tests assert the values in the last column, without a GPU. Where a brief and this table differ on the physics, the table wins, and the thread lists the difference in its handover.
- **Where the numbers come from.** The research note of 2026-11-28 computed each value with numpy. The rows for the eight Abstract dashes and `CarplayIsland` were then checked against published sources and re-computed the same day (the sources are in each brief's References line). The other rows' physical constants and citations are from memory, because the web budget had run out: step 1 of each of those dashes finds a source for its row and re-derives its values. An effect that `docs/specs/optics.md` lacks is added there first, with its source, as the cluster prompt already asks.
- **What the kit can draw today.** A cluster cannot supply its own fragment shader: `fh6_kit::Paint` is `Solid`, `Gradient`, `Image`, `Glass` or `OpticalGlass`. A per-pixel field therefore takes one of four routes: (a) closed-form evaluation on the CPU into existing shapes, with a `Shape::Polyline` drawn in chunks of at most 8 segments; (b) a texture built once at load; (c) the dash's own small compute pass that writes a texture registered with `Images::register_view`, as `fh6_kit::Fluid` does; (d) a new kit paint with a CPU reference and a test. Routes (a) to (c) stay inside the dash. Route (d) is shared code: it goes in the handover and the coordinator schedules it.
- **Noise.** Seeded noise is allowed as a labelled input (grain seeding, dither, a sparkle map). It never draws the figure.

### Psychedelic
| Dash | Model | The figure obeys | Its tests assert |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KineticLiquidMetal` | Hydrostatics of a free surface, a U-tube column, Rayleigh–Plateau dripping | Surface tilt tan θ = a_lat ÷ (g + a_vert). Column: x″ + 2ζω₀x′ + ω₀²x = −a_t, ω₀ = √(2g ÷ l). On a thread of radius R the fastest-growing disturbance has kR = 0.697. | 1.0 g lateral tilts the surface 45.00°, 0.5 g tilts it 26.57°. The `fluidSlosh` period of 0.62 s is a column of l = 0.191 m. Drop radius 1.89 R. Volume is conserved. |
| `CymaticResonanceField` | Modes of a thin free plate (Kirchhoff–Love), Chladni figures | W(r, θ) = [J_m(λr ÷ a) + C·I_m(λr ÷ a)] · cos m(θ − θ₀), with λ and C from the free-edge conditions, solved at load. J_m alone, as the older brief has it, is a drumhead and not a plate. | For ν = 0.30: λ² = 5.3583 for mode (2,0), 9.0031 for (0,1), 12.4390 for (3,0). Mode (m, n) has m nodal diameters and n nodal circles. |
| `ChronoMultiverse` | The Rosensweig instability of a ferrofluid | Spikes appear above a critical field, as a jump with hysteresis; below it the surface is exactly flat. Spacing from k_c = √(ρg ÷ σ). Surface ζ = A · Σ cos(k_i · x) over three wave vectors 120° apart. | k₁ + k₂ + k₃ = 0. ζ is unchanged by a 60° turn about a peak. Peak spacing is 2λ_c ÷ √3. λ_c does not depend on the field. |
| `IsochromaticLoadPath` | Photoelasticity: the stress-optic law over Flamant's line load | Fringe order N = t(σ₁ − σ₂) ÷ f_σ. Dark field I = I₀ sin²(πN). Round one load the fringes are circles tangent at the load, with σ₁ − σ₂ = 2P ÷ (πd). Circular polarisers show no isoclinics. | On circles of diameter 1, 1/2 and 1/3 the stress difference is 0.636620, 1.273240 and 1.909859 × P. N = 0.5 gives I = I₀. The four wheel loads sum to m·g for every G. |
| `AbyssalShearBloom` | Navier–Stokes by Stam's Stable Fluids; light where the shear passes a threshold | Shear rate γ̇ = √(4u_x² + (u_y + v_x)²). Emission: ∂e/∂t + u·∇e = k · max(γ̇ − γ̇₀, 0) − e ÷ τ. The kit's `Fluid` exposes dye only, so the shear-to-light step is one more dispatch (route c). | Rigid rotation gives γ̇ = 0, so an eddy's core is dark. Pure shear u = (s·y, 0) gives γ̇ = s. The projection never adds energy. With no forcing the lagoon goes black. |
| `HarmonographDrive` | The lateral harmonograph: two damped pendulums | x = A_x sin(ω_x t + p_x) e^(−d_x t), and y likewise. The figure closes only if ω_x ÷ ω_y = p ÷ q. Detuned by δ, it walks through its family at f_beat = q · f_x · \|δ\|, where δ comes from the gear ratio learned from speed ÷ rpm. | x = sin t, y = cos 2t lies on y = 1 − 2x². x = cos t, y = cos 3t lies on y = 4x³ − 3x. A 3:2 figure detuned by 1% repeats every 50 x-periods. |
| `AeroSmokeTunnel` | Potential flow round an ellipse (the Joukowski map) | ζ = z + c² ÷ z. F(z) = U(z e^(−iα) + a² e^(iα) ÷ z) + iΓ ÷ (2π) · ln(z ÷ a). The smoke lines are ψ = Im F = constant, with α the body slip angle. | For semi-axes 90 and 36: ψ is constant on the ellipse, the speed at the shoulder is 1.4 U, the far field is uniform. Line spacing goes as 1 ÷ U. |
| `AuroraVeil` | Auroral emission along magnetic field lines | Height profile of a ray: q(y) = q_max · exp(1 − y − e^(−y)). Rays lean with the dip angle, tan I = 2 tan(latitude). Colour from the emission lines (oxygen 557.7 and 630.0 nm, nitrogen 427.8 nm) through `spectrum_to_linear_srgb`, as the colour law allows. | The profile peaks at 1 for y = 0 and integrates to e. The dip is 76.88° at 65° magnetic latitude. |

| `LiquidGlass` | Everything in `docs/specs/optics.md` | Fresnel, Snell per colour channel, Beer–Lambert, GGX, the smooth-union outline. Built and tested. | The tests of optics.md section 13. |
| `SapphireMystery` | A domed contact lens (optics.md section 4); first-order lume decay | A hemisphere magnifies by n. Lume charge q ← q · e^(−dt ÷ τ). | Two half steps equal one whole step exactly. q is 1 ÷ e after τ. |
| `VernierLoupe` | A contact magnifier (truncated ball lens); the vernier | Magnification m = n ÷ (n − (n − 1) · t ÷ R). Least count = main division ÷ N. | For n = 1.7682: t ÷ R = 1 gives m = 1.7682, and the aplanatic point gives m = n² = 3.1265. Ten divisions over nine of 100 rpm read to 10 rpm. |
| `ShibuyaChromaticPrisma` | Prism dispersion (optics.md section 10) | δ = θ₁ + θ₂ − A with Snell at both faces and an index per wavelength. The split is the deviation at the three channel wavelengths, never a pixel offset. | A = 60°, n = 1.5 gives δ_min = 37.18°. N-BK7 gives 38.647° with an F to C spread of 0.708°. |
| `DichroicLouvre` | A quarter-wave interference stack | R = ((n₀ n_L^(2N) − n_s n_H^(2N)) ÷ (n₀ n_L^(2N) + n_s n_H^(2N)))². Tilting moves the band to shorter wavelengths: λ_c(θ) = λ_c(0) · √(1 − sin²θ ÷ n_eff²). No absorption: R + T = 1. | Eight pairs of 2.35 and 1.46 on 1.52 at 550 nm reflect 0.998704. R + T = 1 at every wavelength and angle. |
| `LumenEtch` | An edge-lit light guide | Critical angle θ_c = asin(1 ÷ n). Guided flux dΦ/dh = −κ(h)Φ, brightness B = κΦ. A uniform etch fades as e^(−h ÷ λ); an etch graded as κ = 1 ÷ (L − h) is evenly bright. | θ_c = 42.16° for n = 1.49. With L = 500 the graded etch gives B = 0.002 everywhere. |
| `ApertureNine` | An iris of nine arc-edged blades | The opening is the intersection of nine discs of radius R_b whose centres sit at distance e: inscribed radius R_b − e. Stops between two radii = 2 log₂(r₁ ÷ r₂). Such an iris is an exact circle only at e = 0, so R_b is the radius the brief wants round. | R_b = 200: e = 0 gives a circle of 200; e = 72 gives an inscribed radius of 128.00 and a corner radius of 130.82. From 200 to 128 is 1.288 stops. |
| `FibreOpticCorona` | Step-index fibres in a hexagonal faceplate | NA = √(n₁² − n₂²). Centred hexagonal numbers H(k) = 3k(k − 1) + 1. A pulse travels at s = v·t along true arc length of each Bézier fibre. | n₁ = 1.48 and n₂ = 1.46 give NA 0.2425 and a half-angle of 14.03°. H = 1, 7, 19, 37, 61. |

The span sets θ through the inverse, so the tach stays linear. Each facet is lit by E = E₀ · max(0, n·l). | a = b = 1, γ = 60°, θ = 30°: H = 0.4330, S = 0.8321, L = 0.9014. One sample per 0.5 m with a peak hold, so a kerb strike is never lost. The bounding radius is d + R. The rosette is unchanged by a turn of 360° ÷ N. For N = 6 at d = R neighbouring centres are R apart. Two petals pass the square of one.
